# WORKLOG — Receipt Analyser SaaS

## 2026-07-14 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-ac-manual-expense-entry — Upload page: "Enter manually" — type a brand-new expense with no photo/OCR (crossed into Codex's lane on explicit user authorization)

- **Files:** `routes/expenses.js` (new `POST /api/expenses` — the FIRST endpoint that can create an expense from scratch; `validateManualCreate` reuses `sanitizeExpensePatch` then layers a required-field floor). `public/js/components/upload.js` (new "Upload a file"/"Enter manually" toggle; a manual-entry form; 4 new pure helpers: `normaliseUploadMode`, `resolveManualCategories`, `validateManualEntry`, `manualErrorMessage`). `public/css/app.css` (toggle pill + form styling, matching existing `.act-view-toggle`/`.act-field` conventions). Tests: `tests/expenses.create.route.test.js` (**new**, 12), `tests/frontend/upload.test.js` (+13).
- **Context:** QA doc (`Untitled document (2).md`, 2 parts — part 1, date-filter presets, was already shipped in PR #244; this covers part 2 only): "put 2 options here one choose file for upload receipt another manually input the receipt details." Planned first (read-only) — found the real gap BEFORE writing any code: `routes/expenses.js` only ever had `PATCH` (edit an existing row), never a way to create one. Building the "Enter manually" form without that backend piece would have been building a form with nowhere to send it.
- **Required-field floor — Owner-confirmed via clarifying question before building:** Merchant + Date + Amount + **Category** are all mandatory (category is NOT defaulted to "Others" the way OCR receipts can be — there's no OCR here to guess one, so the person typing it commits to a real pick). Enforced on BOTH sides: the client form won't submit without all 4 (`validateManualEntry`, client-side UX only), and the server independently re-validates the exact same floor (`validateManualCreate`) — the client check is never trusted as the real boundary.
- **Reused, not rebuilt:** `sanitizeExpensePatch` (`lib/expenseValidation.js`) for the allow-list/type rules (unknown/spoofed fields like `account_id`, `id`, `voucher_no`, `branch_id` are dropped before the create-specific floor even runs); `sbInsertReturning` (`lib/supabase.js`) for the insert + scope-stamping (confirmed via a failing-then-fixed test: `account_id` is ALWAYS server-stamped to the real value, even in personal mode — the correct security property is that it can never be attacker-controlled, not that the key is merely absent); the branch/company attribution stamp mirrors `routes/upload.js`'s OCR-capture path line-for-line; the manual form's field styling mirrors `activity.js`'s edit-drawer (`.act-field`/`.act-field-input`) so typing an expense by hand looks consistent with editing one; the toggle mirrors the existing `.act-view-toggle` pill pattern.
- **DOM restructuring, checked for regressions:** the existing file-upload UI (drop zone, file list, actions, summary) is wrapped in a `.up-file-section` container so the new toggle can show/hide it as a whole — every line of the OCR upload flow itself (drag/drop, `doUpload`, `renderSummary`, staff-assign grid) is untouched, confirmed by every pre-existing `upload.test.js`/`upload.saveditems.test.js`/`upload.staffassign.test.js` test passing unmodified.
- **Explicitly NOT built** (flagged, not silently decided): no OCR/anomaly/duplicate-detection pass on a manual entry (saves straight to `status:'saved'` — the user typed it deliberately); no new `source: manual` column distinguishing manual from OCR-captured rows (would be a schema change beyond what was asked).
- **Tests: full suite 2064/2064** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +12 new backend route tests — auth/rate-limit gate, branch/company stamping in business vs personal mode, every required-field rejection case, spoofed-field stripping, DB-failure 502 with no leaked internals — and +13 frontend pure-logic tests mirroring the same floor client-side; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** PR opened, then merged on the user's explicit instruction — not the standing default (Jeevan/Owner normally names every merge); flagging the deviation for visibility, same as the prior date-filter PR. Live browser confirmation still owed (no credentials for a real authenticated session here, same caveat as every prior PR this session).
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the Owner-confirmed required-field floor; currency defaults to `opts.config.default_currency` (already exposed by `GET /api/config`) rather than the schema's hardcoded `USD` fallback.

## 2026-07-14 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-ab-activity-date-presets — Activity date filter: This Month / Month Selection / Custom Period + YYYY/MM/DD display (crossed into Codex's lane on explicit user authorization; fresh rebuild of the abandoned PR #214)

- **Files:** `public/js/components/activity.js` (new pure `normaliseDatePreset`/`thisMonthRange`/`monthRange`/`displayDate`; `buildToolbar`'s date controls rebuilt around a preset `<select>`; state defaults seed `datePreset:'month'` + the current month's range; `displayDate` applied at the 3 READ-ONLY render sites only). Tests: `tests/frontend/activity.test.js` (+16).
- **Context:** QA doc (`Untitled document (1).md`): the date filter was a bare From/To pair with no quick presets, and displayed dates didn't match the region's format. This is the SAME feature Codex attempted in PR #214 (`feature/activity-date-presets`), which went stale and hit a real conflict with C6's later `activity.js` changes and was closed/abandoned — so this is a fresh rebuild off latest `main`, not a revival of that branch. Date format resolved via AskUserQuestion before building: **YYYY/MM/DD** (e.g. `2026/07/14`), not ISO or DD/MM/YYYY.
- **Design — filter engine untouched:** `filterSortPage`'s existing `dateFrom`/`dateTo` inclusive range filter (string-compares `isoDatePrefix` output) is reused exactly as-is. The 3 presets just compute an ISO `{from,to}` and feed it into that same filter — This Month and Month Selection compute silently; Custom Period shows the original free From/To pair, unchanged behaviour, no output-format change to the filter itself.
- **Correctness detail caught during planning (read-only trace before coding):** `expense_date` is rendered at 5 places, not 3 — the table cell, card, and recently-deleted list are pure DISPLAY (reformatted to `YYYY/MM/DD`), but the edit-draft value and the edit-form `<input>` field feed back into a live PATCH. Reformatting those would have corrupted the round-trip (sending `2026/07/14` back to a server expecting `YYYY-MM-DD`). Deliberately left those 2 sites untouched, raw ISO.
- **`monthRange('YYYY-MM')`:** correct 28/29/30/31 last day via `new Date(y, m, 0).getDate()` (day 0 of the next month = the last day of this one) — leap-aware without a lookup table. Malformed input (`'2026-13'`, free text, empty) → `null`, never throws; the toolbar falls back to `thisMonthRange` if a picked month somehow fails to resolve.
- **Tests: full suite 2015/2015** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +16 net new — preset normalisation, `thisMonthRange`/`monthRange` date math incl. Feb leap/non-leap and month-end boundaries, `displayDate`'s ISO/timestamp/non-ISO/null cases, and a `monthRange` → `filterSortPage` integration test proving exactly one calendar month is selected; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** PR opened, then merged on the user's explicit instruction ("build it and merge it") — not the standing default (Jeevan/Owner normally names every merge); flagging the deviation here for visibility.
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the confirmed date-format decision — `displayDate`'s Asian-format target is now settled (`YYYY/MM/DD`), trivial to change later if reconsidered (one function).

## 2026-07-14 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · feature/barat-c5-submission-ping — C5 follow-up: Telegram "month ready" ping to bound admins (Jeevan-assigned, sequenced into my lane)

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (new `notifySubmissionReady(branchId, period, deps)`, exported), `routes/submissions.js` (accepts an optional `deps` param mirroring `routes/receipt.js`'s convention; POST handler fires the ping, fire-and-forget, after BOTH success branches — update-existing and new-insert; PATCH/withdraw untouched). Tests: `tests/bot.submissionping.test.js` (**new**, 11), `tests/submissions.route.test.js` (+5, plus the `app()` test helper now accepts `deps` for injection).
- **Context:** finishes C5's deferred bot hook — the in-app "Ready to close" badge (C5, already live) is the primary signal; this is a bonus ping for admins who've bound the Telegram bot. Jeevan's spec, followed literally: ping text `📋 <Branch> submitted <YYYY-MM> — ready to close.`; submit only, never on withdraw; must never throw and never block the submission response.
- **`notifySubmissionReady`:** resolves the branch's `office` name (`branches` table) and its `company_id` if it has one; looks up bound admins (`bot_bindings`, `role:'admin'`, `active:true`, `chat_id is not null`) — **scoped to that `company_id` when present** (multi-company correctness: an admin in a different company must never get another company's submission ping), falling back to an account-wide admin lookup when the branch has none (single-company deployments). Sends to every bound admin found; a missing branch row degrades to a generic "A branch" label rather than skipping the ping entirely. Every layer (branch lookup, bindings lookup, each individual send) has its own try/catch so one admin's failed send never blocks another's, and the whole function can never throw — matches `tgSend`'s own never-throws contract, with defense-in-depth on top since this is a *notification*, not the primary write.
- **Route wiring:** `routes/submissions.js` didn't previously accept a `deps` override at all (unlike `routes/receipt.js`'s established pattern) — added `deps = {}` so `notifySubmissionReady` is injectable for hermetic tests, avoiding a real network call to Telegram during route tests (the existing `stubDb` harness only intercepts Supabase REST calls, not Telegram's API). Call sites: `notifySubmissionReady(s.branch_id, period).catch(() => {})` right after both the update-existing and new-insert success paths, never awaited — proven with a dedicated test that a ping promise which never resolves still lets the response return, and one that rejects still returns 201.
- **Tests: full suite 2017/2017** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +11 new `bot.submissionping.test.js` covering the resolve/scope/fallback/never-throws contract, +5 in `submissions.route.test.js` proving the route actually calls it from both success branches with the right args, never on PATCH/withdraw, and never lets a slow/rejecting ping affect the response; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** PR opened, not self-merged — pushing branch `feature/barat-c5-submission-ping`, reporting the SHA per the task's instruction (push regardless of whether `gh pr create` succeeds).
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the literal spec — ping text, submit-only scope, and fire-and-forget semantics all follow Jeevan's message verbatim; company-scoping the admin lookup is my own judgment call (the spec didn't say, but leaving it unscoped would leak a submission notification across company boundaries in a multi-company deployment) — flagged for review.

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-aa-deleted-purge-reminder — Recently-deleted bin: display-only "permanently removed on <date>" reminder (crossed into Codex's lane on explicit user authorization)

- **Files:** `public/js/components/activity.js` (`purgeReminderDate`/`DELETED_RETENTION_DAYS`; `buildDeletedItem` restructured to wrap the existing item + new reminder line), `public/css/app.css` (additive-only styling for the new wrapper). Tests: `tests/frontend/activity.test.js` (+6).
- **Context:** QA doc (`Untitled document (4).md`): "whenever user open recently deleted on the bill list show the remind date that shows when bill permanently delete from system." Read `routes/expenses.js` first — confirmed there is **no auto-purge job today**; `deleted_at` is a permanent soft-delete with no scheduled cleanup anywhere. So this is explicitly a **display-only reminder** of a stated retention policy (30 days, `DELETED_RETENTION_DAYS`), not a promise a job enforces yet — flagged clearly in code comments and here, not silently implied as automatic. A real auto-purge job would be a separate, infra-lane piece (a scheduled job on the VPS).
- **Layout care (no new bug):** `.act-deleted-item` is a fixed 4-column CSS grid (merchant/amount/date/recover, +message wrapping to row 2). Adding a 6th child inline would have shifted the recover button and status message onto unexpected rows via grid auto-placement — caught by reasoning through the grid's implicit-placement rules before writing any DOM code, not by trial and error. Fix: `buildDeletedItem` now returns a wrapper (`act-deleted-row`) containing the ORIGINAL, byte-for-byte-unchanged item grid, with the reminder line as a sibling below it. `.act-deleted-list`'s existing flex gap applies to any direct child, so spacing between rows is unaffected.
- **Lane note:** this is Codex's file per the round-2 split; built on the user's explicit authorization ("continue build this"). Deliberately did **not** touch the already-open, already-conflicting PR #214 (`feature/activity-date-presets`, also edits `activity.js`/`app.css`) — tested a merge of that branch against latest `main` locally (never pushed) and found a genuine content conflict from C6's later changes, not just the routine WORKLOG clash. Force-resolving and pushing to someone else's in-progress PR branch risks colliding with their own next commit, so that one is left for Codex/Jeevan to reconcile; this PR is a clean, independent branch off latest `main` instead.
- **Tests: full suite 2001/2001** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +6 net new — `purgeReminderDate`'s date math incl. a month/year boundary case, an explicit-retentionDays override, never-throws on missing/malformed `deleted_at`, and the documented default constant; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** PR opened, not self-merged. Live browser confirmation still owed (same caveat as the export-menu PR — no credentials for a real authenticated session here). Whoever resolves PR #214 next should expect a conflict against this file too (both touch `buildDeletedItem`'s general area) — flagging so it isn't a surprise.
- **Assumptions:** 30-day retention is my own default (the plan's first-listed option); not confirmed by the Owner. Easy to change (`DELETED_RETENTION_DAYS`, one constant) once a real number is decided — and should be, once/if an actual purge job is ever built, so the display and the enforcement never quietly drift apart.

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-z-export-menu-editmode-indicator — Data-editor: trim export menu to Excel+Statement PDF, green Edit Mode indicator (crossed into Codex's lane on explicit user authorization)

- **Files:** `public/js/components/grid.js` (toolbar rebuilt around two new pure descriptors; `setExportBusy` fixed to iterate a collected button array instead of naming each removed variable; `is-editing` hook class added to the table), `public/css/app.css` (additive-only green styling). Tests: `tests/frontend/grid.test.js` (+4).

This is normally Codex's lane (`public/js/components/*.js` + `public/css/*`) per the round-2 split — planned first (read-only), collision-checked against every open PR (grid.js was untouched by any open PR; app.css overlaps open PR #214, so the CSS addition is strictly additive-only to avoid a hard conflict), then built on the user's explicit authorization to cross the lane boundary for this task.
- **Fix 1 — export menu trimmed:** removed Export CSV, Export PDF (with receipts), Export template, and Statement Excel; kept ONLY Export Excel (`doManagedExport('xlsx')`) and Statement PDF (`doStatementExport('pdf')`) — the Owner's explicit constraint ("don't change any output setting") is met because the kept buttons' formats/handlers are byte-for-byte unchanged, only the surrounding button DOM for the removed four is gone.
- **Bug caught before it shipped:** `setExportBusy()` referenced all 5 button variables by name (`exportBtn`/`excelBtn`/`pdfBtn`/`statementExcelBtn`/`statementPdfBtn`) — naively deleting 4 of them would have left a ReferenceError crashing every export click, kept buttons included. Fixed by building the toolbar from two new pure descriptors (`managedExportButtons()`, `statementExportButtons()`) and collecting whichever buttons actually exist into one array that `setExportBusy` disables — structurally can't reference a button that isn't there.

Added a `ledgerTableClass(editMode)` hook (`ledger-table is-editing`) and additive-only CSS (new selectors, zero edits to existing rules) that greens the editable columns/cells using `--moss`, the same green already used for the "Editing enabled" badge — one consistent "edit is on" cue, no new color introduced.

- **Verification:** hermetic tests only — no live browser click-through, since this app requires a real authenticated session and I don't hold credentials for the live/production Supabase instance; manually traced the full toolbar-build + `setExportBusy` + `render()` code path end-to-end to confirm no stray reference to a removed variable remains (grepped for all 5 old button names — zero hits) and that `mount()`'s external call signature/return shape is unchanged (no caller in `manage.js`/`app.js`/`nav.js` affected).

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-y-stuck-batch-final-reply — Album batch "stuck" with no error: root-caused + safety-net fix (plan mode diagnosis, then user-directed implementation)

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (new `sendFinal()` helper + `[group flush:start]`/`[group flush:done]` structured logs in `flushGroup`). Tests: `tests/bot.flushgroup-guard.test.js` (+4), plus 5 pre-existing test files' `tgSend` fakes fixed to return a realistic success shape (`tests/bot.batch.test.js`, `tests/bot.batch.photostorage.test.js`, `tests/bot.business.staffscope.test.js`, `tests/bot.hardening.qa.test.js`, `tests/bot.flushgroup-guard.test.js` itself).
- **Symptom (live QA screenshot):** an album's "Got your receipts — reading them all now…" ack arrived, then silence — no summary, no error, ever.
- **Diagnosis (plan mode, read-only):** `flushGroup`'s try/catch only guards against a THROW, and every terminal reply already existed and looked complete — but `tgSend` (lib/telegram.js) **never throws**: it swallows a Telegram/network failure internally and just `console.error`s, returning `undefined`. So a lost final message was structurally invisible to flushGroup's own error handling — indistinguishable from "stuck forever" on the user's side. Separately: the whole album buffer (`groups`, an in-memory Map) has zero persistence or boot-recovery, so a `pm2 restart` mid-flush (this session restarted the bot twice, for PR #227/#229) silently strands an album with no trace at all — a second, structural cause that a code-only fix cannot close (would need a durable `pending_batches` record; scoped out, flagged to the user, explicitly not built here).
- **Fix — safety net (not the durable fix):** `sendFinal()` checks `tgSend`'s own return value for flushGroup's three TERMINAL replies (download-fail / the main summary / the catch-all error) and retries once after a short delay before giving up — bounded to exactly one retry, not a queue. Added `[group flush:start]`/`[group flush:done]` logs (groupId, chatId, image count, saved/skipped/failed counts, elapsed ms) so a future "bot got stuck" report can be checked against server logs — did flush:start even fire for that groupId? Did it reach flush:done?
- **Regression risk found + fixed while wiring this in:** the new retry schedules its delay via `deps.setTimeout` — several existing test files fake `setTimeout` (a non-draining single-pass queue, for the debounce timer) AND have their `tgSend` fakes implicitly return `undefined` (no explicit return statement). Both together deadlocked those tests: a retry timer gets queued from inside an already-awaited fake-timer callback that can't be re-entered to drain it. Root-caused via `git stash`-free direct testing, fixed by making each affected fake return `{ ok: true }` (its real intended semantics — these tests are checking WHAT was sent, not resilience/retry behavior). The new `tryFinal` tests use REAL timers with 1ms delays instead of the hand-rolled fake queue, sidestepping the same class of deadlock for good.
- **Tests: full suite 1980/1980** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +4 net new in bot.flushgroup-guard.test.js covering retry-once-on-failure, no-retry-on-success, and the start/done logs; zero regressions; the previously-hanging combination now runs the 93 affected tests in <1s).
- **What's next — explicitly NOT done, flagged to Owner:** the restart-loss cause (in-memory `groups` Map with no persistence) is not fixed by this PR. A durable fix (a `pending_batches` table + recover-on-next-update sweep) was scoped in the plan file but deliberately held back — this PR is the safety-net-only option, chosen by the user directly rather than the "durable" or "both, staged" alternatives offered.

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-x-menu-keyboard-web-nudge — Telegram Web still drops the persistent menu keyboard even with is_persistent:true; mitigate by re-sending it more often

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (`handleTodayExpenses`, `handleWeeklyRecord`, the `🧭 Bot Guide` reply, and the "didn't catch that" fallback in `handleUpdate` now pass `MAIN_MENU_KEYBOARD` as their reply markup — previously sent with none). Tests: `tests/bot.menu.test.js` (+4).
- **Context:** User reported (with a screenshot of Telegram Web) that the persistent menu bar had vanished again — same bug as issue #162 D12 (2026-07-09), which added `is_persistent:true` and was Owner-confirmed fixed at the time. Root cause of the *recurrence*: `is_persistent` is only a request to the client; it does not guarantee the keyboard stays visible, and Telegram Web is known to sometimes drop/collapse it anyway — this is a Telegram client-side bug, not something bot code can force. No further server-side lever exists to *guarantee* it stays up.
- **Mitigation (not a guaranteed fix):** the bot previously sent `MAIN_MENU_KEYBOARD` only once, on `/start` (or on binding for business chats). Every other reply went out with NO keyboard at all, so once Telegram Web dropped it, nothing nudged it back until the user typed `/start` again. Now, every reply that doesn't already carry its own keyboard (Today Expenses, Weekly Record, Bot Guide, and the generic "didn't catch that" fallback) re-attaches `MAIN_MENU_KEYBOARD`. Same buttons, no visible change to a client that's rendering it correctly — but gives a client that dropped it several more chances per session to redraw it, without the user needing to remember `/start`. `handleTodayBills` was left untouched — both its business and personal replies already carry a different (inline) keyboard for deleting bills, and Telegram only allows one `reply_markup` per message.
- **Tests: full suite 1958/1958** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +4 net new, zero regressions).
- **Caveat flagged to the user:** if Telegram Web still drops it after this, the manual workaround is tapping the small keyboard icon next to the message box — that's a Telegram Web UI quirk, outside what this bot can control server-side.

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-w-todaybills-delete-list-nextdone — QA doc: list-delete bills numbers + Next/Done workflow (2 of 4 reported issues; other 2 are Telegram-client behavior, not bot code)

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (`tryResolveBillDelete` rewritten to accept a list, `handleCallbackQuery` gets `delnum:next`/`delnum:done`). Tests: `tests/bot.business.todaybills.test.js` (+9).

Only 2 of the 4 are bot-code bugs; the other 2 (Telegram's menu button in the text-input bar intermittently disappearing; the device back button not closing Telegram's own menu) are Telegram client-app behavior — the bot server has no hook into either, so they're out of scope here and were NOT touched. Flagged back to the user rather than faking a fix.
- **Fix 1 — list input:** typing "1, 2, 4, 8" after tapping 🗑️ Delete a bill used to fail the old `/^\d+$/` single-number check and silently fall through to the main-menu keyword router. Now a comma/space-separated list is recognised (`/^\d+(?:[\s,]+\d+)*$/`), de-duplicated, and every number is resolved — each with its own outcome line (deleted / already gone / not the submitter / no such number) in one combined reply. Single-number replies keep byte-identical wording to the pre-existing flow (only gains a keyboard, see below) — zero behavior change for that path, verified by the pre-existing test suite passing unmodified.
- **Fix 2 — Next/Done loop:** any successful delete (single or as part of a list) now attaches a ➡️ Next / ✅ Done inline keyboard. **Next** re-arms the same chat (same `delnum:ask` code path) for another round of typing, WITHOUT resetting the running per-session `deleted` list. **Done** posts a numbered summary of everything deleted this session and clears the pending entry (same single-use safety property as before — a stray later number can't delete anything without a fresh 📋 Today Bills tap).
- **Tests: full suite 1954/1954** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +9 net new, zero regressions — every pre-existing delete-flow test, including the #139 single-use safety test, passes unmodified).
- **Scope:** business mode only (`pendingBillDelete`/`delnum:*` never existed in personal mode); personal mode's per-bill ❌ flow (`delask:`) is untouched.

## 2026-07-13 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-v-lineitem-edgecases — Harden line-item extraction against edge cases Codex's C2 will depend on (Jeevan-directed, de-risks the statement/reports category split)

- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (`assembleExpense`'s line-item block — reconciliation guard added). Tests: `tests/ocr.lineitems.test.js` (+9).
- **Context:** Jeevan confirmed Codex has taken C1–C7 (stood down from that territory entirely, don't touch `public/js/components/*.js`/`lib/statement*.js`/`lib/reports.js`/`lib/excel.js`/their routes) and asked me to harden 4 specific line-item edge cases in `lib/ocr.js`/`lib/categorize.js` only, ahead of Codex's C2 (statement/reports category-splitting) landing.
- **Checked all 4, 3 were already correct (no code change) — locked in with tests so they can't silently regress:**
  1. **Off-template category:** re-confirmed (via `ruleCategory`, `baseDecision`, `llmWatchdog` — all gate on `allow.has(cat)`) that the real categorizer structurally cannot emit an off-list category. Added the case that WAS a genuine untested path: the categorizer **throwing** (network/LLM failure) — items keep their description/amount but get no `category` key at all (never a bad value); a fresh test locks this in.
  2. **Missing/null category on one item:** already handled — an item without a `category` field simply has no `category` key on the stored line item; siblings with a category are unaffected. Tested.
  3. **Mixed currencies within one receipt's line items:** confirmed structurally impossible — `coerceLineItems` only ever keeps `{description, amount}`, so a hostile/hallucinated `currency` (or `category`) field on a raw OCR line item is stripped before it ever reaches the categorizer or `assembleExpense`. Tested with a hostile-input receipt proving the categorizer itself never even sees the injected fields.
- **Item 4 — genuine gap, fixed:** line items were fx-scaled but never reconciled against `expense.amount` (the final converted total) — OCR misreads or per-item `round2()` could leave the array summing to something slightly different from the stored total, which Codex's C2 would then have needed its own reconciliation logic for. Now: after building the item array, if it doesn't sum exactly to `expense.amount`, attribute the remainder to the **largest** item — but only within a **rounding-scale tolerance** (≤ RM1 or ≤2% of the total, whichever is larger). A bigger mismatch is a genuine OCR misread, not rounding — deliberately left AS-IS rather than fabricating a corrected line that never appeared on the real receipt; that case is for the existing anomaly/review path, not silent papering-over here. Runs AFTER fx-scaling, against the final converted total (tested with a receipt whose EUR items scale to MYR then still need a small post-conversion correction).
- **Result:** Codex's C2 can now sum `expense.line_items` directly and trust it matches `expense.amount` whenever the receipt was genuinely just rounding-off — zero extra reconciliation logic needed on the aggregation side for the common case.
- **Tests: full suite 1935/1935** (`.env` moved aside/restored; +9 net new vs. this branch's base on `main`, zero regressions — the pre-existing fx-scale test's fixture (items summing to 6 against a 500 total) deliberately falls OUTSIDE the tolerance, so it stayed untouched and still passes unmodified).
- **What's next:** hold — item 3 (bot Telegram-ping helper) still has nothing to build until Codex's C5 defines the interface it needs. PR opened, not self-merged.
- **Assumptions:** the RM1-or-2% tolerance is my own judgment call (the plan only said "attribute a rounding remainder to the largest item" without a numeric threshold) — flagged in the PR for Jeevan/Owner to adjust if a different bound is wanted.

## 2026-07-12/13 · Claude (Sonnet/Opus, for Jeevan) · receipt-photo ROOT CAUSE (nginx mis-route) FIXED live + UI polish + Premala customisation plan

- **⭐ THE receipt-photo bug — SOLVED at the infra layer (not the app).** After #198/#204/#206/#207/#208 all shipped and the Owner STILL saw "no photo available" on brand-new receipts, I stopped trusting the app and curled the LIVE HTTP paths directly. Finding: `https://31.97.70.50/receipt/by-id/:id` → **404**, but the same route on the app's own port `http://127.0.0.1:3005/receipt/by-id/:id` → **401** (route exists, just needs auth). The box is nginx-fronted + shared-tenant: `location /receipt/` in `/etc/nginx/sites-available/receipt-analyser` proxied to **:3001 = Barat's FrictionAI app** (a stale leftover from when receipt code lived in FrictionAI), while `location /` correctly went to **:3005 = the Premala app**. So every dashboard photo request hit the wrong backend → 404 → "no photo available", and (worse) Premala **session tokens in the Authorization header were forwarded to FrictionAI** (same-team, internal, but a real cross-tenant hygiene leak). **Fix (root, Owner-authorized):** backed up the config, repointed both `/receipt/` blocks (:80 + :443) `:3001`→`:3005`, `nginx -t`, `systemctl reload nginx`. **Verified live:** `/receipt/by-id/…` 404→**401**; FrictionAI `/app/` still 200 (untouched). This ALSO closes the token leak (photo requests no longer touch :3001). Read-only DB/storage checks confirmed the data was always fine (RC-2026-0074/75/76/77/78 all have valid stored JPEGs, 100–320KB). **Lesson logged in MEMORY_LEDGER GOTCHA:** the deploy only verifies `/health`+`/ready` (both apps 200), so a mis-routed path deploys "green" while broken — curl the actual `:443` path vs `:3005` direct when a route misbehaves.
- **#215 — review queue UI (`public/js/components/activity.js`):** (1) raw `needs_review` leaked into the UI → new `statusLabel()` renders "Need Review" / Saved / Approved / Rejected / Awaiting Category (unknown → Title Case), applied to the status filter, the table/card status cell, and the drawer picker; (2) Approve/Reject showed a bare "Action failed" that hid the API's real reason → new `decisionErrorText()` surfaces the server message (e.g. the staff-first valve 403 "the branch clerk decides first — the admin valve opens 7 days after flagging", which was working as designed, just invisible). +6 tests. Suite 1902. Merged + deployed.
- **#210 — Team page (`team.js`/`app.css`):** the view-login (👤) icon now opens a centered modal popup (same overlay convention as the receipt drawer) instead of an inline panel; branch cards now a responsive grid (3 desktop / 2 tablet / 1 mobile) instead of a tall vertical spine. Merged + deployed.
- **#212 — Payments (`payments.js`):** hide the branch filter/export selector from a clerk (they only ever have one branch; server already hard-scopes). Merged. *(NB: the Payments page itself is slated for removal per the plan below — this becomes moot but harmless.)*
- **#203 — session-expiry UX** (`api.js`/`app.js`/`login.js`): auto-detect a dead/expired session (401 WITH a bearer) → clean "Your session expired. Please log in again." re-login prompt instead of a wall of "could not load" panels. Merged + deployed.
- **#205/#209/#218 — docs:** logged #204 and the #206/#207/#208 arc; **#218 = the Premala customisation + line-item plan** (`docs/plans/2026-07-13-premala-customisation-and-lineitem-plan.md`), handed to Barat.
- **▶ Premala customisation plan (for Barat):** remove Payments page (manual PDF handoff, drop Send-to-Admin); move Petty Cash Statement export into Manage→Data editor; Manage Category tab = chart-of-accounts list only (remove column/template builder, keep code); remove Companies/Add-company UI (single company; `STATEMENT_COMPANY_NAME="BUMI ERA NEW CONSTRUCTION (M) SDN BHD"`); Overview+Reports admin filters + live-update polling; **⭐ line-item category splitting** (selling point) — mostly an aggregation-layer change since `expenses.line_items` already persists per-item category+amount. All removals = hide-behind-flag, code retained (ADR-020).
- **Assumptions/notes:** the nginx change touches a root-owned, shared-tenant config — done only after explicit Owner authorization naming the host; FrictionAI verified unaffected. Recommend rotating current dashboard sessions since old session tokens may sit in FrictionAI's logs from the pre-fix mis-route.

## 2026-07-12 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-s-statement-row-overflow — Petty Cash Statement PDF: fix stray "TOTAL"/"0.00" fragments on near-blank pages (row split across a page boundary)

- **Files:** `lib/statementPdf.js` (`tableRow` overflow guard). Tests: `tests/statementPdf.test.js` (+1).
- **Bug (Owner-reported, screenshots):** on a multi-page statement, near-blank pages appeared carrying a lone "TOTAL" (mid-page) or "0.00" (right-aligned) with a stray empty box — the summary table looked broken.
- **Root cause (found by reading + reproduced by rendering):** in `drawStatement`, only the Section-B **data** rows are wrapped in `pageBreak()` (which adds a page + repeats the column header). The category **TOTAL** rows, the **SUBTOTAL** row, and the **footer** (A. TOTAL CASH IN-HAND / B. TOTAL EXPENDITURE / BALANCE) are drawn by bare `tableRow()` calls with **no** overflow guard. When one of those landed within `ROW_H` of the page bottom, pdfkit drew the cell borders off the bottom of the current page but flung the cell **text** (`TOTAL`, `0.00`) onto a fresh page — the stray fragment. Data-dependent: only shows once the statement is long enough to overflow (the real 10-category chart of accounts + pad rows does; the 3-category test model didn't, which is why it slipped through).
- **Fix (one guard, design untouched):** `tableRow` now starts a fresh page BEFORE drawing if the row wouldn't fit (`ctx.y + ROW_H > PAGE_BOTTOM`). Every row — total, subtotal, footer — now renders intact on one page. The data rows are unaffected (their `pageBreak()` already ran, so `ctx.y` is at `MARGIN` and the new guard is a no-op there — no double page, header-repeat still works). Zero layout change when a row already fits (the common case).
- **Verified visually + tests:** rendered the previously-fragmenting case (10 categories, padRows sweep 0–8) to page images before/after — confirmed the stray "TOTAL"/"0.00" fragment pages are gone and every total/footer row is intact, sign-off still on its own clean page. Full suite **1906/1906** (hermetic; +1 structural regression test: a long multi-page statement renders one valid PDF with a bounded page count — catches any re-introduction of runaway fragment pages; pixel-level "no orphaned TOTAL" stays a visual check per this file's own convention).
- **What's next:** merge (Owner-directed this session) → auto-deploys. Receipt-card work (PR #216) already live; this stacks cleanly on top (touches only `tableRow`, disjoint from the evidence-page code).
- **Assumptions:** none — additive guard, no data/schema/route change.

## 2026-07-12 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-r-receipt-card-redesign — Petty Cash Statement PDF: receipt appendix redesigned as bordered ID-cards (Owner request), fixes small-photo upscale blur

- **Files:** `lib/statementPdf.js` (`drawEvidencePage` rewritten; new `fitScale`, `formatDisplayDate`, `evidenceDetailLines` — first two exported/node-tested). Tests: `tests/statementPdf.test.js` (+7).
- **Owner feedback on the LIVE PDF (not a fresh request — a revision of #200's "match client sample" redesign, `88542cc`):** the full-bleed "one big image + thin caption strip at the bottom" layout looked like "an image dump, not an accounting report," and pdfkit's `fit:[w,h]` option **upscales** a photo smaller than its box — a small receipt photo was being stretched blurry. Full Owner spec (verbatim, pasted as the build instruction): bordered card per receipt, title "Receipt #N", a details block (Date/Vendor/Amount/Branch/Staff) up top, the photo in its own bordered box **never stretched past its real size**, ref number underneath.
- **What changed (evidence mode only — `routes/statement.js`'s call site; `drawPlainReceiptPage`/manageExport.js's plain path is untouched, per the Owner's explicit "will not touch" list):**
  1. **The card:** `doc.roundedRect(...).stroke()` frames the whole page content area; title + details block + a separately-bordered photo box + centered "Ref: <id>" all drawn inside it.
  2. **The blur fix — `fitScale(imgW, imgH, boxW, boxH)`:** `pdfkit`'s built-in `fit` option scales an image to *fill* the box regardless of direction (upscaling a small image). This computes the scale itself via `doc.openImage()` (gets real pixel dimensions) and **caps at 1** — a big photo still shrinks to fit; a small photo is drawn at its real size instead of blown up. Pure, exported, unit-tested directly (the one piece of this that's meaningfully testable — page-arithmetic is exercised as before, but pixel-level layout still isn't, per this file's own existing test-file note that "the Owner does the final visual diff").
  3. **Higher-DPI for PDF-source receipts:** the evidence-mode photo box is smaller than a full page, so a PDF-sourced receipt (rasterised via `lib/pdf.js`) is now rendered at `EVIDENCE_PDF_DPI=220` instead of the default 150 when `receiptEvidence` is on — plain mode's DPI is unchanged.
  4. **Not done (flagged, not hidden):** no auto-rotation for sideways photos — a real, separate piece of work, out of scope for this pass.
- **Verified by actually looking at it, not just tests** (tests can't "see" a layout): generated a real sample PDF with a genuinely small photo (80×60) and a genuinely large one (1800×2400), rasterised both pages to PNG, and visually confirmed — bordered rounded card, correct title/details/ref, small photo at its real (unstretched) size, big photo shrunk to fit within its box. Screenshots reviewed inline before shipping.
- **Tests: full suite 1905/1905** (hermetic, no `.env`; +7 net new, zero regressions — all 14 pre-existing `statementPdf.test.js` tests needed no changes since page-count arithmetic is unchanged by the card redesign).
- **What's next:** PR to Jeevan for review — **not self-merged**, per the Owner's own explicit instruction in the build spec ("I won't merge it myself").
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the Owner's own written spec, followed literally (card style, field order, "no extra editing").

## 2026-07-11/12 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat/Jeevan) · diag/jeevan-ready-storage-probe + diag/jeevan-ready-object-probe + fix/jeevan-hreq-arraybuffer-status — root-caused the live "Could not load the receipt" reports down to zero remaining hypotheses; VPS proven able to download real receipt images end-to-end

- **Context — Barat found a real bug.** After #198 (album/PDF photo storage) and #204 (clearer error wording) shipped, the Owner still saw "Could not load the receipt" on a brand-new receipt. Barat traced it to `lib/http.js`'s `hreq()`: the `encoding:'arraybuffer'` path (used for every Supabase Storage / Telegram binary fetch) returned `r.arrayBuffer()` unconditionally — a 4xx/5xx error body (JSON) was silently handed back AS IF it were valid image bytes. Effect: a broken thumbnail with **no log line at all**, and the real Storage/Telegram error was invisible to every caller's own catch block.
- **Fix (`lib/http.js`, PR #206):** on the arraybuffer path only, throw (carrying `err.status` + a short body snippet) when `!r.ok`, instead of returning the error body as a Buffer. The text/json paths are deliberately untouched — Supabase REST/Telegram/Groq callers there legitimately inspect non-2xx bodies themselves; changing that would be a wide, unrelated behaviour change. `tests/http.test.js` +4 (2xx still returns raw bytes; 400/403 now throw with `.status` + message; text/json path unchanged).
- **Diagnostics added so the actual VPS state is visible without SSH (PRs #207, #208):** `/ready` (readiness probe, `routes/misc.js` + `lib/storage.js`) gained two new informational dep verdicts — neither gates the existing 200/503 (that stays tied to DB reachability only):
  - `deps.storage` (`probeStorage`) — can this server authenticate to the `receipts` bucket at all (bucket-metadata read).
  - `deps.object_read` (`probeStorageObject`, opt-in via `?deep=object`) — can this server actually **download** a real stored receipt object (the exact operation that serves a card's photo), reported as up/down/no-data with a byte count or status+reason.
  `tests/storage.test.js` +7, `tests/ready.route.test.js` +6.
- **Verified live against the real Premala VPS after each deploy:**
  - `curl /ready` → `{"ok":true,"deps":{"db":"up","storage":"up"}}` (storage bucket auth confirmed working on the live server).
  - `curl /ready?deep=object` → `{"ok":true,"deps":{"db":"up","storage":"up","object_read":"up"},"object_read_detail":"136998 bytes"}` (the VPS successfully downloaded a real, recently-stored receipt image end-to-end — the exact operation a dashboard card's photo needs).
  - Also verified read-only, off-VPS, against the same live Supabase project via a scratch script: a specific recent receipt (RC-2026-0073) has a valid stored JPEG (86934 bytes, correct magic bytes), `fetchReceiptObject()` returns it correctly, its DB row's branch/company scope resolves correctly for both admin and clerk lookups, and the `receipts` bucket config is sane (`public:false`, expected mime types).
- **Net result: every layer that can be tested without SSH now checks out** — DB reachable, bucket auth working, actual object download working, code paths (upload/serve/scope) all correctly wired, and the one real bug found (`hreq` swallowing error bodies as bytes) is fixed. The remaining "no photo" reports are narrowed to two non-mysterious cases: (a) receipts captured before #198 deployed, which never had an image saved and cannot be backfilled (re-send in Telegram — this is exactly what #204's message tells the user), or (b) a stale cached dashboard view before a hard refresh.
- **Tests: 1890/1890** (`.env` moved aside, restored; baseline before this arc was 1873 → +4 http + 7 storage + 6 ready = +17, matches).
- **What's next:** Owner to hard-refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R) and send one brand-new Telegram receipt, confirm the photo now shows. If a *freshly-sent* receipt still fails to show a photo after a hard refresh, that would be a genuinely new lead (not yet reproduced) — capture its voucher number + whether viewed as admin or clerk for the next diagnostic pass.
- **Assumptions:** none stated beyond what's verified above; the `?deep=object` probe intentionally tests "the newest receipt_url row at request time" (not a pinned specific receipt), so a repeat curl can legitimately report a different byte count as new receipts arrive — this is expected, not a regression.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Opus, for Jeevan) · fix/jeevan-receipt-image-error-message (PR #204, merged + deployed) — a permanently-missing receipt photo now reads as such, not a generic "could not load"

- **Files:** `public/js/components/activity.js` (new pure `receiptErrorMessage(err)`, wired into the single shared `loadReceiptImage` catch); `tests/frontend/activity.test.js` (+2). No backend/route change.
- **Why — Owner-reported (with screenshot):** dashboard cards whose receipt image won't load all showed the same vague *"Could not load the receipt."* — no way to tell a one-off network blip from a photo that is genuinely gone and needs re-sending.
- **Investigated before coding (did NOT re-fix a working pipeline):** traced all 4 capture paths + serve route (`routes/receipt.js`) + renderer — all correctly wired; #198 already closed the album/PDF `receipt_url=null` gap and is deployed. Compared against the reference the Owner pointed at (FrictionAI `_live/server.js` `uploadNotePhoto`, read-only): it fetches the Telegram file once and persists it durably. **Our ADR-018 already does the architecturally-equivalent thing** — fetch-once-then-store — just to a PRIVATE bucket + auth-gated serve route instead of a public URL (correct for financial documents). No architecture change warranted.
- **Actual root cause of the "won't load" cards:** `/receipt/by-id/:id` returns **404** only when NEITHER the durable Storage copy NOR the live Telegram `file_id` proxy can produce bytes — e.g. receipts captured before durable storage existed, whose only path is Telegram's `getFile`, which stops resolving an old `file_id` over time (a real Telegram-side limitation; `tgFileUrl` itself is a plain, correct `getFile` call). Those photos are unrecoverable — they must be re-sent. Every OTHER failure (401 session, 502 upstream) stays a genuine "try again."
- **Fix:** a 404 now shows *"This receipt has no photo available. If you have the original, re-send it in Telegram to attach one."*; all other errors keep the generic retry message. One choke point → card thumbnail, drawer image, and review-queue thumbnail all benefit.
- **Verification:** 2 new unit tests + **verified live in the browser** (loaded the real bundle, confirmed `Activity.receiptErrorMessage` returns the exact per-status strings). Full suite **1881/1881** (`.env` moved aside, restored). Merged (PR #204) + auto-deployed to the Premala VPS (`3b8cc06`, deploy success).
- **What's next / owed:** Barat to smoke-test the pipeline with **NEW** receipts (single, then album, then PDF) sent after today's #198 deploy — an old receipt now correctly reading "no photo available, re-send" is working-as-intended, NOT a bug. A brand-new receipt still showing no photo WOULD be a real bug (needs the path + the VPS `[storage]` log line).
- **Assumptions:** none — the change is confined to the error-message text shown on a failed receipt fetch; the load/serve/store paths are untouched.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Opus, for Barat) · feature/dupdel-one-message — dupedit menu: Delete now turns the SAME message into confirm, then into "Deleted"

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (dupedit menu's "🗑️ Delete" button now points at a new `dupdel:` callback instead of reusing `delask:`; new `dupdel:` handler mirrors `delask:`'s ownership/already-deleted checks but EDITS the tapped message in place — via `tgEditMessage` with a new Yes/Cancel keyboard — instead of sending a fresh message; the confirm buttons still point at the existing, unchanged `del:<id>`/`del:cancel` flow, which already edits in place, so the SAME message_id carries through menu → confirm → deleted). Tests: new `tests/bot.dupdel.test.js` (7 tests: edit-in-place with keyboard, full menu→confirm→deleted round trip stays on one message_id, not-yours/already-gone/unknown-id/lookup-failure all edited in place, no-message_id defensive fallback), `tests/bot.duplicate.test.js` (updated the dupedit menu's button assertion from `delask:` to `dupdel:`).

- **Why — Owner screenshot + explicit request.** The duplicate-receipt "Edit staff / Delete" menu was 3 separate chat bubbles: the menu, then a NEW "Delete this bill? Yes/Cancel" message, then that one turning into "Deleted". Owner wanted it collapsed to feel like ONE message walking through 3 states. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion before writing any code: `delask:` is ALSO used by the "📋 Today's Bills" LIST (multiple bills, each with its own "❌ #N" button) — editing THAT message in place on one delete tap would wipe out every other bill's button, so Owner confirmed scope: **single-receipt dupedit menu only**, Today's Bills list stays exactly as-is.

- **Design: new `dupdel:` prefix, not a change to `delask:`.** Reusing `delask:` for both callers and switching it to edit-in-place would have broken the Today's Bills list (proven by its own pinned tests in `tests/bot.delask.test.js`, all still green — untouched). `dupdel:` is a parallel handler with the same ownership/security checks (re-verifies `rep_chat_id` at confirm time, same as every other delete path here), just `edit()` instead of `send()`. The LAST step (confirm → "Deleted") needed zero changes — `del:<id>` already picked edit-vs-send based on whether the incoming update carries a `message_id`, and since `dupdel:` handed it a message that's already been edited (same message_id), the whole chain naturally stays on one bubble.

- **Tests: 1858/1858** (`.env` moved aside per the contamination gotcha, restored after; zero regressions; +7 new, +0/-0 net on existing files beyond the one button-callback assertion update).

- **What's next:** open PR for review; **not self-merging** — waiting for Barat's explicit go-ahead, same as every prior change this session. Bot needs a restart after this merges + deploys for the new one-message flow to take effect live.

- **Assumptions:** none — traced the exact 3-message chain in the code (`dupedit:`/`delask:`/`del:`) before writing anything, confirmed the Today's-Bills-list side effect with the Owner before scoping the fix.

- **Files:** `lib/statementPdf.js` (new `fitToWidth` single-line truncation helper used in `tableRow`; category-header row now writes the name into the wide `desc` column instead of the narrow `date` column). No test change (behaviour is pixel-level; page arithmetic unchanged — statement tests 29/29 + full suite 1864/1864 still green).
- **Why:** on a real render (Premala-shaped statement), long category names — "Printing & Stationery", "Travelling Expenses", "Hardware & Loose Tools" — wrapped to a 2nd line and overlapped the first data row. Looks unprofessional on a client-facing audit document.
- **Root cause — diagnosed empirically, not guessed:** the category name was drawn in the 66px-wide `date` column, and `tableRow` relied on pdfkit's `{ lineBreak:false, ellipsis:true }` to keep it on one line. A `doc.heightOfString(...)` probe proved those options are NOT honoured in this pdfkit build — a too-wide string still reports 19px (two lines) vs the 15px row height, so it overflows into the row below.
- **Fix (two parts):** (1) `fitToWidth(doc, str, maxW)` measures with `widthOfString` and hard-truncates with a plain-ASCII "..." (never the U+2026 glyph — same standard-Helvetica tofu risk as a Unicode arrow) so NO cell can ever wrap/overlap, a general safety net for every row. (2) the category name moves to the WIDE description column — matching the client's BUMI ERA sample (category label sits in DESCRIPTION) — so realistic names show in full rather than being truncated in a cramped cell.
- **Verification:** re-rendered the statement to PNG and eyeballed it — all six category names now sit on one clean line in the description column, no overlap, matching the sample. `manageExport` plain path unaffected (shares `tableRow` but its cells already fit). Full suite 1864/1864 (`.env` aside, restored).
- **What's next:** push, PR, wait for explicit merge word. Companion task (setting Premala's company name on the statement header) is a separate production-config decision — raised with the Owner, not bundled here.
- **Assumptions:** none — the fix is confined to text fitting/placement; totals, amounts, and page counts are unchanged.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Opus, for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-statement-receipt-compilation — Statement PDF receipt appendix redesigned to match the client's sample ("big image + one-line annotation", no placeholder pages)

- **Files:** `lib/statementPdf.js` (redesigned the `receiptEvidence` appendix — see below), `tests/statementPdf.test.js` (rewrote the 3 evidence-mode page-arithmetic tests to the new behaviour; net test count unchanged). NO change to `routes/statement.js` (already passes `receiptEvidence:true` and only consumes the buffer) or `routes/manageExport.js` (plain path untouched).
- **Why:** Owner compared our export against a client's real sample (`Petty cash summary_Mugilan.pdf` — a hand-assembled Excel summary page followed by bare full-page receipt screenshots). Verdict: the summary/excel page already matches; the target is the **receipt compilation** below it. #194's first cut annotated each receipt with a 6-line caption block AND emitted a dashed "Receipt Not Available" placeholder page per missing receipt — neither is in the client's sample. Owner's steer: make the receipts *look how they're used to* (big, clean, full-page) **and** keep a light annotation "to help even more" — i.e. do both, not one or the other.
- **What changed (evidence mode only):**
  - **Image is the star:** one big, full-width, aspect-preserved receipt image per page (was a smaller image squeezed above a 6-line caption). A `RECEIPT COMPILATION` header opens the section on the first page; a small grey `Receipt #N` label sits top-left.
  - **Compact one-line annotation strip** at the bottom instead of the stacked caption: `date · description · (currency) amount · Staff · Branch · Ref`, only the parts that exist. Uses a WinAnsi-safe middot separator (`·`) — deliberately NOT a Unicode arrow/em-dash, which pdfkit's standard Helvetica renders as tofu (caught this in a mockup before shipping).
  - **No per-expense placeholder pages.** Missing/unreadable receipts are omitted from the compilation (matches the sample) and instead collected onto a SINGLE final "Expenses without an attached receipt (N)" list page — helpful for the admin without cluttering the pack with blank pages. Skipped entirely when every expense has a receipt. `skippedReceipts` diagnostic count unchanged.
- **Deliberately untouched:** the summary/statement page(s) (Owner said they're already good — dual-currency, category grouping, yellow/green totals, balance box, sign-off all as-is), and `drawPlainReceiptPage` (the `manageExport` "Export PDF with receipts" path stays byte-for-byte the old plain-image behaviour — its tests pass unmodified).
- **Verification:** TDD (red→green) on the page-arithmetic tests. Full suite **1864/1864** (`.env` aside, restored; zero regressions — `manageExport` plain-path tests still green). Beyond the unit tests (which can't introspect rendered pixels), **generated a real PDF from this code and rendered it**: the compilation page shows the big receipt image + `RECEIPT COMPILATION` header + the one-line strip (middots render correctly), and the "not attached" page lists the receiptless expenses — matching the approved mockup.
- **Known follow-ups (flagged, not silently skipped):** (a) a genuinely tall/narrow receipt leaves whitespace since the image is aspect-fit — acceptable, and real photos fill better than the A4-page test fixture used here; (b) the summary page's INR column still needs a real FX source to be non-zero on expense rows — left for a separate decision, Owner said the excel page is fine for now.
- **What's next:** push branch, open PR against `main`, wait for explicit merge word (never self-merge). Merge → auto-deploy; no bot restart needed (this is the web export path, not the bot).
- **Assumptions:** the "one summary page for all missing receipts" (vs total omission) was the Owner-approved middle ground ("omit placeholder pages, but a small note so the admin knows"). Receipt `#N` numbers real receipts only, in date order.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Opus, for Barat) · feature/bot-album-pdf-photo-storage — store the receipt PHOTO for Telegram album & PDF captures (dashboard thumbnails + PDF-export photos)

- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (`flushGroup` album path: after resolving each `g.fileIds[i]`→URL, also store a durable copy via the EXISTING `downloadAndStoreReceiptImage(url)` from `lib/ocr.js`, index-aligned, then `metaForImage(i)` carries `receipt_url: receiptUrls[i]`; `handlePdfDocument` PDF path: upload each rasterised page BUFFER via the EXISTING `uploadReceiptImage(buf)` from `lib/storage.js` — we already hold the bytes, no re-download — switch its single `meta` to a per-page `metaForImage(i)` carrying `receipt_url`, and pass `metaForImage` into the `runBatch` call; new imports of `downloadAndStoreReceiptImage`/`uploadReceiptImage`; both resolved through `deps` for hermetic tests). Tests: new `tests/bot.batch.photostorage.test.js` (album: each image stored once + receipt_url threaded per-image; storage-failure→null still saves; personal mode unchanged), extended `tests/bot.pdf.test.js` (+2: each page uploaded + receipt_url per page; storage-failure→null), and added hermetic `downloadAndStoreReceiptImage`/`uploadReceiptImage` stubs to the `baseDeps` of every existing test that drives an album or PDF (`tests/bot.batch.test.js`, `tests/bot.batch.dupfeedback.test.js`, `tests/bot.hardening.qa.test.js`, `tests/bot.pdf.qa.test.js`, `tests/bot.business.staffscope.test.js`).

- **Root cause — diagnosed by code inspection (two Explore passes), confirmed against the Owner's screenshot.** Owner sent receipts as a Telegram **album** ("Got your receipts — reading them all now… → Batch processed") and saw no photo on the dashboard. There are 4 ingest paths; only 2 stored the image: single-photo (`expenseFromPhoto`, `lib/ocr.js:703`) and dashboard bulk-upload (`routes/upload.js:141`). The **album** (`flushGroup`) and **PDF** (`handlePdfDocument`) paths never called any storage upload — their per-image meta only set `receipt_file_unique_id`, so rows saved with `receipt_url = null`. Downstream, the dashboard card thumbnail (`activity.js` `hasReceiptImage`) and PDF-export receipt append (`routes/manageExport.js`/`routes/statement.js`) both key off `receipt_url`, so null = no photo anywhere, silently. **Not a broken bucket** — the `receipts` bucket is live and the single-photo path uses it fine; this was a missing feature in exactly two ingest paths.

- **Fix mirrors the proven pattern** already shipped at `routes/upload.js:141‑144` (`uploadReceiptImage` per image → `receipt_url: path || null`). Fail-safe throughout: the store helpers never throw (null on failure) → the row still saves imageless, exactly as before. Once `receipt_url` is populated, the dashboard Cards view shows the thumbnail and PDF exports attach the photo **automatically — zero frontend/export-code change needed.**

- **Deliberately untouched (protect the working setup):** single-photo path, dashboard-upload path, all frontend, all export code. Personal mode is covered by a dedicated regression test (image still stored, no `branch_id` added).

- **Tests: 1856/1856** (`.env` moved aside per the contamination gotcha, restored after; zero regressions; +5 new tests, + hermetic stubs added to 5 existing files so a real bucket is never hit).

- **Known caveat (told the Owner):** rows saved BEFORE this fix (e.g. the 4 currently visible) had their image bytes discarded at capture time — this can't retro-fill them; they must be re-sent (as single photos) to get a stored photo. Everything captured after this deploys stores its photo automatically.

- **What's next:** open PR for review; **not self-merging** — waiting for the Owner's explicit go-ahead. Bot must be restarted after merge+deploy for the fix to run live (and its `.env` is currently missing `BOT_TOKEN`/`SB_URL`/`SB_KEY` — only `GROQ_KEY` present — so it can't run/be live-tested until those are restored).

- **Assumptions:** none — the two gap paths were confirmed by reading `flushGroup`/`handlePdfDocument` directly and comparing against the two working paths; `runBatch`'s `resolveMeta` already consumes `metaForImage(i)`, so no change to Jeevan's `lib/massupload.js` was needed.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Opus, for Barat) · feature/bot-skip-not-saved-clarity — Telegram bot: make "receipt NOT saved — please re-upload" loud and unmissable

- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (`SKIP_MESSAGES` blurry/not_receipt/unclear rewritten to explicitly say the receipt was NOT saved + ask to re-upload — shared by both the single-photo path and the album per-photo thumbnail re-send; catch-all read-failure reply aligned to the same wording), `lib/bot.js` (`formatBatchSummary` now prints a loud `⚠️ N receipt(s) were NOT saved — please re-send them` line right under the `✅ Batch processed` header whenever `skipped + failed + duplicates > 0`; `skipFooter` reworded from "skipped rather than guessed / nothing was guessed" → "NOT saved — please re-upload"). Tests: `tests/bot.batch.test.js` (updated 4 pinned-wording assertions, +3 new: loud NOT-saved header plural/singular + a clean-batch-has-no-warning guard), `tests/bot.batch.dupfeedback.test.js` (strengthened the album re-send caption test to assert NOT saved + re-upload), `tests/ocr.nonreceipt.test.js` (updated the `unclear` wording assertion, +1 new: every skip reason states NOT saved + re-upload).

- **Why — diagnosed a live report from Barat before writing any code.** Barat sent ~6 bill photos in one Telegram album; only ~4 appeared in the dashboard. Investigated (two Explore passes over `lib/bot.js`/`routes/webhook.js`/`lib/ocr.js` ingest + `grid.js`/`routes/expenses.js`/`lib/supabase.js` listing). **Root cause: the missing receipts were never saved — the bot refuses blurry/unreadable (`isImageUnclear`/`isEmptyReceipt`) and duplicate (`rejectDuplicates:true`) photos at ingest, by design.** Proved it was NOT a dashboard/branch-scope hiding bug: one of the album's own photos (Budi Madani RON95, 49.30) IS in the dashboard, and all album photos share one binding/branch — so if one is visible, none are hidden. The bot already sent skip messages and even re-sent each blurry thumbnail, but the wording was too soft (led with a cheerful `✅ Batch processed`), so dropped photos were easy to miss.

- **Owner's decision (confirmed via AskUserQuestion before building):** keep blocking blurry/duplicate (do NOT force-save junk), but make the "this one was NOT saved — please re-upload it" message loud, explicit, and per-photo. This change is **messaging clarity only — no change to what gets saved, no DB/branch/scope changes.**

- **Reused, not rebuilt:** the per-skipped-photo thumbnail re-send already exists (`summary.skippedDetails{image_index,reason}` from `lib/massupload.js` → `flushGroup` re-sends `sendPhoto(fileIds[image_index], skipMessage(reason))`); only the TEXT `skipMessage` returns changed, so both single-photo and album paths get clearer wording from one edit.

- **Tests: 1851/1851** (`.env` moved aside per the contamination gotcha, restored after; zero regressions; wording-only diffs + new coverage).

- **Deliberately out of scope (flagged, not fixed):** (a) a blurry photo the model still reads *plausibly* is saved with maybe-wrong numbers and no warning — the opposite symptom (an extra row, not a lost one); (b) an album of >10 photos silently drops the 11th onward (`MAX_GROUP_IMAGES` cap) — could add a "only first 10 read" notice later. Both noted for possible follow-up.

- **What's next:** open PR for review; **not self-merging** — waiting for Barat's explicit go-ahead. Optional live check (via the receipt-saas-bot skill): send a mixed album (2 clear + 1 blurry) → confirm the clear ones save, the blurry one returns its own thumbnail saying "NOT saved — please re-upload," and the summary shows the loud NOT-saved warning.

- **Assumptions:** none — diagnosis is a direct code read plus a screenshot cross-check, confirmed with Barat before any edit.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-j-multibranch — issue #164 (QA PR-J, C2): data-model finding — STOP AND REPORT, not built

- **Files:** `tests/auth.test.js` (+1 regression-guard test). No feature code — this is a scoped-out finding per the issue's own rules ("make it testable either way, even if the output is just a written finding").
- **The load-bearing unknown, resolved:** issue #164 asked whether one clerk can already be bound to 2+ branches before building a branch/staff filter for multi-branch clerks. Checked both layers: `lib/auth.js` `scopeForUser` requires and returns exactly ONE `branch_id` per clerk (throws if missing), and `schema.sql`'s `users` table stores `branch_id` as a single scalar FK — no `users<->branches` join table exists anywhere in the schema. **Confirmed: the data model does not support multi-branch clerks today.**
- **Why this stops here instead of getting built around:** per the issue's explicit instruction, this is an architecture decision (a join table, or a scope-shape change to carry a branch LIST), not a UI-only filter — and per house rule, an unclear architecture call goes back to the Owner, not silently decided by whoever's building the ticket. Options for the Owner, laid out in the issue #164 comment: (a) add a `user_branches` join table + widen `scopeForUser`/`scopeEq` to filter on a branch list; (b) keep one-clerk-one-branch and instead give a "watches multiple branches" person the `admin` role scoped down some other way (e.g. a future company-scoped role); (c) something else — Owner's call.
- **Tests: 1847/1847** (`.env` moved aside per the GOTCHA — none present this session; zero regressions; +1 new). The new test locks in the CURRENT single-branch-only contract as a regression guard, so if it ever needs to change, that's the signal the architecture decision has actually been made.
- **What's next:** posted the finding as a comment on issue #164 (does NOT close it — it's the Owner's decision to make). Small PR opened for the regression-guard test only. Not self-merging.
- **Assumptions:** none — this is a direct code+schema read, not an inference.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-q-credkey-autogen — recoverable branch passwords now actually work in prod (auto-generate CRED_ENC_KEY)

- **Files:** `lib/credCrypto.js` (new `ensureCredKey()` — persisted auto-key), `server.js` (boot-time `ensureCredKey()` before `app.listen`), `.gitignore` (+`.cred_key`), `.env.example` (CRED_ENC_KEY now auto-provisioned + `CRED_ENC_KEY_FILE`). Tests: `tests/credKey.test.js` (**new**, 5).
- **Root cause (diagnosed, not assumed):** the recoverable-password feature (PR #185) was live but INERT — the dashboard showed "No password on file" for EVERY branch, new and old. `lib/credCrypto.js` `key()` returns null when `CRED_ENC_KEY` is unset, and that key was never set on the VPS `.env` (deploy uses `git archive`, which excludes `.env`; nobody set it by hand). So `encryptSecret()` returned null on every `createBranch` → no recoverable copy stored. Confirmed the mechanism by reading the code path; the empty-key state on prod matches the observed "no password" symptom on a freshly-created branch.
- **Fix (Owner-approved via AskUserQuestion: auto-generate on the server, chosen over a manual env var / GitHub secret / CTO SSH):** `ensureCredKey()` — explicit `CRED_ENC_KEY` env still wins; otherwise reuse a persisted key file (`CRED_ENC_KEY_FILE`, default `.cred_key` in the app root) or generate `randomBytes(32)` once and persist it `0600`. Called synchronously at boot in `server.js` before serving, so the first `createBranch` already has a key. Fail-safe: a write failure still yields an in-memory key for that boot (never crashes startup). Key value is never logged (only which path: env vs auto-provisioned).
- **Survives redeploys (verified against `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`):** `git archive` never bundles `.cred_key` (gitignored); the deploy's `tar -xzf … -C /opt/receipt-analyser` extracts over existing files and does NOT delete untracked ones; `npm ci` only touches `node_modules`; `pm2 restart` preserves the fs. So the key generated on first boot stays stable across every future deploy → previously-encrypted passwords stay readable.
- **Security note:** the key now lives on the VPS filesystem (same box that already holds `SB_KEY`, i.e. full DB access) rather than "operator-held only" — a real posture shift from PR #185's wording, explicitly chosen by the Owner for a zero-config solution. Does not widen the blast radius (an attacker with the box already has the DB service key). `password_hash` (scrypt, one-way) remains the ONLY thing login checks; `password_enc` is re-display only.
- **Tests: full suite 1825/1825** (hermetic — no `.env` AND no `.cred_key` written into the repo during the run; unit tests point `CRED_ENC_KEY_FILE` at `os.tmpdir()` and set `CRED_ENC_KEY` explicitly, so they never hit the generate-in-repo path). Verified `tests/branches.test.js`'s `createBranch`-with-no-key cases still expect `password_enc:null` and pass (ensureCredKey runs only in server boot, never in those unit tests).
- **Inherent caveat (no code fixes it):** branches created BEFORE this deploys have `password_enc=null` (encryption was off) and still show "No password on file" until the admin clicks **Reset password once** (stores a fresh recoverable password). No backfill possible — the original plaintext was never stored. Branches created/reset AFTER this show the SAME password on every 👤 view, until Reset — exactly the requested behaviour.
- **What's next:** merge → auto-deploys. Live verify: server log shows key auto-provisioned; a NEW branch shows its password on repeat 👤 clicks (stable); an old branch needs one Reset.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-p-bot-username-autodetect — bot-binding link now clickable on WhatsApp (auto-detect BOT_USERNAME)

- **Files:** `lib/telegram.js` (new `resolveBotUsername()` — Telegram `getMe`, fail-safe, exported), `server.js` (boot-time non-blocking fill of `cfg.BOT_USERNAME` when unset), `.env.example` (BOT_USERNAME documented as optional/auto-detected). Tests: `tests/telegram.test.js` (+4).
- **Root cause (confirmed against the LIVE server, not assumed):** `curl -sk https://31.97.70.50/api/config` returned `"bot_username":""`. The frontend `botDeepLink()` returns null on an empty handle, so both the credential panel and `copyBlockText()` (Issue 4's copy-all block) fell back to printing the RAW bind token instead of a `https://t.me/<bot>?start=<token>` URL — which WhatsApp can't render as a tappable blue link. The code was correct; `BOT_USERNAME` was simply never set on the VPS `.env`.
- **Fix (Owner-approved: auto-detect, not a manual env var):** the server calls Telegram `getMe` (using the bot token it already has) at boot and fills `cfg.BOT_USERNAME` when blank — so the deep link works with zero config and self-heals if the bot is ever swapped. Non-blocking (fired in the `app.listen` callback next to the existing `registerWebhook` boot call) so it can NEVER delay `/health` and race the deploy pipeline's post-deploy health check. An explicit `BOT_USERNAME` env var still wins (operator override). Fail-safe throughout: a `getMe` hiccup leaves the handle blank, i.e. exactly today's behaviour, no regression.
- **No frontend/schema change** — `botDeepLink()`/`copyBlockText()` already build the correct URL the moment `bot_username` is non-empty (verified live that the served `team.js` already contains them, PR #185).
- **Tests: full suite 1811/1811** (hermetic, no `.env`; +4 net new — `resolveBotUsername` returns/strips-@/null-on-failure/never-throws). The `getMe` stub is GET-shaped (no request body), unlike the existing POST `stubTelegram`.
- **What's next:** merge → auto-deploys to the VPS. Post-deploy verify: `/api/config` `bot_username` is now populated; the copy-all block's `Bot link:` line is a real `https://t.me/...` URL; pasted into WhatsApp it's blue/tappable.
- **Assumptions:** the bot has a username (every BotFather bot does — required at creation). If `getMe` ever fails at boot, the handle stays blank and the link degrades to the raw code exactly as before — no worse than today.

## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-o-credentials-recoverable — Admin dashboard QA round, Issues 1+4: view branch credentials anytime + copy-all icon

- **Files:** `lib/credCrypto.js` (**new** — AES-256-GCM encrypted-at-rest secret storage), `lib/config.js` (+`CRED_ENC_KEY`), `lib/branches.js` (`createBranch` also stores `password_enc`; new `getBranchCredentials`/`resetBranchPassword`), `routes/branches.js` (new `GET /api/branches/:id/credentials`, `POST /api/branches/:id/reset-password`, both admin-only + security-event logged), `schema.sql` (`users.password_enc`), `public/js/components/team.js` (`viewedCredentialPanel`/`copyBlockText` pure functions; `renderCredPanel` reused for both creation AND re-view, copy-all icon, Reset-password button for legacy branches; user-icon button on each branch card), `public/css/app.css`, `.env.example`. Tests: `tests/credCrypto.test.js` (**new**, 6), `tests/branches.credentials.test.js` (**new**, 9), `tests/branches.credentials.route.test.js` (**new**, 7), `tests/frontend/team.test.js` (+8).
- **⚠️ Deliberate security-policy change — Owner-approved TWICE in-session (once in plan mode, once again explicitly re-confirmed via AskUserQuestion after the harness itself flagged the tradeoff and paused for re-confirmation before I touched the route wiring):** branch-clerk passwords are now ALSO stored encrypted-at-rest (recoverable), reversing ADR-025's original hash-only design. This is a real reduction in the "even we can't see your password" guarantee — recorded here for anyone auditing later. Mitigations built in from the start, not bolted on:
  - **Never plaintext at rest.** `password_enc` is AES-256-GCM ciphertext, keyed by `CRED_ENC_KEY` (operator-held, never in the DB). `password_hash` (scrypt, one-way) remains the ONLY thing login actually checks — `password_enc` exists purely for re-display.
  - **Fails closed, not open.** No `CRED_ENC_KEY` configured -> `encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret` both return `null` -> nothing is ever stored in a recoverable form and the dashboard shows "no password on file" (same UX as a legacy branch) rather than silently falling back to plaintext.
  - **Every view is logged.** `GET .../credentials` and the reset endpoint both call `logSecurity('cred_view'|'cred_reset', { branch_id, admin_user_id })` — an audit trail of who looked at which branch's login and when.
  - **Admin-only**, same `businessOnly`/`checkDash`/rate-limited gate as the rest of the branch-CRUD surface; a clerk gets 403.
  - **Reset still exists and is the recommended remediation** on suspected compromise — revokes the clerk's live sessions immediately, same contract as `softDeleteBranch`'s revoke.
- **Issue 4 (copy-all icon), built into the SAME panel:** one tap copies `Username / Password / Bot link` as a single paste-ready block (`copyBlockText`, omits the password line entirely for a legacy branch rather than printing "null"). The bot-binding link stays a plain `https://t.me/...` URL (unchanged), so it stays live/blue when pasted into WhatsApp.
- **Tests: full suite 1800/1800** (hermetic, no `.env` present; +30 net new). `CRED_ENC_KEY` is set at the top of the new test files themselves (before their first `require` of anything that reads it) rather than via a shared env fixture — `node --test`'s default per-file process isolation makes this safe without leaking into other test files; verified by re-running the pre-existing `branches.test.js`/`team.test.js` afterward with zero regressions.
- **What's next:** Owner/reviewer merges (not self-merged). This is the last of the 5 admin-dashboard QA-round items (#2/#3 → PR #183, #5 → PR #184, this → this PR). Once `CRED_ENC_KEY` is set on the live server, EXISTING branches remain "no password on file" until their next Reset (there is no backfill — the original plaintext was never stored, only the hash, so there's nothing to encrypt retroactively).
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the Owner-approved policy change itself, confirmed explicitly in-session before any route/DB wiring was written.
