## 2026-07-11 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-n-bot-photo-storage — Admin dashboard QA round, Issue 5: Telegram bot photos now get a durable copy (root cause of "missing receipt photos")

- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (new `downloadAndStoreReceiptImage()` helper + wired into `expenseFromPhoto`, imports `hreq`/`uploadReceiptImage`). Tests: `tests/ocr.photostorage.test.js` (**new**, 5).
- **Investigation first (QA doc `qa 2 (2).md` Issue 5: "recipe [receipt] photos missing from certain column boxes"), per the plan's own instruction not to guess a fix blind.** Traced `receipt_url`/`receipt_file_id` through both ingestion paths:
  - **Root cause found:** ADR-018's durable receipt-image storage (`lib/storage.js` `uploadReceiptImage`, private Supabase bucket) was wired into the **dashboard manual-upload route only** (`routes/upload.js`). The **Telegram bot's own photo path** (`expenseFromPhoto` in `lib/ocr.js`) never called it at all — `receipt_url` was `undefined` for every bot-captured receipt (confirmed via the pre-existing code comment on that line: "optional, undefined for every existing caller"). Bot-sourced receipts (the large majority of all receipts, since Telegram is the primary capture channel) relied ENTIRELY on the live Telegram `file_id` proxy (`routes/receipt.js`'s `serveTelegram` → `tgFileUrl` → Telegram's `getFile` API using the current `BOT_TOKEN`) to redisplay the photo on every dashboard view — which is fragile by the storage module's own documented rationale (`lib/storage.js`'s top comment literally calls the old proxy-only approach "fragile ... breaks on bot-token rotation") and Telegram doesn't guarantee `file_id`s stay resolvable indefinitely.
  - **Secondary, smaller finding (not fixed here, documented for awareness):** `uploadReceiptImage` is fail-safe-by-design (never throws, returns `null` on any storage error) — on the dashboard-upload path this means a storage hiccup silently drops the image with zero admin-facing signal and no retry. Acceptable/intentional per its own doc comment; flagging in case Owner wants a retry/alert added later.
- **Fix (single-photo bot path, scoped deliberately — see What's next):** `downloadAndStoreReceiptImage(fileUrl, deps)` downloads the Telegram-hosted bytes and calls the same `uploadReceiptImage` the dashboard path already uses, returning the durable object path or `null`. Wired into `expenseFromPhoto` running in `Promise.all` alongside the OCR read (independent of it) so it adds zero latency to the user-visible reply. Same fail-safe contract throughout: a storage hiccup just means the row saves without a durable image, exactly the dashboard path's existing graceful-degrade — never blocks or delays the receipt-capture flow.
- **Tests: full suite 1776/1776** (hermetic, no `.env` present; +5 net new for the extracted helper, zero regressions). The `expenseFromPhoto` wiring itself has no direct network-stubbed integration test — consistent with this file's own established precedent (see the 2026-07-xx WORKLOG entry on the `awaitingDate` branch: "structurally identical to that already-proven code path").
- **What's next:** the batch/album path (`flushGroup` in `lib/bot.js`, issue #152's territory) has the exact same gap — its `assembleExpense` calls also never pass `receipt_url`. Deliberately NOT included in this PR to keep it reviewable; same fix pattern applies (download + `uploadReceiptImage` per image in the batch). Flagging as a direct follow-up, not forgotten. Owner/reviewer merges (not self-merged).
- **Assumptions:** none — purely additive (`receipt_url` was always `undefined` for bot captures before this; now it's populated best-effort). No schema change needed (the column already exists, added for the dashboard path).

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · qa/pr-l-batch-album (stacked on qa/pr-k-telegram) — Batch/album capture: staff attribution + per-photo failure feedback + duplicate handling (#152)
- **Files:** `lib/massupload.js` (`skippedDetails`, `duplicates`/`duplicateDetails` — additive to `runBatch`'s summary), `lib/telegram.js` (`tgSendPhoto`), `lib/ocr.js` (`receipt_file_unique_id` param + unconditional exact-resend reject, reusing #162's `duplicate`/`duplicateOf` shape), `lib/bot.js` (batch staff wizard, per-photo resend, batch duplicate replies, index-aligned downloads, `rejectDuplicates` extended to the batch path), `schema.sql` (+`receipt_file_unique_id` column/index). Tests: `tests/massupload.test.js` (+4), `tests/telegram.test.js` (+3), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+4), `tests/bot.business.staffscope.test.js` (5 assertions updated for the new `file_unique_id` meta field), `tests/bot.batch.dupfeedback.test.js` (**new**, 7), `tests/bot.batch.wizard.test.js` (**new**, 11).
- **What — TDD, RED→GREEN. Design signed off by Jeevan/Owner; branched on TOP of `qa/pr-k-telegram` (#162, unmerged) since this genuinely extends that PR's `duplicateRejectedText`/`buildDuplicateKeyboard`/`dupview:`/`dupedit:` infra — reused, not rebuilt.**
  1. **Staff attribution wizard:** `startBatchStaffWizard` — one self-editing message walks each saved receipt ("Receipt N of M · merchant · amount — who's this for?"), last-picked floats to top (re-applied every step, not just at start — a real bug caught by TDD), "👥 Same for all remaining" (no-ops until at least one pick exists — there's no "same as" without a first pick), "⏭ Skip" (leaves `awaiting_staff`, dashboard-fixable). Every item marked `awaiting_staff` up front (bulk), same #162 D11 principle; each pick restores that item's OWN prior status (e.g. `needs_review`) rather than clobbering it. Keyed by chatId (one wizard per chat at a time, same v1 simplification as `pendingBillDelete`).
  2. **Per-photo failure feedback:** `runBatch` now records `{image_index, receipt_index, reason}` per skip (`skippedDetails`) — the correlation the issue flagged as the hard part. `flushGroup` now downloads with **index alignment preserved** (a failed download is `null`, not silently dropped — a genuine bugfix: a partial download failure used to vanish with no `failed[]` entry at all) so `g.fileIds[image_index]` always resolves to the right photo; resends it via the new `tgSendPhoto` with a reason-specific caption (reuses `skipMessage()`, same wording as the single-photo path).
  3. **Duplicates:** extended `rejectDuplicates` (#162 D13) to the batch path — was single-photo-only, a real gap now closed. Caught and fixed a **latent counting bug in `runBatch`** in the process: a `duplicate:true` result still carries a truthy `expense` (built, never inserted) — without a dedicated `duplicates`/`duplicateDetails` bucket it would have silently miscounted as `saved`. Added **exact-resend detection** via Telegram's `file_unique_id` (stable across resends, unlike `file_id`) — a NEW, unconditional (not opt-in) check in `assembleExpense`, using the SAME already-fetched `history` array as the anomaly detector (one `loadHistory()` call now, was two). Applies to both the single-photo and batch paths. **Scope note:** #152's original spec distinguished "near-duplicate keeps saving + inline Undo" from "exact resend rejects" — superseded by #162's stricter live-QA-driven policy (any heuristic match rejects); flagging the supersession rather than silently diverging.
- **Tests: full suite 1697/1697** (hermetic — ran with no `.env` present at all; +29 net new, 5 existing assertions updated for the additive `meta.file_unique_id` field, zero regressions).
- **What's next:** Owner/reviewer merges #162 first (this branch is stacked on it), then this. `routes/webhook.js` needed no changes (thin generic transport shim, confirmed by reading it). Dashboard bulk-upload side (issue #153) is a separate lane (Jeevan/Mugi/Barat) — shares the same `duplicates`/`skippedDetails` engine additively, not touched here.
- **Assumptions:** `file_unique_id` is present on every real Telegram `PhotoSize`/`Document` object (test fixtures without it degrade to `undefined` → the exact-check simply no-ops, never crashes — verified). The "same for all remaining" semantics (stamps the last-picked/⭐ person, not a fresh pick) and the #152-vs-#162 duplicate-policy supersession are both judgment calls made explicit here for Owner review, not silently assumed.

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (for Jeevan) · qa/pr-i-bulk-select — QA PR-I: bulk select -> Approve/Delete

- **Files:** `routes/review.js` (new `POST /api/review/bulk`), `routes/expenses.js` (new `POST /api/expenses/bulk-delete`), `public/js/components/activity.js` (Select toggle, tickboxes, tick-all, Approve/Delete, confirm dialog), `public/css/app.css`, `tests/review.bulk.test.js` (new), `tests/expenses.bulkdelete.test.js` (new), `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`.
- **QA ID closed:** B4 — a **Select** button top-right of the Activity toolbar reveals a tickbox per row plus a **"Select all"** checkbox; once rows are ticked, **Approve (n)** / **Delete (n)** buttons appear; either opens a confirmation window before committing.
- **Backend, both new bulk endpoints are pure orchestration, no new business rule:** `POST /api/review/bulk {ids, decision}` loops the ids through the EXACT same `resolveDecision`/`canRoleDecide`/`buildReviewAudit` path the existing single-row `POST /api/review/:id` already uses; `POST /api/expenses/bulk-delete {ids}` loops through the same `canTouchExpense` + `sbSoftDelete` path the single `DELETE /api/expenses/:id` already uses. Each id is judged independently — one clerk-wrong-branch or non-decidable row fails on its own without aborting the rest of the batch; the response carries `{requested, updated|deleted, results:[{id,ok,error?}]}` so the frontend can reconcile exactly which ids landed. Both cap total ids per request (`BULK_REVIEW_MAX_IDS`/`BULK_DELETE_MAX_IDS`, default 200, DoS-adjacent) and are registered before their `:id` sibling routes (same convention as `/api/expenses/payments`) so Express never swallows `bulk`/`bulk-delete` as an `:id`.
- **Frontend:** table and card views both grow an optional tickbox (table: leading `<td>`; card: absolute-positioned top-left) gated on `state.selectMode`, always `stopPropagation()`'d so ticking never also opens the edit drawer. The confirm dialog (`showBulkConfirm`) is a byte-for-byte port of `templates.js`'s existing `showConfirmDialog` pattern, reusing its `.tp-confirm-*` CSS — no new confirm-modal styling. After a bulk action completes, select mode exits, the table reloads from the server, and a one-line outcome summary ("All 3 approved." / "2 of 3 deleted — 1 failed.") is shown next to the Select button for that render.
- **Tests:** TDD red-first for the backend (`tests/review.bulk.test.js`: admin bulk-approve, admin-override on an already-decided row, clerk cross-branch id fails individually while own-branch succeeds, invalid decision 400, empty/missing ids 400, over-cap 413 — 6/6; `tests/expenses.bulkdelete.test.js`: admin bulk-delete, clerk mixed-branch partial success, empty/missing ids 400, over-cap 413 — 4/4) and frontend pure helpers (`bulkApprovePath`, `bulkDeletePath`, `allSelected`, `toggleAllSelected`, `summarizeBulkResult`) added to `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`. Full suite **1664/1664** green (`.env` moved aside/restored).

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (for Jeevan) · qa/pr-h-recently-deleted — QA PR-H: Recently-deleted bin + Recover

- **Files:** `routes/expenses.js` (new `GET /api/expenses/deleted` + `POST /api/expenses/:id/restore`), `public/js/components/activity.js` (bin button + "Recently deleted" overlay panel), `public/css/app.css`, `tests/expenses.recentlydeleted.test.js` (new), `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`.
- **QA ID closed:** B7 — bin icon top-right of the Activity toolbar opens a "Recently deleted" page listing soft-deleted bills, with a Recover action per row.
- **Confirmed first** (per the plan): `expenses.deleted_at` already exists (`schema.sql` line 32, "soft-delete: non-null = hidden from dashboard (recoverable)") — this is a **view + restore on existing data, no schema change**, as anticipated.
- **Backend:** `GET /api/expenses/deleted` mirrors `GET /api/expenses`'s scope/visibility contract exactly (clerk own-branch, admin all) but with `deleted_at=not.is.null` instead of `is.null`, ordered newest-deleted-first. `POST /api/expenses/:id/restore` clears `deleted_at` via the same scoped `sbUpdate` every other write uses (clerk own-branch only, admin anywhere via `canTouchExpense`); refuses 409 on a row that isn't currently deleted so Recover can't double as a silent no-op PATCH.
- **Frontend:** a "🗑 Recently deleted" button (pushed to the right end of the toolbar via `margin-left:auto`) opens an overlay panel — reuses the exact same `.act-drawer-host`/`.act-drawer` modal CSS as the existing edit drawer (separate host element so the two never clobber each other). Each deleted row shows merchant/amount/date + a Recover button; clicking Recover POSTs the restore, removes the row from the panel, and reloads the main table so the recovered bill reappears there immediately.
- **Tests:** TDD red-first for the backend (`tests/expenses.recentlydeleted.test.js`: list filters to deleted-only, clerk branch-scoped list, admin restore, non-deleted-row refused 409, not-found 404, clerk cross-branch refused 403, clerk own-branch allowed — 7/7) and frontend pure path-builders (`deletedRowsPath`, `restorePath`) added to `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`. Full suite **1654/1654** green (`.env` moved aside/restored).
- **Live-verified** via `window.Activity.mount()` stub-mounting in the browser preview (no jsdom in this repo): bin button renders and opens the panel; panel fetches and lists the deleted row; clicking Recover issues exactly `POST /api/expenses/del-1/restore`, the panel item is removed (empty-state message shown), and the main table refetches and now includes the recovered merchant alongside the still-live row.
- **What's next:** PR-I (B4 bulk select), PR-J (C2 multi-branch clerk — data-model check first), PR-K (D10–D13 Telegram bot). Not merging this PR myself — Owner/reviewer to merge.

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (for Jeevan) · qa/pr-g-rejected — QA PR-G: rejected-bill recategorise + permanent delete

- **Files:** `lib/supabase.js` (new `sbHardDelete`), `routes/expenses.js` (new `DELETE /api/expenses/:id/permanent`), `public/js/components/activity.js` (Status-cell chevron + delete icon), `public/css/app.css`, `tests/expenses.harddelete.test.js` (new), `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`.
- **QA IDs closed:** B9 (rejected → recategorise inline via a (v) chevron on the Status column, reusing the exact PATCH shape the awaiting-category picker already used: `{category, status:'saved'}`) and B8 (permanent delete on rejected bills — **ONE CLICK, explicitly NO confirmation dialog per Owner spec**, unlike every other delete in this app which uses a two-step confirm).
- **B9:** clicking the chevron on a rejected row's Status cell opens an inline category picker (same categories list as everywhere else); picking one PATCHes `category` + `status:'saved'`, which moves the bill out of the Rejected filter into ordinary saved state. Wired in both the table and card views via a single shared `buildStatusCell()` builder so neither view drifts from the other.
- **B8:** new backend contract — `DELETE /api/expenses/:id/permanent` hard-deletes (via new `sbHardDelete`) **iff** the row's current `status === 'rejected'`; anything else (saved/needs_review/approved/not-found/wrong-branch-for-a-clerk) refuses (409/404/403) with nothing written. Frontend fires this immediately on the delete-icon click — no confirm step, matching the Owner's explicit instruction not to "fix" this into consistency with the rest of the app.
- **Both actions `stopPropagation()`** inside the Status cell so they never also trigger the row/card's open-drawer click.
- **Tests:** TDD red-first for the backend (`tests/expenses.harddelete.test.js`: admin hard-delete, non-rejected row refused, not-found, clerk-cross-branch refused, clerk-own-branch allowed — 5/5) and for the frontend pure helpers (`isRejected`, `recategorizeBody`, `permanentDeletePath` — added to `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`). Full suite **1654/1654** green (`.env` moved aside/restored), up from 1644.
- **Live-verified** (no jsdom in this repo, so DOM behaviour is checked via `window.Activity.mount()` stub-mounted in the real browser preview with a fake `apiFetch`, per this repo's existing verification convention): chevron toggles the picker without opening the drawer; selecting a category and clicking Apply issues exactly `PATCH /api/expenses/rej-1 {category:"Software", status:"saved"}` and the row leaves the Rejected bucket; clicking the delete icon issues exactly one `DELETE /api/expenses/rej-2/permanent` with **no intermediate confirmation UI**, no drawer opens, and the row is removed from view immediately.

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (for Jeevan) · qa/review-card-details — review-queue cards show the receipt + details

- **Files:** `public/js/components/activity.js` (`reviewMetaParts` pure helper + rebuilt `buildReviewItem` + `onOpen` wired into `renderReview`), `public/css/app.css` (review-item now a thumbnail+details flex row), `tests/frontend/activity.test.js`.
- **Why (Owner request 2026-07-10):** the Activity **review queue** cards showed only merchant + amount + the flag reason. To actually *see* a flagged receipt before Approve/Reject you had to leave and open it separately — inconvenient. Now each card shows the receipt itself so it can be judged in place.
- **What's on the card now:** a **receipt thumbnail** (image, or a "Receipt PDF" link for PDFs — via the same auth'd `loadReceiptImage`/`hasReceiptImage` the table & card views already use), a **meta line "date · category · ref"** (`reviewMetaParts`, which drops blanks and never leaks the `__pending__` sentinel), the **remark** (staff/OCR note — useful context), and the existing flag reason + Approve/Reject. **Clicking the thumbnail opens the full detail drawer** (`onOpen`) for a closer look.
- **Scope note (staff):** the expense row only carries `staff_user_id`/`branch_id` as **UUIDs** — no human name without a staff-directory lookup (which clerks can't call: `/api/branches` 403s them). Rather than show a raw UUID, staff-name is left out; adding it would mirror `payments.js`'s best-effort directory fetch and is a clean follow-up if wanted. Everything shown is directly displayable, real data.
- **Tests:** TDD red-first — `reviewMetaParts` (order date→category→ref, skips blanks + `__pending__`, garbage-tolerant, never throws). Full suite **1647/1647** green (`.env` moved aside/restored), +3 over the 1644 baseline. The card DOM itself is render-layer (no jsdom here) so it's verified live.
- **Live-verified** in the browser preview via stub-mounted `Activity`: a needs_review row with a receipt renders the thumbnail (image loads), the meta line "2026-07-01 · Food · RC-2026-0041", the remark "client lunch", and the flag reason; clicking the thumbnail opens the full drawer titled "Flagged Vendor"; a receipt-less needs_review row degrades gracefully (no thumbnail, meta "2026-07-03 · Others" + everything else intact). No console errors; `.act-review-item` computes to the intended flex row.
- **What's next:** not merging myself — Owner/reviewer to merge. NB this touches `activity.js` like my open PRs #160/#161/#165; distinct function (`buildReviewItem`) so conflicts should be minimal, but whichever merges first, the rest may need a trivial rebase.

## 2026-07-10 · Claude (for Jeevan) · qa/pr-a3-review-reminder — QA A3 refinement: conditional pre-export review reminder

- **Files:** `public/js/exportGuards.js` (new `countUnreviewed`, `reviewReminderMessage`, `runExportGuard`), `public/js/components/reports.js` + `payments.js` + `grid.js` (all three export buttons rewired), `tests/frontend/exportGuards.test.js`.
- **Why:** Owner clarified A3. Codex's PR-A shipped an **always-on** generic "Confirm you've reviewed this data before exporting?" modal that fired on *every* export regardless of whether anything actually needed reviewing — annoying and uninformative. The Owner wants a **conditional reminder**: only interrupt when there are receipts still in the review queue, say how many, and offer a non-blocking **"Download anyway"** (a reminder, not a hard gate).
- **What counts as "unreviewed" (Owner decision, 2026-07-10):** the **review queue only** — receipts with `status === 'needs_review'` (the ones sitting in Activity's review queue awaiting Approve/Reject). Blank category/remark do NOT trigger it. Count comes from the already-scoped `GET /api/review` (clerk = own branch, admin = all), so the reminder respects isolation for free.
- **Behaviour** (`runExportGuard`, the single entry point all three export buttons now share): empty view → "No data to export." (unchanged); **0 unreviewed → export runs immediately, no popup**; **>0 unreviewed → reminder "You still have N receipt(s) waiting for review in Activity. Download anyway?"** with `Download anyway` / `Go review`; **count unknown** (no apiFetch, or the `/api/review` fetch errored) → falls back to the old generic reviewed-confirm so there is always *some* checkpoint and it never blocks or silently skips.
- **Tests:** TDD red-first — `countUnreviewed` (counts only `needs_review`, garbage-tolerant), `reviewReminderMessage` (singular/plural + count), and `runExportGuard` decision matrix (empty→onEmpty/no-fetch; 0→proceed/no-popup; >0→reminder-with-count/`Download anyway`, proceeds only on confirm; fetch-error and no-apiFetch→generic fallback). The DOM modal is dependency-injected (`opts.confirm`) so the orchestrator is fully testable without a browser. Full suite **1652/1652** green (`.env` moved aside/restored), +8 over the 1644 baseline.
- **Live-verified** in the browser preview (real modal DOM, not the injected fake): 2 unreviewed → popup reads "You still have **2 receipts** waiting for review in Activity. Download anyway?" with `Download anyway` + `Go review`, no export before clicking; 1 unreviewed → correct singular "**1 receipt**"; clicking `Download anyway` proceeds and closes; **0 unreviewed → no popup at all, export runs directly**. No console errors.
- **What's next:** not merging myself — Owner/reviewer to merge. This closes the last open item from Codex's PR-A/E/F scope (A3's pre-export confirm). Remaining QA-round work: my PRs #160/#161/#165 + Barat's issues #162/#164 all awaiting review, then VPS deploy.

A6 was already substantially implemented on `main` by `routes/statement.js` + `lib/statementPdf.js` (PDF statement appends image receipts and rasterised source-PDF pages); this branch preserves that path and leaves the existing renderer/route tests green. There is no separate **"See Report"** button/string in the current codebase (`rg "See Report"` returned none), so the PR will flag that as an Owner/product wording mismatch rather than inventing a new surface.
- **Tests:** TDD red first on the new frontend tests (missing `exportGuards`, `statementExportRows`, `receiptKind`/`receiptLabel`), then green. Full suite **1635/1635** with `.env` moved aside/restored.
- **What's next:** push branch and open PR against `main`; stop for Claude-J review/merge per QA workflow.
- **Assumptions:** Status-line text counts as the requested popup/toast for empty exports on these existing screens; the shared confirmation modal is the canonical popup for extracts.

## 2026-07-09 · Claude-J (for Jeevan) · feat/jeevan-m2-business-frontend (5th push) — security fix: Agent branch-isolation bypass (critical) + HQ-owned template/category authorization + true categories-only save + legacy-category preservation

- **Context.** Owner requested an independent second opinion on PR #154 from GPT-5.5 Codex, run out-of-session; its findings landed at `docs/reviews/2026-07-09-pr154-independent-review.md` (also added `AGENTS.md`, Codex's own guide file mirroring `CLAUDE.md`). Per fable-mode discipline, every claimed finding was independently re-traced against the actual code (not taken on faith) before any fix — see verification notes per finding below. Four confirmed findings were fixed this push; the rest (concurrency CAS, statement branch-selector, config-endpoint exposure, Manage retry, CSV-guard hardening) are deferred, listed at the end.

- **Finding #1 (CRITICAL, confirmed) — Agent chat bypassed branch isolation.** `routes/agent.js` built `writeDeps` ONCE at `mountAgent()` call time (module scope), so `req.scope` (per-request, set by `checkDashSession`) never reached `lib/agent.js`'s `sbSelect`/`sbUpdate`/`sbSoftDelete` calls. Verified directly: `lib/supabase.js`'s `scopeEq(undefined)` returns `{}`, so an unscoped call filters ONLY by `account_id` — a branch-A clerk's Agent chat (`listExpenses`, auto-confirmed edits, confirmed deletes) could read and write every other branch's expenses, the exact invariant M2 exists to protect. **Fix:** `routes/agent.js` now builds a fresh `scopedDeps(req)` per request, injecting the request's trusted `req.scope` AFTER any caller-supplied opts (same "scope always wins" contract `stampScope()` already uses), passed into both `runAgent()` and `confirmProposal()`. `req.scope` is `undefined` in personal/static-token mode (`checkDash` never sets it there), so this is byte-identical/no-op for personal accounts.
- **Finding #3 (HIGH, confirmed) — a clerk could overwrite the whole account's HQ-owned template/categories.** ADR-021: "the FieldSpec is HQ-owned... staff contribute rows, never columns." `POST /api/templates` had only `checkDash` (authentication), no role check — any signed-in clerk could replace the account-wide OCR extraction fields and category set (which the category editor I added earlier this session made directly reachable from the clerk's own Manage tab). **Fix:** new `adminWriteOnly` middleware on `POST /api/templates` — 403 when `req.scope.role !== 'admin'`; a no-op when `req.scope` is unset (personal mode). Same policy applied to the Agent's `proposeAddTemplateField`/`ADD_TEMPLATE_FIELD` tool (both propose-time and confirm-time, matching every other kind's re-validate-fresh pattern) — a clerk asking the assistant to add a field is now refused too.
- **Finding #4 (HIGH, confirmed, my own new code from earlier this session) — categories-only save could revert/lose columns.** The category editor (`templates.js`) resent a locally-cached snapshot of `columns` on every categories save because `POST /api/templates` always overwrote `columns` from whatever csv it was given; a stale/racing snapshot could silently revert a concurrent columns save (verified: a fast double-click sequence — Save template, then Save categories before the first's response lands — reproduces it deterministically). **Fix, architectural not cosmetic:** new `lib/templates.js` `saveCategories()` — a TRUE partial update that never reads OR writes `columns` at all (PostgREST only touches JSON keys present in a PATCH body, so omitting `columns` leaves the DB row's columns completely untouched regardless of timing). `routes/templates.js` routes a JSON body with `categories` and no `csv` to this new path. The client (`templates.js`) no longer tracks/resends columns for a categories save at all — `columnsToCsvHeader()` and the stale `currentColumns` cache were deleted outright (dead code after the fix, not kept "just in case").
- **Finding #6 (MEDIUM, confirmed — also the Owner's own live-reported bug: "if i press on it and if i didnt select category it just turns to blank").** `grid.js`'s category `<select>` set `.value` to the row's stored category unconditionally; if that category had since been renamed/removed from the account's list, no `<option>` matched, so the select silently resolved to `''` — and blur-without-choosing committed that empty string, erasing the classification. **Fix:** the row's current value is now always injected as a real `<option>` first (labeled "(not in category list)") when it's not in the account's list, so `.value` always matches something and blur-without-choosing preserves it. Live-verified: legacy category "Old Travel" not in the account's list → cell shows it selected → blur without choosing → **zero PATCH requests fired** (previously would have silently saved `category: ''`).
- **Files:** `routes/agent.js`, `lib/agent.js`, `routes/templates.js`, `lib/templates.js`, `public/js/components/grid.js`, `public/js/components/templates.js`, `tests/agent.route.test.js` (+6), `tests/agent.proposals.test.js` (+4), `tests/templates_route.test.js` (+6), `tests/templates.categories.test.js` (+6), `tests/frontend/templates.test.js` (−4 dead tests for the deleted `columnsToCsvHeader`), `docs/reviews/2026-07-09-pr154-independent-review.md` (Codex's review, new), `AGENTS.md` (Codex's guide file, new).
- **Tests: full suite 1625/1625** (`.env` aside, restored; +18 net new; zero regressions).
- **QA (live-in-browser, stub-mount, no real credentials):** legacy-category preservation verified end-to-end (select renders the legacy option, blur-without-choosing sends no PATCH); categories-only save verified to POST `{categories, name}` with no `csv` key at all, hitting the new safe server path.
- **What's next — deferred from the same review, NOT fixed this push (Owner should prioritize next):**
  - **Finding #2 (HIGH)** — the grid's optimistic-concurrency check is non-atomic (SELECT-then-PATCH, not a single atomic predicate) — a genuine lost-update race under concurrent edits. Needs `sbUpdate()` to accept an `expectedUpdatedAt` and fold it into the same PostgREST PATCH predicate (`updated_at=eq.<expected>`), which is a `lib/supabase.js` frozen-contract change — bigger, needs its own careful pass + a deterministic concurrent-request test.
  - **Finding 5 (MEDIUM)** — Payments' branch selector is built from loaded expense rows, so a branch with zero expenses (e.g. a brand-new branch) can't be selected for its own opening statement.
  - **Finding 7 (LOW)** — `GET /api/config` (unauthenticated, needed for pre-login bootstrap) now also exposes the account's custom category names; should split into public-bootstrap vs authenticated-account config.
  - **Finding 8 (LOW)** — `manage.js` marks a sub-page "loaded" before its load() promise resolves, so a transient load failure can't be retried without a full page reload.
  - **Possible Finding A** — the CSV formula-injection guard only checks the first raw character; whitespace/control-prefixed payloads (`\t=1+1`, `  =1+1`) aren't neutralized — needs verification against the actual target spreadsheet client before hardening the regex.
  - Full proposal-ownership binding (Finding 1's secondary concern — proposals have no creator identity, so possession of an ID is currently the only check) is now substantially mitigated as a side effect of the scoping fix (a wrongly-confirmed proposal now executes under the CONFIRMER's own scope, not the creator's, so cross-branch execution is blocked either way) but explicit binding is still a good hardening, not done here.
  - The Owner's UI wishlist from the same session (Team page redesign to match Mugi's concept file, grid excel-conveniences — date pickers/undo-redo, PDF export walkthrough) is untouched — this push was scoped entirely to the confirmed security/data-integrity findings.
- **Assumptions:** `req.scope` is the correct and only trust boundary for both branch isolation and role (mirrors every existing route's contract, `lib/auth.js`'s `scopeForUser`); no schema change was needed for any of these four fixes.

Verified through the **real** running server that `/api/config` now returns `excel_lite_grid: true` (the flag flip works end-to-end through the capability path), business mode on, 10 categories.
- **What's next:** Owner review + merge (PR #154, no self-merge). **OPS:** each live instance's `.env` (VPS) needs `CAP_EXCEL_LITE_GRID=true` + restart for the Data editor to appear there — flagged in `docs/clients/premala.md §3`. Only the repo's local `.env` was flipped this session.

Coverage split: `datePromptText` pure-function tests; `tryResolveDate` + `handleUpdate` integration tests (parseable/unparseable/no-pending/lookup-failure/oldest-first, mirroring `tryResolveRemark`'s existing coverage shape); `assembleExpense`-level regression tests locking the exact reported bug (trailing-timestamp date → high confidence + correct date; genuinely illegible date → low/flagged). The `expenseFromPhoto` trigger-side wiring itself (the `sbUpdate`+`tgSend` call in the new branch) has no direct network-stubbed test — consistent with the pre-existing `awaitingCategory` branch immediately above it in the same function, which also has none; structurally identical to that already-proven code path.

- **Assumptions:** none beyond what's already verified — `conf.perField.expense_date` is computed on the RAW model output (already the correct source per PR #150), so this branch fires only for genuinely unparseable dates, never for the trailing-timestamp case that PR #150 already fixed.

## 2026-07-08 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · fix/barat-issue149-date-confidence — fix: unreadable dates no longer silently pass as high-confidence (parts 1+3 of #149; part 2 is a design call, see PR)
- **Files:** `lib/money.js` (`parseDateLoose` — pure, never falls back; `normaliseDate` now built on it + tolerates a trailing time-of-day), `lib/confidence.js` (`isPresent` uses `parseDateLoose` for `type: 'date'` instead of a bare non-empty check), `tests/money.test.js` (+4), `tests/confidence.test.js` (+3). My files, no shared-contract signature change (`normaliseDate`/`isPresent` same signatures, `parseDateLoose` is a new additive export).
- **What — TDD, RED→GREEN. Live-QA bug from Premala (issue #149, Owner directive: "if the bot can't see something clearly it should say so, not guess").** Root cause: a real receipt's date carried a trailing OCR timestamp (`30/06/2026 12:46:53`); `normaliseDate`'s anchored regex didn't match it at all and silently fell back to `todayISO()` — and because `lib/confidence.js`'s `isPresent` only checked "is the raw string non-empty" for date fields, the model's (correct!) raw reading counted as high-confidence right as it was about to be silently overwritten with the wrong value. Two-part fix: (3) `normaliseDate` now strips a trailing `HH:MM[:SS]` before matching, so this exact case parses correctly instead of defaulting to today; (1) extracted the parsing into `parseDateLoose(raw)` — pure, returns `null` instead of ever guessing — and `isPresent` now requires a date to actually parse, not just be non-empty, so a truly garbled/illegible date now correctly scores 'low' and flows into the *already-existing* `needs_review`/⚠️ machinery in `lib/ocr.js`/`lib/bot.js` (no new plumbing needed there). Amount parity checked too: `isPresent` for currency/number already casts the RAW value with `Number()` independent of coercion, so a garbled amount was already low-by-default — added regression tests, no code change needed.
- **Tests: full suite 1538/1538** (`.env` aside, restored; +7 net new; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** Part 2 of #149 (having the bot proactively ASK for the field, mirroring the `awaitingCategory` picker, vs. today's passive "⚠️ Needs review — check your dashboard") is explicitly flagged in the issue as a UX design call for the Owner (which fields, threshold, wording) — not built here; Claude raised it back to Barat rather than guessing at bot copy for a live client-facing flow. Owner review + merge for this PR (no self-merge).
- **Assumptions:** `parseDateLoose` does not calendar-validate (e.g. doesn't reject month 13) — same scope as the pre-existing regex, not expanded; only the two issues named in #149 (trailing time, silent-pass) are addressed here.

## 2026-07-08 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · feat/barat-webhook-selfsigned-cert — feat: registerWebhook() accepts an optional self-signed cert path (Jeevan's follow-up from the Premala VPS deploy)
- **Files:** `lib/config.js` (`WEBHOOK_CERT_PATH`, optional), `lib/http.js` (`hreq` now passes a `FormData` body through untouched instead of `JSON.stringify`-ing it — `json:true` still governs response parsing only; no other caller passes FormData today, so this is additive), `lib/telegram.js` (`registerWebhook(certPath = cfg.WEBHOOK_CERT_PATH)`), `.env.example`, `tests/telegram.test.js` (+5). My files, no shared-contract change.
- **What — TDD, RED→GREEN.** Root cause (Jeevan, from the live Premala VPS deploy): `registerWebhook()` only ever sent a plain JSON `setWebhook` body. On a self-signed host, Telegram will not trust the cert unless it's uploaded WITH `setWebhook` via the multipart `certificate` field — the codified path silently doesn't work there at all; a manual `curl -F certificate=@...` was the only workaround (Premala is live today via that manual step). Fix: `registerWebhook` takes an optional `certPath` (defaults to the new `cfg.WEBHOOK_CERT_PATH` env var so `npm run setup-webhook` picks it up with zero call-site changes). When set, it reads the cert file and POSTs multipart/form-data (url/allowed_updates/secret_token/certificate) instead of JSON. Hosts with a real CA-trusted cert (Render/Fly) leave the var unset and get the exact unchanged JSON path.
- **Tests: full suite 1539/1539** (`.env` aside, restored; +5 net new for `registerWebhook`: skip-when-no-PUBLIC_URL, unchanged-JSON-path, multipart-with-cert, cfg-fallback-when-no-arg, never-throws-on-network-error; zero regressions elsewhere from the `hreq` FormData change).
- **What's next:** Owner review + merge (no self-merge). Optional follow-up: once Premala gets a real (Let's Encrypt) cert, `WEBHOOK_CERT_PATH` can just be unset — no code change needed to fall back to the plain path.
- **Assumptions:** Node's global `FormData`/`Blob` are available (Node 20 on the VPS, per the deploy record) — no new dependency added.

## 2026-07-08 · Claude (Sonnet, for Barat) · fix/barat-issue17-invalid-utf8 — fix: reject invalid-UTF-8 headers at the template parse boundary (closes remaining part of #17)
- **Files:** `lib/templates.js` (`hasReplacementChar` + check in `parseTemplate`), `tests/templates.test.js` (+1), `tests/templates_route.test.js` (flipped the "QA: invalid UTF-8" test from documenting-the-gap to asserting-the-fix). My file (`lib/templates.js` owner: Barat), no shared-contract change.
- **What — TDD, RED→GREEN.** Issue #17 had two parts: (1) control-char/NUL stripping — already closed by the earlier P6 fix (`stripControls`); (2) invalid-UTF-8 byte sequences silently lossy-decoded to U+FFFD and persisted — still open. Root cause: by the time a CSV/text body reaches `parseTemplate` as a JS string, body-parser has already lossily decoded any malformed UTF-8 to U+FFFD — the original bytes are gone, so the fix can only *detect* the replacement char and reject (400), not recover the original text. Added `hasReplacementChar()` and a check in `parseTemplate` right after the existing empty/too-many-columns boundary checks; flipped the RED test (`tests/templates_route.test.js` — previously asserted 200 + lossy label, now asserts 400) and added a direct unit test in `tests/templates.test.js`.
- **Tests: full suite 1534/1534** (`.env` aside, restored; +1 net new; zero regressions).
- **What's next:** Owner review + merge (no self-merge). Scoped to `parseTemplate` (CSV path) only — `parseExcelTemplate` reads through ExcelJS's own XML text decoding, not raw bytes, so it isn't exposed to the same lossy-decode path; left unchanged.
- **Assumptions:** none beyond the existing #17 QA-test documentation of current behavior.

## 2026-07-08 · Claude-J (Opus, fable-mode, for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-security-scan-runbook — DOCS: pre-handover staging security-scan runbook (closes SEC-9, the one launch-gate item that needs a live URL)
- **Files:** `docs/ops/2026-07-08-staging-security-scan-runbook.md` (**new**), this WORKLOG. Docs-only, no code.
- **What:** the security-hardening audit (`docs/specs/2026-07-07-security-hardening-audit.md`) is verified in code + tests for every item **except SEC-9 (external scan)**, which can only run against a live deployed URL. This runbook is that pass: a copy-paste, ~30-min, run-once-per-deploy checklist against a **staging** instance before client handover. Three layers cheapest-first: (§2) `npm audit --omit=dev` on the deployed lockfile; (§3) Dockerized **OWASP ZAP baseline** against the SPA + `/api` root, with our expected-GREEN headers (CSP/HSTS/no-store/no-stack-leak) and the known-N/A noise called out (SameSite/CSRF — we use bearer tokens, no cookies, so no CSRF surface); (§4) the **manual auth + isolation probe checklist** — the core, because ZAP can't understand our branch/role semantics. Every probe has an expected status: unauth→401, bad-login→401→429, **cross-branch read (clerk A vs branch B, three ways: list / receipt-by-id / statement?branch=) → zero B data** (the non-negotiable launch-blocker), clerk→admin-write→403, malformed→400-not-500. (§5) watch the **SEC-6 security log** (PR #143) fire live during §4 and confirm no PII in the lines; (§6) a hard GO/NO-GO gate; (§7) a fill-in sign-off record to keep beside the deploy record per client/deploy.
- **What's next:** Owner names the merge (docs-only, no code, independent of #143). Actual execution is an **ops action** — run it once the first Premala staging deploy exists over HTTPS (needs two clerk logins in two branches to exercise §4.4). Optional tiny follow-up once #143 merges: add a "see this runbook" pointer to the audit's SEC-9 row (deliberately not touched here to avoid a conflict with #143, which edits that same file).
- **Assumptions:** endpoint paths + statuses are grounded in the actual routes on `main` (`/api/login`, `/api/expenses`, `/receipt/by-id/:id`, `/api/statement`, `/health`, `/ready`) and the frozen isolation contract (`scopeForUser`/`scopeEq`, session-forced branch), not invented; SEC-6 log-line shape assumes PR #143 is on the deployed build.
## 2026-07-08 · Claude-J (Opus, fable-mode, for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-sec6-securitylog — SEC-6: central security-event logging + re-verified the security audit
- **Files:** `lib/security-events.js` (**new** — middleware), `lib/log.js` (`logSecurity` added — my file), `server.js` (**shared touch** — mount `securityEvents` early, announced per SOP §4.6), `tests/security-events.test.js` (**new**, +5), `docs/specs/2026-07-07-security-hardening-audit.md` (verification-pass banner + SEC backlog statuses). No other lanes touched.
- **What — closes SEC-6 (ADR-025) + a full re-audit of the launch gate against live code (Owner request, driven by the *Vibe Coder Security Checklist* PDF), TDD (RED→GREEN).** (1) **SEC-6:** ONE central middleware (`lib/security-events.js`) hooks `res 'finish'` and emits a structured `logSecurity` line for the security-relevant final statuses — **401→auth_fail, 403→scope_deny, 429→rate_limit** — so no route needs its own call and none can be forgotten. **ADR-002 safe:** method/path/status/ip only (path excludes the query string; nested-object fields dropped by `line()`); source IP included deliberately (a security log's whole purpose). Mounted after headers/CORS, before routes. (2) **Re-audited every SEC item vs merged `main`** (post #131/#132/#134/#137/#138): **8 items the 07-07 doc marked ⚠️ were already ✅** (password policy, file-upload magic bytes, localStorage, M2 validation, rate limiters, RLS 13/13) — the audit was stale/under-reporting. **All code-layer launch-blockers now closed;** only OPERATIONAL gates remain (HTTPS-in-prod/deploy, Groq ZDR, secret rotation).
- **Tests: full suite 1533/1533** (`.env` aside, restored; +5; zero regressions). `node -c server.js` + `lib/security-events.js` clean. RED confirmed first (module-not-found), then GREEN.
- **What's next:** Owner names the merge. Security posture is now **verified**, not asserted; the audit doc reflects it. Remaining launch gates are the ops steps already tracked (deploy → ZDR → secret rotation → uptime/log-drain).
- **Assumptions:** logging the source IP in the *security* channel is the correct call under ADR-002 (it's the identifying datum a security investigation needs, and the rate limiter already keys on it) — distinct from the general no-PII-in-logs rule for app logs; admin privileged-action auditing (float edits, mark-paid) is deferred as a nice-to-have (those are 200s, not caught by the status-based middleware) — SEC-6's blocking scope was failed-auth/deny/limit, which this covers.

Wired into `server.js`'s global error handler (`logError('unhandled', err, {status, method, path})`) — message + scalar route context only, never bodies. (2) **#13 `GET /ready`** — a **deep** readiness probe (vs `/health` liveness): confirms the Supabase data plane is reachable via a **PII-free** `voucher_counters` limit-1 select, returns `{ok, deps:{db}}` (200 up / 503 down) with **no data rows echoed**. Rate-limited (`READY_RATE_MAX`, default 60 — it touches the DB, SEC-7) and 3s-timeout-bounded (unref'd) so it can't hang. **Groq is deliberately NOT pinged** (polling would burn AI quota / risk 429; AI failures surface via #12 logs).
- **Tests: full suite 1501/1501** (`.env` moved aside, restored; +11 new; **zero regressions**). `node -c server.js` + `routes/misc.js` clean. RED confirmed first (module-not-found + `/ready` 404), then GREEN.
- **What's next:** Owner names the merge. The remaining #13 half is an **ops action, not code** — point an external uptime monitor (UptimeRobot → Telegram/email) at `/health` (liveness) and optionally `/ready` (depth); documented in the deploy runbook (#137). #12 Phase 2 (Sentry with PII-scrub) stays deferred behind an ADR.
- **Assumptions:** `voucher_counters` is a safe reachability target (core, PII-free, present on any provisioned instance); `/ready` unauthenticated like `/health` (standard for probes) but rate-limited + trivial-query so it's not an amplification vector; scattered per-route `console.error` calls are left as-is for now — Phase 1 wires the one central handler, not a repo-wide log migration (that would touch other lanes). Minor: this branch + PRs #136/#137 all prepend WORKLOG → trivial append-only conflicts on whichever merges after the others (keep all entries).

## 2026-07-08 · Claude (for Barat) · feature/barat-m2-company-scope — #133: make the company_id filter NULL-TOLERANT (fixes Jeevan's review block on #134)
- **Files:** `lib/supabase.js` (company_id moved OUT of the hard-eq `scopeEq` into a new `scopeOr(scope)` that emits a PostgREST `or=(company_id.eq.<x>,company_id.is.null)` group, appended in `sbSelect`/`sbUpdate`/`sbSoftDelete`; `stampScope` now stamps **account_id last too** — same override-protection branch_id/company_id already had, per Jeevan's minor note — my file). Tests: `tests/supabase.scope.test.js` (+4 net: rewrote the 4 company-filter asserts to the or() shape; added a faithful `applyFilters()` PostgREST-simulator + two "row actually returned" tests — a company-clerk STILL gets a null-company petty-cash float (the exact statement-breaking case) and still sees own-branch null-company expenses but never another company's rows; + an account_id-override-protection test).
- **What changed — folds Jeevan's #133 into PR #134, closing his review block.** The first cut filtered company with a hard `company_id=eq.<x>`. Jeevan traced the concrete break: `lib/pettyCash.js` inserts `petty_cash_floats`/`cash_in` with **no** company_id (null-by-design in v1), and `routes/statement.js` (via #132) passes a company-clerk's full scope to those reads → `+company_id=eq.co-1` against always-null rows returns zero → **a company-clerk's statement silently shows no opening float / no cash-in.** Same mechanism hid legacy null-company expense rows from a company-clerk's dashboard/reports/excel/review (#133 note 1). CI missed it (no test exercised statement × company-clerk). Fix: match company as `(company_id = theirs OR company_id IS NULL)` until a backfill stamps legacy rows, after which the null branch matches nothing and it degrades to plain equality. branch_id still hard-isolates, so no other company's *real* rows appear. Admin / single-company clerk (no company_id) → no company filter, unchanged. Writes, personal mode, branch-only business mode all byte-identical.
- **Tests: 1504/1504** (`.env` moved aside per the GOTCHA, restored after; net +4; zero regressions — includes #132's route-wiring suite now merged into this branch).
- **What's next:** re-request Jeevan's review (Tier-2 isolation — **do not self-merge**); `Closes #133` added to the PR body. **FUTURE-ROLE CAVEAT flagged:** for a company-scoped-only role (company_id, no branch_id — not shipped yet), the is.null tolerance would expose legacy null-company rows across companies until the backfill runs; acceptable transitionally per #133, revisit before that role ships. Remaining #107 item is Groq ZDR (pre-live ops on the Groq account, not code); likely next code pickup is #17 (`/api/templates` label sanitization).
- **Assumptions:** company_id values are slugs/uuids (no `,`/`)` that would break the or() group syntax); a backfill to stamp legacy `company_id` is a separate ops task, not code.

- **Files:** `lib/supabase.js` (`scopeEq` now applies `company_id` when the scope carries it, mirroring `branch_id`; insert stamping refactored into a shared `stampScope()` that stamps `company_id`+`branch_id` — my file), `lib/ocr.js` (**shared-file touch, announced per SOP §4.6**: `assembleExpense` + `expenseFromPhoto` accept & persist optional `company_id`, mirroring the existing `branch_id`/`staff_user_id` attribution I added in #107), `lib/bot.js` (binding's `company_id` threaded into every write-meta site — single photo, image-doc, album batch, PDF — via a new `bindingMeta()` helper; my file). Tests: `tests/supabase.scope.test.js` (+9: company filter on select/update/soft-delete, cross-company isolation both directions, insert auto-stamp/override, admin sees-all, branch-only back-compat), `tests/bot.business.staffscope.test.js` (+3: multi-company photo meta carries both ids, assembleExpense stamps/omits company_id).

(2) A bound business chat's captures (photo/album/PDF/image-doc) stamp the binding's `company_id` beside `branch_id`, so per-company reports/statements need no branch→company join. **Purely additive + optional:** no scope / no company_id → byte-identical to before (personal mode + single-company business both unchanged; the pre-existing 1456 tests stay green).
- **Tests: 1490/1490** (`.env` moved aside per the STATUS.md GOTCHA, restored after; +34 new; zero regressions). `node --check` clean on all 3 lib files.
- **What's next:** open the PR (Tier-2 — isolation/auth — needs Jeevan's cross-review; **do not self-merge**). Pairs with Jeevan's issue #131 (wiring `req.scope` into route call sites): that work can now pass a company-carrying scope and it will actually filter. **Table-awareness caveat still holds (#131):** only pass a scope to `sb*` calls on tables that carry `company_id`/`branch_id` (`expenses`, `submissions`, `petty_cash_floats`, `cash_in`) — never `branches`/`companies`/`users`/`field_templates`.
- **Assumptions:** `bot_bindings.company_id` (Phase 8, already in `schema.sql`) is populated by `lib/branches.js` at provisioning; a single-company client leaves it null → binding stays branch-only (bindingMeta omits the key). No migration needed — the `company_id` columns on `expenses`/`bot_bindings` landed with Phase 8's M2-P8 block.

## 2026-07-08 · Claude-J (Opus, fable-mode, for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-m2-scope-wiring — issue #131: thread req.scope into the DB helpers (defense-in-depth isolation)
- **Files:** `routes/expenses.js` (GET list, PATCH write, DELETE soft-delete, optimistic-concurrency pre-read), `routes/reports.js`, `routes/excel.js`, `routes/review.js` (queue read + decision write), `routes/receipt.js` (receipt-by-id lookup), `routes/statement.js` (petty_cash_floats + cash_in + expenses reads), `routes/submissions.js` (reads + writes), `tests/routes.scope-wiring.test.js` (**new**, +10). No `lib/*` touched (Barat's `lib/supabase.js` hook from #119 is the mechanism; this only passes `scope` to it).
- **What — the #119 follow-up (issue #131), TDD (RED→GREEN).** PR #119 added the `scope` param to `lib/supabase.js` but nothing passed it; this wires `scope: req.scope` into the branch-partitioned `sb*` calls so a **clerk's reads/writes are hard-filtered/stamped by `branch_id` at the DB layer** — defense-in-depth *behind* the existing in-memory `clerkVisible`/`canTouchExpense` guards (kept as the belt). Admin scope carries no `branch_id` → sees all branches; **personal mode (no `req.scope`) is a no-op → byte-for-byte unchanged.** Real fix, not just belt-and-suspenders, on `GET /api/excel` (a clerk's export had **no** branch filter before) and `GET /api/expenses`/`/api/reports`/`/api/review` (in-memory filtering was the only guard).
- **Scoping decision (documented, evidence-based):** `lib/pettyCash.js` left unchanged — `routes/pettyCash.js` writes are **admin-only** (scope is a no-op for admin, who legitimately sets any branch's float) and its reads are already **route-forced to the clerk's own `branchId`** (isolated-by-param, SEC-2). Threading scope through the lib layer there buys ~zero isolation for real signature churn. `canTouchExpense`/decision pre-reads left unscoped so their explicit 403 "not your branch" semantics don't silently become 404.
- **Tests: 1490/1490** (`.env` aside; +10; zero regressions). New test asserts at the DB-URL level: clerk request → `branch_id=eq.<their branch>`; admin → no filter; personal → no filter. All 7 edited routes syntax-checked clean.
- **What's next:** Owner merges (Barat may want to eyeball it since it consumes his #119 hook — no `lib/` change though). Closes #131. Optional tiny follow-up noted in the PR: thread scope through `lib/pettyCash` if you want the petty-cash reads DB-filtered too (currently param-isolated).
- **Assumptions:** `req.scope` is set only in business mode (`checkDash` → `scopeForUser`); every touched call is a no-op when it's absent, which the full personal-mode suite staying green confirms.

- **What — Phase 10 of the M2 epic (#106), ADR-023/024, executed from `docs/handoff/2026-07-07-phases-8-10/03-phase10-statement-export.md` + spec `docs/specs/2026-07-06-petty-cash-statement-format.md` with TDD (RED→GREEN watched every step).** The client-facing deliverable: one **Petty Cash Monthly Statement of Account** per branch × month, in **both** `.xlsx` and `.pdf` (the PDF appends receipts, one per page, in statement-line order). **Architecture (spec §3): one model, two renderers.** (1) **`lib/statement.js`** — pure, no-I/O `buildStatementModel`: Section A (Balance B/F + cash-in top-ups), Section B (expenses grouped by the company's category set in FieldSpec order, per-category subtotals + pad rows, unknown→Others, empty categories still render), footer balance (SIGN RULE §6), claims — **reuses Phase 9's `computeBalance`/`computeClaims`** so the numbers can't drift; the canonical **+75.09 surplus / zero-claim** case is re-asserted at the model level. (3) **`lib/statementPdf.js`** — pdfkit A4 portrait, draws the statement (fills/strokes per §2b, Section-B pagination repeats the column header), then appends receipts: image→1 page, PDF→one page per source page via **`lib/pdf.rasterizePdf`** (reused, not re-wrapped — keeps the `PDF_MAX_RENDER_PIXELS` bomb guard); missing/unreadable receipt skipped + counted (`{ buffer, pages, skippedReceipts }`), never fatal. (4) **`routes/statement.js`** — `GET /api/statement?branch=&period=&format=xlsx|pdf`; 404 unless `multiUser` AND `pettyCash`; admin any branch, clerk hard-forced to own branch (SEC-2); own limiter default 30 (exports are heavy, SEC-7); period/format validated; **filename from validated tokens only** (no header injection); correct content-types + `Content-Disposition`.

## 2026-07-07 · Claude-J (Opus, fable-mode, for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-m2-pettycash — M2 Phase 9: petty-cash floats + claim engine
- **Files:** `lib/account.js` (flag `pettyCash`, default OFF), `schema.sql` (**Migration M2-P9**: `petty_cash_floats` + `cash_in` tables + additive `expenses.claim_override` CHECK column, RLS enabled on both new tables — SEC-8), `lib/pettyCash.js` (**new** — pure maths + thin DB wrappers), `routes/pettyCash.js` (**new**), `lib/expenseValidation.js` (`claim_override` added to the PATCH allow-list with enum/null validation), `server.js` (**shared-file touch** — mount `pettyCash`, 2 lines; announced here per SOP §4.6), tests: `tests/pettyCash.test.js` (**new**, +13 pure-maths), `tests/pettyCash.route.test.js` (**new**, +12), `tests/account.flags.test.js` (+2), `tests/expenses.patchvalidation.test.js` (+1), `tests/expenses.scope.test.js` (+2).
- **What — Phase 9 of the M2 epic (#106), ADR-023, executed from `docs/handoff/2026-07-07-phases-8-10/02-phase9-pettycash-claims.md` with TDD (RED→GREEN, watched every test fail before implementing).** The heart of the Premala deliverable: branch petty-cash floats, running-balance maths, reimbursement claims — **additive + flag-gated** so `pettyCash` OFF ⇒ the product without petty-cash is byte-for-byte. (1) **Flag** `pettyCash` (default OFF, a per-client choice, not implied by the business default). (2) **Frozen maths (spec §6), pure + I/O-free in `lib/pettyCash.js`:** `computeBalance` → cashInHand = balance_bf + Σ cash_in, expenditure = Σ branch/period expenses (non-finite ⇒ 0 + reported `excluded`), balance = cashInHand − expenditure (**positive = surplus/no-claim, negative = overspent**); `computeClaims` → walk expenses in `expense_date` order (tie-break `created_at`, then `id` for full determinism), once the running total **strictly exceeds** cashInHand that expense (in full) and all after are claim; per-row `claim_override` (`'claim'`/`'petty_cash'`) beats the computed label without changing the running total. **Claim status is computed on read, never stored** — it can't drift. (3) **`routes/pettyCash.js`** mirrors the submissions house pattern: 404 unless `multiUser` AND `pettyCash`; GET summary (admin any branch, branch param required; clerk hard-forced to own branch, param ignored — SEC-2); float PUT + cash-in POST/DELETE are **admin-only** (the Owner's rule: only the admin sets floats, incl. Head Office); per-route limiter (SEC-7); period/amount/branch server-validated (SEC-5); no PII in logs. (4) **`claim_override`** rides the EXISTING `PATCH /api/expenses/:id` (no new route) — validated in `sanitizeExpensePatch`, role-guarded by the same `canTouchExpense` (clerk own-branch, admin anywhere).
- **THE canonical correctness test (the client's own sheet got the sign wrong):** **float 3000, expenditure 2924.91 ⇒ balance +75.09 (surplus), ZERO claims** — asserted exactly, at both the pure layer (`tests/pettyCash.test.js`) and through the route (`tests/pettyCash.route.test.js`). Amounts chosen to force IEEE-754 drift (10.10+20.20=30.299…) so the `round2` idiom is genuinely exercised.
- **Tests: 1357/1357** (`.env` moved aside per convention; +30 new; **zero regressions** — no existing assertion touched; the 1327 pre-existing tests run flag-free and stay green ⇒ product without petty-cash is byte-for-byte). `node -c server.js` clean.
- **⚠️ Migration to apply (Supabase SQL editor):** the **M2-P9** block in `schema.sql` (additive create-tables + nullable `claim_override` column; safe on live data). Still pending from prior phases: M2-P4, M2-P7, M2-P8.
- **What's next:** Owner merges this PR (do **not** self-merge), then Phase 10 (statement dual export, pdfkit ADR-024, `03-phase10-*.md`) off fresh `main`. Phase 10's statement model calls this phase's maths.
- **Assumptions:** the crossing expense is claimed in FULL (simplest defensible rule, spec §6, noted in code); `company_id` on `petty_cash_floats`/`cash_in` is left null in v1 (nullable/additive — the summary is branch-scoped and a branch lives inside one company; stamp it later if a company-level roll-up needs it); reimbursement *state* rides the existing `payment_status` (Phase 4), unchanged here.

- **The real gap found by live-verifying, not just trusting green tests:** `grid.js`/`payments.js` were never wired into `public/index.html` (no `<script>` tags) or `public/js/app.js` (not in the `dep()` resolver, `viewComponents` registry, or — the actual bug — **`Nav.buildNav()`'s call site never passed `sections`**, so `buildViews` would have mounted the Payments/Ledger panels but the **nav buttons themselves stayed the classic six**. No unit test caught this because none exercised the real `enterApp()` integration path (a coverage blind spot, now on the record). Fixed by computing the section list **once** in `enterApp` (`Nav.sectionsFor({business: !!deps.session})`) and passing the SAME list to both `Nav.buildNav(...)` and `buildViews(sections, mount, deps)` — nav buttons and mounted panels can no longer disagree.

Both gracefully show "Could not load…" against the fake (non-DB-backed) token — expected, not a bug. `.env` and the dev server were restored/stopped after.
- **What was already built (this slice, TDD, +23 tests since Phase 6 1/2):** `payments.js` — Pending/Paid tabs, month/branch/staff filters, admin bulk-select + sticky "N selected · total" bar wired to the Phase-4 bulk mark-paid endpoint, clerk read-only. `grid.js` — inline cell edit (`EDITABLE` allow-list mirrors `sanitizeExpensePatch`), per-field PATCH carrying `expected_updated_at`, a 409 re-fetches instead of clobbering. `routes/expenses.js` — clerk branch-isolation on `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/expenses` (in-memory `clerkVisible`/`canTouchExpense`, ahead of Barat's `lib/supabase.js` scope hook). `nav.js` — `sectionsFor({business})` inserts Payments/Ledger before Settings.
- **Tests: 1283/1283** (`.env` aside; zero regressions). The live browser check above was business-mode only (flag on); personal mode's classic `SECTIONS`/nav/views staying byte-for-byte unchanged rests on the pre-existing `tests/frontend/nav.sections.test.js` unit coverage, which stayed green throughout.
- **Phase 6 COMPLETE** (across #118 + this): login shell, role-aware header, payment tabs, excel-lite grid. **M2 epic (#106) backend + frontend for Phases 1–6 are now all merged/ready** — remaining: Phase 5 bot (Barat, #107), Phase 7 flags + Send-to-Admin, Phases 8–10 petty-cash/statement (Premala).

## 2026-07-07 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-m2-anomaly-payments — M2 Phase 4: staff-first anomaly valve + payments + bulk pay → Phase 4 COMPLETE
- **Files:** `schema.sql` (**Migration M2-P4**: `payment_status`/`paid_at`/`paid_note` + `updated_at` with a touch trigger), `lib/reviewDecision.js` (**`canRoleDecide`** pure gate + role-aware audit), `routes/review.js` (branch-scoped queue + staff-first/valve/override wiring, `ANOMALY_VALVE_DAYS`), `routes/expenses.js` (**`PATCH /api/expenses/payments`** bulk mark-paid + optimistic-concurrency 409 on the single-row PATCH), tests: `tests/reviewDecision.valve.test.js` (**new**, +11), `tests/review.route.scope.test.js` (**new**, +5), `tests/expenses.payments.test.js` (**new**, +7).
- **What — Phase 4 of the M2 epic (#106), TDD.** (1) **Staff-first anomaly:** clerk decides own-branch flagged rows first (audit gains `decided_by_role`); admin may **override** an existing decision any time (`overrode:true`) but may **originate** on a clerk's branch only after `ANOMALY_VALVE_DAYS` (default 7 — the safety valve); Head-Office/untagged rows are admin-first (spec carve-out); clerk queue is branch-filtered. (2) **Payments:** `payment_status` (pending|paid, default pending) + `paid_at`/`paid_note`; **admin-only write** via a dedicated bulk endpoint (clerk 403, flag OFF 404) — an id list PATCHed in `BULK_PAY_CHUNK` (100) chunks via PostgREST `id=in.(…)`, capped at `BULK_PAY_MAX_IDS` (1000), **reversible** (`paid:false` resets), own rate limiter (SEC-7). Registered BEFORE `/:id` so Express can't swallow the path. (3) **Optimistic concurrency:** opt-in `expected_updated_at` echo on the single-row PATCH → **409 + fresh `updated_at`** on mismatch (the excel-lite grid's autosave guard); a DB trigger bumps `updated_at` on every write path so no route has to remember to.
- **Tests: 1253/1253** (`.env` aside; +23 new; zero regressions — personal review/PATCH behaviour byte-for-byte unchanged, all guards additive/opt-in).
- **⚠️ Migration to apply (Supabase SQL editor):** the M2-P4 block in `schema.sql` (additive `alter table` + trigger — safe on live data; existing rows default to `payment_status='pending'`).
- **What's next (Jeevan):** Phase 6 frontend (my slice: login shell, role-aware shell, payment tabs, excel-lite grid — to Mugi's `concept-fresh.html`), Phase 7 flags + Send-to-Admin, Phases 8–10 petty-cash/statement (Premala). (Barat: #107 — scope hook B1, bot Phase 5, `assembleExpense` branch/staff persist.)
- **Assumptions:** valve compares row age from `created_at`; override is admin-only (a clerk never re-decides — 409); payment writes ONLY via the dedicated endpoint (the general PATCH allow-list never included payment fields); `expected_updated_at` compares as an exact string echo of what the client loaded.

## 2026-07-07 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-business-account-multibranch-spec — security audit + hardening gate (ADR-025)
- **Files:** `docs/specs/2026-07-07-security-hardening-audit.md` (**new**), `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-025**), `docs/plans/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch.md` (new **HARD CONSTRAINT #5 Security Gate** + frontend/Mugi framing fix), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner security directive** ("no hackers, no raw data can infiltrate/inject our DB, RLS all there; rate-limiting present but generous for the first client") + the *Vibe Coder Security Checklist* PDF + a file-storage video. Did an **evidence-based audit** (grep'd the codebase, not just asserted): **no raw SQL in app code** (PostgREST + encodeURIComponent), **no secrets in `public/`**, auth **token in sessionStorage**, **RLS enabled on all 5 tables with zero anon/public policies**. Mapped all 3 checklist sections (frontend/backend/habits) to our state with evidence.
- **Storage video = our ADR-018 already.** We **never** store file blobs in Postgres (columns are `receipt_file_id`/`receipt_url` strings); files go to a **private Supabase Storage bucket (object storage) + CDN**, DB holds the URL string. Exactly the video's 3 steps. Made ADR-018 a required business prerequisite + added magic-byte upload validation (SEC-3).
- **One real finding:** `overview.js` caches expenses to **`localStorage`** (financial data at rest in the browser) → SEC-4 (gate off under `multiUser`, clear on logout). Plus M2-specific gaps → a **SEC-1…SEC-10 hardening backlog** folded into the plan as a **Security Gate (HARD CONSTRAINT #5)**: RLS on every new table, server-side role gating + isolation tests, file-upload validation, M2 input validation, per-route rate limits (generous env defaults), security-event logging (no PII), CI `npm audit`, JWT-scoped RLS on the roadmap.
- **Defense-in-depth recorded (ADR-025):** (1) physical per-deployment silos (cross-company leak impossible), (2) the `scopeForUser` bouncer (within-company branch scoping), (3) RLS + service-role mediation, (4) validation/rate-limit/CSP at the edges. **Honest caveat kept:** service-role bypasses RLS → branch isolation is app-side until JWT-RLS.
- **Frontend framing corrected (Owner 2026-07-07):** Mugi's frontend did **NOT** move to us — Mugi retains frontend design authority (#3); **we implement the M2 dashboard to his `concept-fresh.html` design** and customise. Task-split table + Phase-6 wording updated to cite `concept-fresh.html` as the design source.
- **What's next:** planning is complete + security-gated; ready to build Phases 1–10 with each carrying its SEC-n slice. No paying-client go-live until the ⚠️/yes SEC items close (+ operationalisation gaps: hosting/HTTPS, monitoring, logs, CI dep-scan — 16-layer #7/#12/#13/#8).
- **Assumptions:** rate-limit "generous for first client" = raise env caps, not remove limiters. Docs-only push; the SEC items become code across the phase PRs.

## 2026-07-07 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-business-account-multibranch-spec — statement exporter design finalised (ADR-024)
- **Files:** `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-024** pdfkit), `docs/specs/2026-07-06-petty-cash-statement-format.md` (§3 rewritten — architecture + receipt-append rules), `docs/plans/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch.md` (Phase 10 detailed), this WORKLOG.
- **What — finished the Premala exporter design after rendering the sample.** Installed poppler (`pdftoppm`, earlier this session) and rendered the client PDF locally to *see* it (confidential; images inspected then deleted, never in repo). Two findings drove the design: (1) **§2b visual styling** (red serif title, blue italic headings, grey headers, category banner rows, yellow per-category totals, green subtotal, red negative balance, borders, fixed empty rows, Prepared/Checked/Approved sign-off) — captured last push; (2) **receipt-append rules:** one source doc → its own page(s) in statement-line order; a multi-page receipt (the flight booking) appends all its pages; **fit-inside-page, aspect preserved, top-aligned**; raw, no caption.
- **Architecture — one model, two renderers (spec §3):** `lib/statement.js` (StatementModel builder + maths, pure/testable) → `lib/statementXlsx.js` (exceljs) + `lib/statementPdf.js` (pdfkit); `routes/statement.js` (`format=xlsx|pdf`, scoped). Maths tested once; each format a thin renderer.
- **CTO dependency decision — ADR-024: add `pdfkit`** (pure-JS PDF writer, no native binary). Rationale: exceljs writes xlsx only, pdfium only *reads* PDFs — we need a writer; pdfkit draws the styled statement AND embeds receipt pages with least custom code; PDF-source receipts rasterised via the existing pdfium first. Chosen over pdf-lib (more manual) and hand-rolling (fragile); distinct from the rejected native `sharp` (ADR-014) because pure-JS. Owner brief: "the exact export of a PDF, just like the sample." Deps: `express, dotenv, exceljs, @hyzyla/pdfium` → **+pdfkit**.
- **What's next — planning is now COMPLETE for the whole M2 + Premala scope** (Phases 1–10 spec'd, task-split, ADRs 021–024). Ready to **build all at once**: (a) merge Barat's PR #109 first (after my review — it touches `lib/excel.js`/`lib/templates.js`, Tier-2 + cross-lane), then (b) execute Phases 1→10 (Phase 1 already up as PR #108). Pending Owner input to *run* it live: Premala's company list + per-company category sets + branches/staff.
- **Assumptions:** pdfkit approved (CTO call, Owner-deferred); statement PDF uses A4; receipt caption is a later optional enhancement (sample has none). Docs-only push.

## 2026-07-07 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-business-account-multibranch-spec — Premala petty-cash statement (ADR-023)
- **Files:** `docs/specs/2026-07-06-petty-cash-statement-format.md` (**new** — anonymised format spec), `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-023**), `docs/clients/premala.md` (petty-cash §4b + flags), `docs/plans/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch.md` (new **Phases 8–10**), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner supplied two real sample files** (a construction co. + a trading co.) showing Premala's actual deliverable: a **Petty Cash Monthly Statement of Account** (per company × branch × month) with the **receipt snapshots appended one per page**, exported today by hand in Excel (broken `#REF!` formulas + a sign error) and emailed over WhatsApp. **CONFIDENTIAL:** raw files kept OUT of the repo (real company/staff/bank data); everything captured is anonymised.
- **Decisions (ADR-023):** (1) **Company dimension** — Group→Company→Branch→Staff (Premala runs multiple companies; each owns its category set — samples had 9 vs 5 categories). (2) **Petty-cash float** admin-editable per branch/month (incl. Head-Office). (3) **Claim = maths, not a manual list:** `Balance = Cash-in-hand − Expenditure`; negative = claim owed to staff → reimburse via Phase-4 `payment_status`. (4) **Dual export** (Excel + PDF), both required, faithful to the sample; PDF = statement + appended receipt pages.
- **CTO catch (proves accurate scan):** their sample shows `BALANCE −75.09` but `3000 − 2924.91 = +75.09` — a **manual sign error** (same family as the `#REF!` cells). Decision: **correct accounting wins** — positive = surplus (no claim), negative = claim; we compute what their sheet gets wrong (a feature). Optional per-client "match their exact convention" toggle, OFF by default. Never reproduce `#REF!`.
- **Reuses existing spine:** receipt-image storage (ADR-018), currency FX (ADR-005, never the LLM), `payment_status` (Phase 4), `exceljs` (ADR-012), FieldSpec categories (#12). New flags `companyDimension`/`pettyCash`. PDF render approach = reuse-existing-deps-first (`@hyzyla/pdfium` already in tree; `sharp` stays rejected, ADR-014) — **no new npm dep committed**.
- **What's next:** Phases 8–10 added to the plan (company dimension → petty-cash/claim engine → statement dual export), all flag-gated + Tier-2 (money + schema). Build order still Phase 1 (PR #108) first; petty-cash builds on branches+expenses+payments. Pending Owner: Premala's company list + each company's category set + branches/staff; then provision the isolated instance (ADR-015). Faithful-reproduction visual check when the exporter is built.
- **Assumptions:** claim status is **computed** (running balance + `claim_override`), never a stored truth, so it can't drift from the numbers; the general product is untouched (all new flags default off/single-company). Docs-only push.

## 2026-07-06 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-business-account-multibranch-spec — Premala client profile (ADR-022)
- **Files:** `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-022**), `docs/clients/premala.md` (**new** — first client profile), `docs/plans/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch.md` (new **Phase 7** — per-client profiles + Send-to-Admin), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner's new update for Premala (our first client).** Premala's real workflow ≠ the default M2 "live merged master" model: each branch keeps its **own** formatted Excel (receipt snapshots embedded) exported **as PDF** in their existing layout, and hands it to the admin **over WhatsApp** today; the **admin's master file = admin's OWN HQ/petty-cash expenses**, NOT a live all-branch merge. But admin **does** want **live reports/analysis**. Captured as **ADR-022: per-client behaviour = capability-flag PROFILES** (extends ADR-010/020) — Premala is the first profile, NOT a fork.
- **The key CTO framing:** split into a **live-data layer** (rows-not-workbooks, ADR-021 — unchanged; branch receipts still flow into the shared DB so admin analytics stay live) and a **document layer** (flagged off for Premala). Premala flags: `multiUser=true, liveMasterMerge=false, excelLiteGrid=false, branchSubmission=true`.
- **New GENERAL feature — "Send to Admin" branch submission (`branchSubmission`, flag-gated, reusable):** clerk submits their branch's compiled export in-dashboard → lands in the admin dashboard. Replaces the WhatsApp hand-off. **Why it's an upgrade, not just a customisation:** kills a real PII/data-leak vector (receipts leaving the isolated managed-silo instance via a chat app — ADR-021 §11 posture), it's **cheap** (rows already in the DB → lightweight state-change, not a file transfer), and it yields a **free audit trail** (who/branch/period/when). Endorsed by Owner.
- **Parked (blocked on Owner):** Premala's exact PDF export layout (Excel-with-embedded-snapshots → PDF) — samples incoming; build deferred, foundation is Phase 3's receipt-image embed. Don't build blind.
- **What's next:** general MVP build **continues untouched** (Phase 1 identity is up as PR #108 off `feature/jeevan-m2-identity`; Phases 2–6 proceed as the default product). Premala is a flag profile on the same build. When samples land → build the client-specific PDF export. Provision Premala's isolated instance (own Supabase + bot token + Groq keys, ADR-015) before real data.
- **Assumptions:** new flags default to the **general** product behaviour (`liveMasterMerge`/`excelLiteGrid` default true, `branchSubmission` default false) so no existing/other-client behaviour shifts; Premala overrides them. Docs-only; no code this push.
