## 2026-07-06 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-business-account-multibranch-spec
- **Files:** `docs/specs/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch-design.md` (new — full house-format spec), `docs/plans/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch.md` (new — phased implementation plan + task split), `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-021**), this WORKLOG.
- **What — CTO design conversation for the M2 business-account (multi-branch / multi-staff) layer**, landed section-by-section with Owner sign-off from the planning PDF (`receipt analy business.pdf`) + the `concept-fresh.html` admin/team mockup. This is the long-deferred per-client multi-user layer (MUGI.md §5), now specced. **Additive, flag-gated, per-client** (ADR-010/020) — no change to the base product, all existing features intact.
- **The load-bearing decision — rows, not workbooks:** the Owner's flagged "Excel tangling" risk is designed away structurally, not with reconciliation code. Exactly ONE data shape per client (the FieldSpec, #12), **HQ-owned**; staff contribute rows (`branch_id`/`staff_user_id`), never columns. The "live master template" is a `GROUP BY` query over the single `expenses` table, not a stored/synced file; a "branch workbook" is just `buildWorkbook()` on filtered rows. Staff Excel imports are mapped into the canonical FieldSpec; bulk "import data back" is blocked. Shape-mismatch is impossible by construction.
- **Signed-off decisions (all captured in ADR-021 + the spec):** one `account_id` = one client business, branches/staff as internal dimensions (not per-branch accounts); credentialed roles = **HQ Admin + Branch Clerk only**, staff = attribution names (no login, no bot — Owner's cost call); auth = **lighter server-guard** (`crypto.scrypt`, no new dep; opaque sessions reusing `checkDash`; one central `scopeForUser` bouncer; **RLS deferred to later hardening** — CTO call for ship-soon, with the honest app-side-isolation caveat recorded); bot = clerk tool with `/start <one-time-token>` role-lock binding (the honest form of "one-click Telegram login" — one tap, not zero, which Telegram forbids); anomaly = **staff-first + admin safety valve after N days**; payments = per-receipt `payment_status` (admin edits / clerk views) with **chunked bulk mark-as-paid** (researched vs Xero/Bill.com/QuickBooks — QBO lacks bulk-pay, so it's a quality edge); excel-lite v1 = inline cell-edit + autosave, values only (no column changes).
- **Reuses (do NOT rebuild):** `lib/reports` (add branch/staff grouping dims), `lib/excel` `buildWorkbook`, `lib/anomaly`+`routes/review` (staff-first status machine + audit trail), `lib/categorize` (categories from FieldSpec), `lib/bot` (today's-bills/weekly/confirm-delete/media-group), `checkDash` bearer plumbing, `account_settings`/`getCapabilities`. New: `lib/auth.js`, `lib/branches.js`, `routes/{auth,branches}.js`, schema (`branches`/`users`/`sessions`/`bot_bindings` + expense columns).
- **Tier-2** (auth + DB schema + cross-branch isolation) → mandatory independent cross-review before merge. Phased build order in the spec §8 (identity → provisioning → data dims → anomaly/payments → bot → frontend), each phase TDD.
- **Hosting/privacy decisions added (this session, spec §10–11 + ADR-021):** **managed silos** — we host/operate one instance + Supabase project + bot token per client (physical cross-company isolation; strongest, keeps current arch); `account_id` is the free tenant seam if we ever pool later (deliberate migration, since service-role bypasses RLS today). **Data-privacy posture:** we're the processor → answer on technical controls + DPA + ≤72h breach clause + sub-processor disclosure; **OCR provider is a per-deployment config knob** (default Groq — verified 2026-07-06 it doesn't train on data, not retained by default, ZDR toggle available, DPA covers PDPL; **action: enable ZDR**); regulated clients escalate to a DPA-backed provider, self-hosted OCR only if air-gapped. **Domain:** not technically required (free host subdomains + bot webhook suffice); at most one ~$10/yr company domain serves all clients via subdomains, or client brings their own — never one paid domain per client. **Personal vs business = one flag (`multiUser`), not a fork (ADR-010):** flag off = today's product byte-for-byte, existing tests stay green.
- **Amendment (Owner, same session) — receipt image is a first-class audit output:** durable receipt-image storage (**ADR-018**, private `receipts` bucket, both ingestion paths) is a **required prerequisite** for business (permanent + dashboard-viewable); **the Excel export EMBEDS the receipt image per row** (exceljs `addImage`) + hyperlink to full-res, so the `.xlsx` is self-contained for offline audit. Default embed thumbnail; **links-only** fallback for large exports (no resize dep, ADR-014). **NOT business-gated — the Receipt embed applies to the PERSONAL account too** (Owner follow-up); only Branch/Staff columns are business-only; the embed may ship on the personal product independently of M2. Reflected in spec §1/§3.5, plan Phase 3, ADR-021.
- **Amendment (Owner, same session):** the **HQ admin is also a capturer** (HQ audits its own petty cash) → admin gets the dashboard upload + a Telegram bot binding, modelled as a **"Head Office" branch** auto-created on setup. This **amends "clerks-only bot" → admin + clerk are bot-bound; staff still none.** Anomaly carve-out: admin is first decider on Head-Office items (no clerk beneath). Reflected in spec §1/§3.6/§3.7, plan Phases 2 & 5, ADR-021.
- **Task split (Owner-directed, overrides default map for this epic):** **Jeevan** = Supabase schema + identity/auth + provisioning + reports/payments + all frontend; **Barat** = `lib/supabase.js` scope helper + business Telegram bot + Groq ZDR/OCR-provider knob + FieldSpec plumbing; **Mugi off** unless a frontend edit is handed over. Frozen contract `scopeForUser(user)→{account_id,branch_id?,role}` bridges Jeevan's auth and Barat's supabase layer. Phased build (6 phases) in the plan, each its own branch + PR + Tier-2 review.
- **What's next:** commit these docs + open the Tier-2 PR for cross-review (Barat sign-off on the `lib/supabase.js`/schema touchpoints). Then Phase 1 (identity foundation) on `feature/jeevan-m2-identity`. Pre-sale/ops to own before a paying client: company-owned paid Supabase org (ADR-015), enable Groq ZDR (ADR-016), legal entity + DPA + breach runbook, durable Storage for business receipt images.
- **Assumptions:** design-only, no code yet (Owner directed "don't write code yet"); repo/dir name left as-is (ADR-020 cosmetic). Docs-only branch — not yet committed/pushed; awaiting Owner's go to commit + open PR.

## 2026-07-06 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-m2-identity — M2 Phase 1: identity foundation
- **Files:** `schema.sql` (new **Migration M2-P1** block: `branches`, `users`, `sessions`, `bot_bindings` + additive `expenses.branch_id`/`staff_user_id`), `lib/auth.js` (**new**), `routes/auth.js` (**new**), `routes/expenses.js` (`checkDash` → session-aware in business mode), `server.js` (**shared-file touch** — mount `routes/auth.js`, two lines; announced here per SOP §4.6), tests: `tests/auth.test.js` (+12), `tests/checkdash.session.test.js` (+4), `tests/auth.route.test.js` (+8).
- **What — first build phase of the business-account epic (#106, ADR-021, spec `docs/specs/2026-07-06-business-account-multibranch-design.md`).** Adds the credentialed-identity spine, **flag-gated by `capabilities.multiUser`**: with the flag OFF the personal product is byte-for-byte unchanged (static `DASHBOARD_TOKEN`, no login, personal expense rows leave the new columns null). With it ON, `checkDash` resolves an opaque **session** bearer → `{account_id, branch_id, role}` and attaches `req.scope` via the frozen **`scopeForUser`** contract (admin → no branch filter; clerk → hard branch filter). `lib/auth.js`: scrypt hash/verify (Node core, no new dep; constant-time), session mint/lookup/revoke (stores only the token's sha-256), `scopeForUser`. `routes/auth.js`: `POST /api/login` (uniform-timing verify, staff can never log in) + `POST /api/logout` (revoke); both 404 when the flag is off.
- **Coordination — the `scopeForUser` object is produced here; ENFORCEMENT in `lib/supabase.js` is Barat's half (issue #107).** This PR does NOT touch `lib/supabase.js` (his file). So the cross-branch **isolation-at-query-level** test (clerk A can't read branch B's rows through `sbSelect`) lands with Barat's scope hook — the identity + scope object are ready for it. RLS on the new tables is service-role-mediated for v1; JWT-scoped policies are commented, not enabled (later hardening, ADR-021).
- **Tests: 1128/1128** (`.env` moved aside per the standing convention; +24 new, personal baseline unchanged — zero regressions).
- **What's next:** Barat wires the `lib/supabase.js` scope param + the isolation test (#107); then **Phase 2** (provisioning — `lib/branches.js`, auto-gen clerk creds + one-time bot-binding token, Head Office branch, soft-revoke). Tier-2 (auth + schema) → needs independent cross-review before merge; do NOT self-merge.
- **Assumptions:** managed-silo (one `account_id` per instance) so session/`sbSelect` calls stay scoped to `cfg.ACCOUNT_ID`; logout revokes ALL of a user's sessions (safe default for v1); session TTL 12h (`SESSION_TTL_MS`).

## 2026-07-05 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-positioning-delivered-system
- **Files:** `docs/DECISIONS.md` (new **ADR-020**), `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/PROJECT_BRIEF.md` + `MEMORY_LEDGER.md` (positioning banner), `docs/FALC_AGENTIC_ROADMAP.md` (new — CTO brainstorm), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner re-decided the business model:** this is **no longer a SaaS**. It's a **delivered, per-client automation SYSTEM** — we build it once and deploy/customise an instance per client (their template/categories/branding/features). **No** self-serve signup, subscription/billing, app-store listing, or Windows/Mac installer package. **ALL features stay — nothing removed;** the change is packaging/distribution only. Captured as ADR-020 + banners at the top of the three governance docs the startup ritual forces every Claude to read (so no one drifts back to the SaaS framing). "SaaS-only phases" (public onboarding, billing, store/installer, marketplace multi-tenancy) flagged N/A; M2 multi-tenant/roles reframed as a **per-client option**, not a universal requirement.
- **16-layer completeness audit — DONE (`docs/SYSTEM_COMPLETENESS.md`).** The Owner clarified the "16-layer image" = the *"Vibe Coding in a Real Tech SaaS"* stack (system design → architecture → frontend → APIs → DB/storage → auth → hosting → CI/CD → security → rate limiting → caching/CDN → error tracking/logs → monitoring/alerts → testing → scaling → …). Audited each layer against `main`: **12/16 solid** (design, architecture, frontend, APIs, DB+storage, auth, security, rate-limiting, testing; caching + scaling adequate-or-N/A). **4 real gaps to be a proper working system, in priority order: hosting/deploy (#7), monitoring & alerts (#13), error-tracking/log-aggregation (#12), CI automation (#8)** — i.e. the *operationalisation* phase (turns "feature-complete on a laptop" into "a client instance that stays up and is debuggable"). CDN + multi-tenant-scale flagged N/A for the delivered-per-client model; multi-user roles = per-client option. **No SaaS-distribution layer (signup/billing/store/installer) is needed.** Added to this PR/branch.
- **Also — Falc agentic roadmap (CTO brainstorm):** `docs/FALC_AGENTIC_ROADMAP.md` lays out ~16 candidate capabilities (bulk edits, full template mgmt, standing category/auto-approve rules, proactive briefings, persistent audit log + undo, in-chat receipt ingestion, export trigger…) grouped by value, sequenced, all inheriting the ADR-019 propose→confirm safety spine. Candidate list to prioritise, not committed scope.
- **What's next:** Owner shares the 16-layer image → do the completeness audit; Owner picks the Falc roadmap items to build next (recommend: bulk edits + standing rules + a persistent audit log first). Docs-only PR — self-authored, needs Owner naming to merge.
- **Assumptions:** repo/dir name (`receipt-analyser-saas`) left as-is (cosmetic; a rename churns history for no functional gain) — the word "SaaS" now means "the system" in prose. No code change (the system already runs single-account, config-driven, template-customisable = the delivered-system shape).

## 2026-07-05 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · feat/jeevan-agentic-write-actions — real gap found + fixed, all 4 action types live-verified
- **Files:** `lib/agent.js` (`listExpenses` now projects `id`; the 4 `propose*` tool descriptions now explicitly instruct the model to get the id from a prior `listExpenses` call, never ask the user to type one), `tests/agent.test.js` (+1 regression test), this WORKLOG.
- **The Owner was right that Falc wasn't fully done.** My own first click-through (previous session) only "worked" because I pasted a raw UUID by hand. Re-reading the code exposed why: `listExpenses`'s returned items had **no `id` field** — the only read tool that gives the model a way to identify *which* expense the user means. Without it, `proposeEditExpense`/`proposeDeleteExpense`/`proposeReviewDecision` are **unreachable through normal conversation**, because no real user knows or types a UUID. This is a genuine functional gap, not a nitpick — confirmed by re-running the *exact* prior failing prompt ("Change the category of my Taco Bell expense to Travel") both before and after the fix.
- **Fix:** `listExpenses` items now include `id` (the row's real id, passed through verbatim — same untrusted-safe projection as before, `ai_raw` still excluded). All 4 `propose*` tool descriptions updated to say the id must come from a prior `listExpenses` call.
- **Live end-to-end, all 4 action types, one sitting, real Supabase + Groq (auth toggled off then restored):**
  1. **Edit** — the exact one-shot prompt that failed before now completes fully unaided: `listExpenses` → real id found → `proposeEditExpense` → confirm card (`Meals & Entertainment → Travel`) → Confirm → **DB category actually changed**.
  2. **Review-decision** — "Approve the Baker's Cottage expense that's pending review" (temporarily flagged a real row to `needs_review` for the test) → confirm card (`needs_review → approved`) → Confirm → **DB status changed**.
  3. **Add-template-field** — "Add a template field called Payment Method" → confirm card with the honesty caveat visible → Cancel → template genuinely unchanged (verified via `/api/templates`).
  4. **Delete, with Agent auto-mode ON** (re-confirmed) — still always shows the card; Cancel leaves the row untouched.
  5. **Input-lock UX:** while a confirm card is pending, the message input + Send button are both disabled (can't send ambiguously mid-confirmation); Cancel and **"New conversation"** both re-enable input and correctly abandon the proposal with no write.
  6. **Zero console errors** across the entire extended session.
- **Test data restored** to its pre-test state (Taco Bell category, Baker's Cottage status) after verification.
- **Tests: 1059/1059** (+1: `listExpenses` items include the row id).
- **What's next:** ready for Owner's merge decision — all 4 open PRs (#99 UI density/reports drill-down, #100 spec, #101 plan, #102 implementation + this fix) can go in together per Owner's direction ("merge all of it altogether") once named.
- **Assumptions:** none new beyond the prior entry.

## 2026-07-05 · Claude-J2 (for Jeevan) · feat/jeevan-agentic-write-actions
- **Files (10 commits):** `lib/expenseValidation.js` (new, extracted), `lib/reviewDecision.js` (new, extracted), `lib/agentProposals.js` (new — pending-proposal store + auto-confirm gate), `lib/agent.js` (4 `propose*` write tools + `confirmProposal` + forced-stop/auto-mode dispatch + hardened `SYSTEM_PROMPT`), `routes/agent.js` (`autoMode` threaded, new `POST /api/agent/confirm`), `routes/expenses.js`/`routes/review.js` (re-import the extracted validators, behavior byte-identical), `public/js/components/agent.js` (Falc confirm card), `public/js/components/settings.js` (real "Agent auto-mode" toggle), `public/js/app.js` (sessionStorage persistence + wiring), 10 test files.
- **What — builds ADR-019 (spec PR #100), QA report item #8:** "the agent should be agentic, able to do everything in the dashboard, with confirmation like Claude, or an auto-mode." Implements **propose → confirm → execute**: a new `propose*` tool validates only (never writes) and returns a pending diff; the model is forced to stop (`tool_choice:'none'`, no chaining a second action) until a human confirms; Confirm re-validates the row FRESH and executes via the SAME already-validated code the dashboard's own PATCH/DELETE/review/template routes already use — zero parallel write logic.
- **Scope — all 4 action types** (Owner's explicit call): edit expense fields, delete (soft), approve/reject review, add a template field (carries the existing "type/required may not round-trip through CSV" honesty caveat).
- **The key safety property, verified in code, not just claimed:** `canAutoConfirm(kind)` in `lib/agentProposals.js` is a hardcoded `Set(['edit','review-decision'])` — delete and add-template-field structurally **cannot** auto-confirm regardless of the toggle; that gate is consulted from `runAgent`'s loop, never decided by the model. A pending (non-auto) proposal forces the model's next call to `tool_choice:'none'` before returning `{answer, proposal}` to the caller.
- **Injection defense:** `SYSTEM_PROMPT` hardened (same "caged/untrusted DATA" framing already used in `lib/categorize.js`) — retrieved OCR/merchant text is reference-only, never an instruction; a write proposal may only follow the user's own current message. Stated honestly as the one soft/prompt-level layer; the confirm card + confirm-time re-validation + the server-side auto-mode gate are the real, model-independent backstops.
- **Every task independently re-verified by the controller** (not just trusting subagent self-reports) — read the actual diffs for the write-boundary code, the auto-confirm gate, the force-stop loop, the HTTP coercion, and the frontend confirm-card/toggle wiring; re-ran `npm test` myself after each commit.
- **Zero cross-lane edits:** the two extractions (`sanitizeExpensePatch`, review status-guard) are Jeevan's own files (`routes/expenses.js`, `routes/review.js`); the template-add action reuses `lib/templates.js` (Barat's) read-only via its existing exported functions — his file untouched.
- **Tests: 1058/1058** (+220 across 10 test files; baseline was 963 before this branch). Full suite run fresh after every commit, `.env` moved aside per the standing convention.
- **What's next:** open PR for review before merge (Tier-2 — a brand-new pathway that writes financial data). Not yet live-verified in a browser (built across sequential worktree tasks, no dev server driven this pass) — recommend a live click-through (ask the agent to edit a category, confirm it, then try a delete to confirm the always-confirm gate) before merge.
- **Assumptions:** in-memory (not DB-backed) pending-proposal store, ~10min TTL, single-account v1 — a server restart before a Confirm click loses that one pending proposal (acceptable, interactive same-session flow). `autoMode` persisted to sessionStorage only (matches the existing token/theme pattern — never localStorage).

## 2026-07-05 · Claude-J2 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-agentic-write-actions-spec
- **Files:** `docs/specs/2026-07-05-agentic-write-actions-design.md` (new), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner-requested spec (QA report item #8):** "the agent should be agentic, able to do everything in the dashboard, with confirmation like Claude, or an auto-mode." Brainstormed live with the Owner (5 sections, each approved before moving on). Design: a **propose → confirm → execute** pattern — a new `propose*` tool validates only and returns a pending diff; the model must frame it in plain English then stop (forced `tool_choice:'none'`, no chained actions); the chat shows a confirm card (Confirm/Cancel); Confirm re-validates fresh and executes via the SAME already-validated code the dashboard's own PATCH/DELETE/review/template routes already use — no parallel write logic anywhere.
- **Scope — all 4 action types in v1** (Owner's explicit call, safe because nothing executes without confirmation): edit expense fields, delete (soft), approve/reject review, add a template field. Add-template-field and delete are flagged **always-confirm even with auto-mode on** (bigger/less-reversible blast radius); auto-mode (a real new Settings toggle) can only skip the card for edit/review-decision, and that gate lives server-side, never in the model's judgment.
- **Security, layered and honestly graded:** system-prompt hardening (retrieved OCR/merchant text is reference-only, never an instruction — same framing `lib/categorize.js` already uses) is explicitly called out as the ONE soft/prompt-level layer; the confirm card itself (shows a literal diff) + confirm-time re-validation + the server-side (not model-side) auto-mode gate are the real, non-model-dependent backstops.
- **Small in-lane refactor required, no cross-lane edits:** `sanitizeExpensePatch` moves out of `routes/expenses.js` into a new `lib/expenseValidation.js`; the review status-machine check moves out of `routes/review.js` similarly — both files are Jeevan's own. Action #4 (template field) reuses `lib/templates.js` (Barat's) read-only, calling its existing exported save function — his file is not touched.
- **What's next:** Owner reviews the committed spec → writing-plans for the implementation plan → build. Not yet built — spec only.
- **Assumptions:** in-memory (not DB-backed) pending-proposal store, ~10min TTL, single-account v1 — consistent with the agent's existing "no long-lived server-side conversation state" design; acceptable that a server restart before a Confirm click loses that one pending proposal (interactive same-session flow, unlike the bot's durable async confirm pattern).

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · fix/jeevan-reports-year-scoping
- **Files:** `public/js/components/reports.js` (pure helpers `yearRange`/`yearsFromReport`/`pickDefaultYear` + `buildReportsQuery` gains a `year` arg; render layer gains a Year `<select>`), `public/css/app.css` (`.rp-year-group`/`.rp-year-select`), `tests/frontend/reports.test.js` (+13 TDD), this WORKLOG.
- **Bug (Owner-reported):** the Reports chart's **Month** view showed bucket labels spanning EVERY year mixed together (`2018-01, 2023-12, 2024-04, 2026-05, …`) instead of one year's Jan–Dec; same for **Week**. **Root cause:** `lib/reports.js buildReport()` correctly buckets by `YYYY-MM`, but the client sent NO date range, so it aggregated across all years at once. **`lib/reports.js` is pure + correct — left untouched.**
- **Fix (client-side):** added a **Year selector** to the Reports toolbar, populated from the account's real years (a one-time `period=year` discovery fetch → `yearsFromReport`), defaulting to the current year (or the latest present — `pickDefaultYear`). `buildReportsQuery(period, from, to, year)` now auto-scopes **day/week/month** to that year's `Jan 1 – Dec 31` when no explicit from/to is set; the **year** period stays unscoped (it's the cross-year comparison view); an explicit from/to range still overrides. The selector is hidden for the `year` period.
- **Live-verified** (real Supabase, data spans 2018/2023/2024/2026): reproduced the bug (unscoped month = `2018-01…2026-07` mixed), then confirmed the fix — Month view scoped to 2026 shows only `2026-03…2026-07`; switching the Year dropdown to 2024 shows only `2024-04`. Screenshot in the PR.
- **Tests: 916/916** (+13 TDD, all pure: `buildReportsQuery` year auto-scope for day/week/month, year-period-not-scoped, explicit/partial-range override, invalid-year ignored; `yearRange`; `yearsFromReport`; `pickDefaultYear`). Existing `buildReportsQuery` tests unchanged (the `year` arg is optional/backward-compatible).
- **What's next / flagged:** the **Overview** page's chart has the SAME year-mixing (it also queries with no range) — a separate component (`public/js/components/overview.js`), not fixed here to keep this PR tight; good candidate to fold into the #9 Reports/Overview work. `day` period scoped to a full year can be many bars (365) — acceptable for v1; the from/to inputs remain as a finer override.
- **Assumptions:** fix stays client-side (`buildReport` is a correct pure aggregator — the bug was the caller sending no window); year discovery via one `period=year` fetch; default = current year, falling back to latest-present.

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · feat/jeevan-receipt-image-storage-wiring (ADR-018 / spec #81)
- **Files:** `routes/upload.js` (upload each image to Storage, carry `receipt_url` per-image via `metas[]`), `routes/receipt.js` (new `/receipt/by-id/:id` serve route), `public/js/components/activity.js` (frontend — `receiptByIdPath`/`hasReceiptImage`, render via by-id), `lib/security-headers.js` (**CSP `img-src` fix — see below**), tests (`receipt.route`/`upload.route`/`frontend/activity`/`security-headers`), this WORKLOG. **NOTE:** the `assembleExpense` `receipt_url` echo is **Barat's (#90, merged)** — this PR consumes that seam; my duplicate `lib/ocr.js` edit + `ocr.receipturl.test.js` were dropped on the rebase in favour of his canonical version.
- **What — wires the receipt-image storage foundation (#87 `lib/storage.js`) end-to-end** so dashboard-uploaded receipts finally display (they stored NO image before). Flow: `routes/upload.js` uploads each decoded image (a PDF is already a rasterized page image here) to the private `receipts` bucket via `uploadReceiptImage`, and passes its object path as `receipt_url` per-image through `runBatch`'s `metas[]` (#84) → Barat's `assembleExpense` echo → stored in the existing `expenses.receipt_url`. Serve: new `GET /receipt/by-id/:id` (checkDash) looks the row up **server-side** (browser never sees the raw storage path), streams the durable Storage copy, and **falls back to the Telegram proxy** for old rows (`receipt_file_id`). Frontend renders the image whenever either field is present, fetching by expense id.
- **🐞 Found + fixed a REAL pre-existing bug via live testing — CSP blocked EVERY receipt image.** `lib/security-headers.js` had `img-src 'self' data:` — **no `blob:`**. The dashboard renders receipt images by auth'd-fetching the bytes and setting a browser `blob:` object URL on an `<img>` (the /receipt route is checkDash-gated, so a plain `<img src>` can't carry the bearer). CSP silently blocked the blob URL → naturalWidth 0 → no image, on BOTH the Storage and Telegram paths. It went unnoticed because `BOT_TOKEN` is empty, so the Telegram path always 404'd — no image ever loaded to reveal it. Fixed: `img-src 'self' data: blob:` (blob: URLs can only reference page-created data — not an injection vector). This was the actual reason "receipt images don't display."
- **Live end-to-end verified** (real Supabase Storage + Groq, auth toggled off then restored): dashboard-uploaded a receipt → row got `receipt_url` (`default/<uuid>.jpg`, `receipt_file_id` null) → `/receipt/by-id/:id` streamed it back (200, image/jpeg, `private, no-store`) → **drawer `<img>` decoded 460×520, no CSP violation.** Also proved `lib/storage` round-trip is byte-perfect in pure Node (75→75 identical), isolating the CSP as the real blocker.
- **Tests:** full suite green (my adds: by-id route Storage-serve/Telegram-fallback/neither-404/unknown-404/checkDash; upload uploads+per-image receipt_url + fail-safe; frontend `receiptByIdPath`/`hasReceiptImage`; CSP img-src blob:).
- **What's next:** Telegram-side durable copy (Barat, `lib/bot.js`/`expenseFromPhoto` — pass the already-downloaded photo bytes to `assembleExpense` as an upload too, so bot receipts also stop relying on the live proxy) — his lane, spec §3. Backfill of old rows is v1-optional (spec decision B: leave on Telegram proxy).
- **Assumptions:** serve-by-id (not by raw path) so the storage path never reaches the browser (spec decision A(i)); PDFs store the rasterized page image (decision C). Left `.claude/launch.json` `autoPort:true` locally (3001 was held by another session) — not part of this PR.
- **Housekeeping:** one synthetic test row (`KEDAI KOPI AH SENG`, MYR 5.70) + a couple of orphan Storage objects remain from verification — Owner can delete anytime.

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-menu-buttons (3rd commit, same branch)
- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (`assembleExpense` accepts an optional `receipt_url`), `lib/supabase.js` (new `sbRpc`), `lib/categoryMemory.js` (`recordCorrection` now atomic), `db/2026-07-04-category-corrections-upsert.sql` (new), `schema.sql` (folded in), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+2), `tests/categoryMemory.test.js` (rewritten for the new call shape), `tests/supabase.insertreturning.test.js` (+2).
- **What changed — 2 of Jeevan's 3 requests (the currency restart was already done, see below):**
  1. **ADR-018 prep (receipt-image feature, foundation in #87):** `assembleExpense` now destructures an optional `receipt_url` and passes it straight into the built `expense` row. Purely additive — every existing caller omits it, so it's `undefined` (dropped from the JSON insert body entirely, never nulls/overwrites the column). Jeevan owns the rest: `routes/upload.js`'s upload + `metas[]` seam (#84), the serve route, the frontend.
  2. **Issue #14 — atomic upsert for `category_corrections`:** `recordCorrection` (`lib/categoryMemory.js`) used to be select-then-insert-or-update — a real race: two concurrent corrections for the same merchant/description could both read "no row", both try to INSERT, and the second would violate the table's existing `unique(account_id, match_type, match_value)` constraint (already in place since the M2-P4 migration) and be silently swallowed by the best-effort try/catch, losing that hit_count increment. Fixed with a new `upsert_category_correction` Postgres function (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, same atomic-counter pattern as ADR-017's `next_voucher_seq`), called through a new `sbRpc(fnName, args)` helper in `lib/supabase.js` (POSTs to PostgREST's `/rpc/<fn>` — deliberately does NOT auto-inject `account_id` like the table helpers do, since RPC argument names are function-specific).
- **⚠️ NOT YET APPLIED to the live Supabase project** — same limitation as the voucher migration: no database-schema/DDL tool available in this session. Someone with SQL Editor access must run `db/2026-07-04-category-corrections-upsert.sql` once (idempotent — `create or replace function`). Until then, `recordCorrection` degrades to its old no-op-on-error behavior (the RPC call fails cleanly, caught by the same try/catch).
- **Tests: 877/877** (+4: `receipt_url` passthrough + omitted-by-default; `sbRpc`'s no-auto-inject behavior + default-args; `recordCorrection`'s new call shape — 2 signals, 1-signal, error-swallow, no-category-no-op — the atomic increment itself is a DB-level guarantee, same as `next_voucher_seq`'s, not independently unit-testable without a real Postgres).
- **Also confirmed (item 3 of Jeevan's message):** the live bot was ALREADY restarted with `DEFAULT_CURRENCY=MYR` active earlier today (boot banner confirms `currency=MYR`) — restarting again now anyway since new code just landed.
- **What's next:** part of the still-open... actually pushed straight to `feat/barat-menu-buttons`, but that PR (#88) already merged earlier today — opening a fresh PR for this commit. Relaying to Jeevan: PR #88 is merged (not open, as his message assumed), and a follow-up bug we found in it (Today Bills date filter) is already merged too as #90.
- **Assumptions:** `upsert_category_correction` returns the full row (`category_corrections`) via `RETURNING *`, matching `sbRpc`'s "return whatever PostgREST gives back" contract — `recordCorrection` doesn't currently use the return value, but it's available for a future caller that wants the post-upsert `hit_count` without a second read.

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-menu-buttons (bug fix, same open PR #88)
- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (new `mytDayBoundsUTC`/`mytWeekStartBoundUTC`; the 3 report handlers switched from filtering `expense_date` to `created_at`), `tests/bot.menu.test.js` (+7).
- **What changed — live QA caught a real bug within minutes of shipping #88:** Barat uploaded 4 receipts; "Today Bills" said "Nothing captured yet today" even though "Weekly Record" showed 5 bills. Root cause: the report handlers filtered by **`expense_date`** — the date OCR read OFF the receipt itself — not by when the receipt was actually uploaded. All 4 receipts had real printed dates from March–June (a common case: photographing an old receipt today). Fixed: **Today Bills/Today Expenses/Weekly Record now filter by `created_at`** (the actual insert timestamp), via two new pure helpers computing the UTC instant bounds of the current MYT calendar day/week (`mytDayBoundsUTC`, `mytWeekStartBoundUTC`) for a `created_at gte/lt` PostgREST filter — display text still shows the friendly MYT calendar date/range via the existing `todayISO_MYT`/`weekStartISO_MYT`.
- **Verified against the LIVE Supabase data before shipping** (not just the test suite): re-ran the fixed query directly — Today Bills now correctly shows all 6 bills captured today (was 0), Weekly Record shows 15 (was 5, undercounting because it too used to filter by the wrong date field).
- **Tests: 873/873** (+7: the two new UTC-bound helpers incl. the midnight-boundary edge case and a week-start/day-start consistency check; both report handlers' updated filter-shape assertions; and — the important one — a direct regression test reproducing the exact bug: a receipt captured today but printed-dated months ago must still appear/count).
- **What's next:** pushed as an additional commit to the still-open PR #88. Restarting the local bot; Barat should re-tap Today Bills / Weekly Record to confirm live.
- **Assumptions:** none new. "Captured" (per the Owner's own wording requesting this feature) means insertion time, not the receipt's own printed date — the printed date remains what's shown per-line in the Today Bills list and is still the correct field for `lib/reports.js`'s period-based financial reporting (a different, legitimate use case, untouched by this fix).

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-category-picker-and-pdf-fix (follow-up, same open PR #74)
- **Files:** `lib/telegram.js` (new `tgEditMessage`), `lib/bot.js` (`handleCallbackQuery`'s catpick branch + `tryResolveRemark` now edit in place; new module-level `pendingRemarkMessages` Map), `tests/telegram.test.js` (+3), `tests/bot.categorypicker.test.js` (+3), `tests/bot.categorypicker.remark.test.js` (+2).

Now the SAME message is edited in place instead:
  - New `tgEditMessage(chatId, messageId, text)` in `lib/telegram.js` — calls `editMessageText`, always explicitly clears the inline keyboard (`reply_markup:{inline_keyboard:[]}` — Telegram does NOT drop an existing keyboard on its own), same Markdown-rejected-retry-as-plain-text safety net and never-throws contract as `tgSend`.
  - Tapping a real category: the prompt message is edited straight to "✅ Saved as X" — no new message.
  - Tapping "Others": the prompt message is edited to "please type a note" (buttons cleared) instead of sending a second message. Since the remark text arrives in a LATER, separate webhook update with no access to that message's id, a best-effort in-memory `pendingRemarkMessages` Map (expenseId -> {chatId, messageId}, same v1 caveat as the existing `groups` media-group buffer) remembers it. `tryResolveRemark` edits that same message into its final "Saved as Others" state once the note arrives, or falls back to sending a fresh message if the mapping is missing (e.g. a bot restart in between) — a cosmetic-only degrade; the expense row itself is always saved regardless.
  - **Every touched dep is injectable** (`tgEditMessage`, `pendingRemarkMessages`) for hermetic tests, matching the rest of the file's convention.

## 2026-07-04 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · docs/jeevan-line-item-categorization-spec
- **Files:** `docs/specs/2026-07-04-line-item-categorization-design.md` (new), this WORKLOG.
- **What — Owner-requested spec:** make per-line-item categorization real. The categorizer (`lib/categorize.js`, #6) already returns a category per line item, but the product keeps only ONE rolled-up category per receipt. Spec'd extracting line items in OCR, persisting the per-item breakdown into the EXISTING `expenses.line_items` jsonb column (no schema change), and showing it in the dashboard. Three concrete gaps identified on `main`: (A) OCR doesn't extract line items — FieldSpec-driven prompt, so `fields.line_items` is undefined → categorizer runs whole-receipt fallback; (B) `assembleExpense` never writes `line_items` (column stays `[]`, `.items` discarded); (C) no per-item display. **Cross-lane:** GAP A+B = Barat (`lib/ocr.js`), GAP C = Mugi (dashboard), categorizer already Jeevan's done. Receipt-level `expenses.category` unchanged (backward compatible).
- **What's next:** Barat + Mugi sign-off on the 5 open decisions (always-extract vs opt-in, stored shape, flagged-line→review-queue, FX scaling of line amounts, per-receipt item cap) → Barat builds OCR extract+persist → Mugi builds display. Reporting-by-line-item is an explicit follow-on, not v1.
- **Assumptions:** reuse the existing `line_items` jsonb (no migration); v1 = extract+persist+display only (no per-line editing, no line-item reporting yet).

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-category-picker-and-pdf-fix
- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (PDF summary parity, batch/single category-picker wiring, `catpick:` callback branch, `tryResolveRemark`), `lib/ocr.js` (`assembleExpense`'s uncertain-category branch, `buildCategoryKeyboard`/`categoryPromptText`/`PENDING_CATEGORY`), `tests/bot.pdf.test.js` (+2), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+4), `tests/ocr.categorypicker.test.js` (new), `tests/bot.categorypicker.test.js` (new), `tests/bot.categorypicker.remark.test.js` (new), `tests/bot.batch.test.js` (+1), `tests/bot.delete.test.js` (2 tests hardened to stay hermetic against the new remark check).
- **What changed — two Owner requests:**
  1. **PDF "no data extracted" fix:** root cause was `handlePdfDocument` calling `formatBatchSummary(summary)` WITHOUT the `saved`/`skippedReasons` args `flushGroup` already passes — a PDF that rasterized fine but yielded nothing usable showed a bare, unexplained "0 saved". Fixed by mirroring `flushGroup`'s `captureAssemble` wrapper in `handlePdfDocument`. Now matches the photo-album path exactly (per-receipt breakdown when saved, honest "not a clear receipt" wording when skipped).
  2. **Interactive category-picker (ADR-019-shaped, no formal ADR filed yet):** when the categorizer itself is uncertain — `lib/categorize.js`'s per-item `flagged` (post-LLM-watchdog, not a naive `source==='fallback'` check — confirmed by reading the source directly; a memory-sourced guess the watchdog strongly disagreed with is ALSO uncertain) — `assembleExpense` no longer silently commits a guessed category. It generates a client-side UUID (`crypto.randomUUID()`, no `sbInsertReturning` needed), inserts the row immediately with `category:'__pending__'`, `status:'awaiting_category'` (nothing is ever lost even if the user never responds or the bot restarts), and returns `{ awaitingCategory:true, categoryOptions }` — a key that's **absent entirely** on the confident path, so every pre-existing caller/test/fake is untouched.
    - **Single photo:** `expenseFromPhoto` sends `categoryPromptText` + `buildCategoryKeyboard` instead of the normal "Captured" message.
    - **Batch (album):** matches the Owner's own worked example (5 photos, 3 confident + 2 uncertain → ONE summary covering the 3, THEN 2 separate follow-up picker messages). `flushGroup`'s `captureAssemble` routes `awaitingCategory` results into a new `awaitingPicks` array (kept OUT of the itemized `saved` breakdown); `formatBatchSummary` gained an `awaitingCount` param that subtracts the pending ones out of the headline "N saved" count and adds an honest "N need a category picked" bucket, so the numbers stay internally consistent.
    - **Buttons:** all of `lib/categorize.js`'s `CATEGORIES` (18 items) as buttons (2/row) + a distinct "Others" button always last. `callback_data` is a short `catpick:<id>:<index>` (never the category text — stays well under Telegram's 64-byte limit regardless of category-name length) / `catpick:<id>:others`.
    - **`handleCallbackQuery`'s new `catpick:` branch:** re-looks-up the row and re-checks ownership (`rep_chat_id`) AND that it's still `status:'awaiting_category'` at confirm time (mirrors the delete flow's re-authorization pattern exactly — refuses gracefully on deleted/wrong-chat/already-resolved/stale-double-tap, never crashes, never leaks). Real category → `sbUpdate` + immediate "Saved" confirmation. "Others" → `sbUpdate` to `status:'awaiting_remark'` and asks for a typed note.
    - **`tryResolveRemark` (new, in `handleUpdate`, checked BEFORE `/start`/summary/delete keyword routing):** the next plain-text message from a chat with an `awaiting_remark` row is treated as that row's remark (`sbUpdate` remark + `status:'saved'`), then stops — otherwise falls through unchanged. Oldest-first if two rows are simultaneously awaiting a remark for one chat (documented v1 simplification, not a bug).
  - **Deliberately NOT touched:** `lib/categorize.js` (Jeevan's file — imported the already-exported `CATEGORIES` constant instead of adding a new export, avoiding a cross-lane edit) and `lib/massupload.js`/`routes/upload.js` (dashboard mass-upload path — an uncertain-category row inserted from there will sit as `awaiting_category` with no Telegram buttons to resolve it; a known, accepted gap since the dashboard has no chat to send buttons to — Jeevan's call whether/how to surface it in the dashboard UI later).
  - **Tests: 797/797** (+~30 new across 5 files: PDF parity, `assembleExpense` branching/regression-lock/every-existing-fake-shape/flagged-not-source, keyboard shape/byte-length/round-trip, prompt text, the full batch 5-receipt scenario matching the Owner's own example, catpick ownership/status/double-tap/deleted/garbage-index/network-failure cases, remark capture + oldest-first + the critical "nothing pending → /start/summary/delete/fallback all unchanged" regression guards). Also hardened 2 pre-existing `tests/bot.delete.test.js` tests that were inadvertently hitting the real network once `tryResolveRemark` was added (now inject `sbSelect: async () => []`).
- **What's next:** open PR (Tier-2 — writes money rows, new DB status values though no schema/CHECK-constraint change needed) → review → merge. Restarting the local bot on this branch for Barat's live test.
- **Assumptions:** `expenses.status` has no CHECK constraint (confirmed by reading `schema.sql`), so `'awaiting_category'`/`'awaiting_remark'` need zero migration. A client-generated `id` (valid UUID) is accepted by PostgREST on insert in place of the column's `gen_random_uuid()` default. `CATEGORIES`' fixed order is what both prompt-time button-building and confirm-time index-resolution rely on — if that array's order ever changes, in-flight (already-sent, unresolved) picker messages would resolve to the wrong category; acceptable given `CATEGORIES` is a static code constant today, not runtime-configurable.
- **Merge note (J1, 2026-07-04):** resolved conflicts with the voucher PR (#72) in `lib/ocr.js` — the uncertain-category branch and the `sbInsertReturning`/`voucher_no`-echo path coexist (an `awaiting_category` row still gets a DB voucher_no, just not shown on the picker prompt); kept both `refLine` (voucher) and `categoryPromptText`/`buildCategoryKeyboard`/`PENDING_CATEGORY` (picker) + merged exports. `lib/bot.js` auto-merged cleanly.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · fix/jeevan-e2e-edit-and-agent-currency
- **Files:** `public/js/components/activity.js`, `lib/agent.js`, this WORKLOG.
- **What changed — 2 real bugs found doing Owner's #1-goal E2E walkthrough (dashboard upload → OCR → save → list/filter → edit → category → currency → Excel → agent chat → reports → review queue), live against the real Supabase (`xurnpvesgcybrnilxszf`) + Groq:**
  1. **Edit-expense was silently broken for every non-integer amount.** The Amount `<input type="number">` had no `step` attribute, so the browser's native HTML5 constraint validation (default step=1) rejected any value with cents (e.g. `21.20`) and silently blocked the whole form's submit — no error shown, the Save button just did nothing. Since virtually every receipt has cents, this made the edit drawer effectively non-functional. Fix: `input.step = 'any'` on the amount field.
  2. **Agent chat answered in `$` regardless of account currency.** `lib/agent.js`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT` told the model "amounts are already in the account currency" without ever saying what that currency IS (`getReport`/`topMerchants` return bare numbers, no currency field) — so the model defaulted to `$`. On this MYR account that's the exact same class of confusion as the MYR/USD data incident earlier today. Fix: interpolate `cfg.DEFAULT_CURRENCY` into the prompt and explicitly instruct the model to use the currency code, never a symbol. Verified live: "Your total spend... MYR 63.40" / "BONDA CAFE (MYR 21.20)", with real `getReport`/`topMerchants` tool calls.
- **Also verified working (no changes needed):** dashboard upload → real Groq OCR → save (confirmed merchant/amount/currency/category all extracted correctly, categorized into the real 18-category taxonomy); Activity search/status/category filters; Excel export (both `/api/excel` directly and the UI button); Reports tab totals/breakdown; review queue Approve (flagged row → `approved`, queue clears).
- **Tests: 775/775** (no new tests added — both are UI/prompt-behavior fixes; existing suite is the regression guard, `.env` moved aside per the standing gotcha).
- **Observed but NOT investigated (flagging for Owner/Barat):** an `M/S Cash` row (`created_at` 2026-07-03T14:53) is live in the table with `status=saved`, `deleted_at=null` — the prior session's WORKLOG entry (`fix/jeevan-config-startup-visibility`) claims an `M/S Cash` row was soft-deleted as junk. Either a different `M/S Cash` row exists, or that soft-delete didn't take. Didn't touch it — financial data, needs Owner confirmation before any action.
- **Still open (unchanged from handoff):** the `db/2026-07-03-voucher-reference-number.sql` migration (PR #72, merged to `schema.sql`) is NOT yet applied to the live Supabase project — my Supabase MCP only reaches the FrictionAI parent (`fziaxuhonvrhqjulhcyu`), not this app's project, confirming the handoff note. Someone with SQL Editor access must run it once. Receipt-image display (Supabase Storage) spec is still unwritten — now unblocked since Barat's voucher PR landed, no longer racing him.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-voucher-reference-number (ADR-017)
- **Files:** `db/2026-07-03-voucher-reference-number.sql` (new), `schema.sql` (folded in, structure only — no backfill loop, matching the file's existing convention), `lib/supabase.js` (`sbInsertReturning`), `lib/ocr.js` (`assembleExpense` echoes `voucher_no`; new `refLine()` helper + export), `lib/bot.js` (batch breakdown shows the ref), `tests/supabase.insertreturning.test.js` (new), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+3), `tests/ocr.nonreceipt.test.js` (+4), `tests/bot.batch.test.js` (+2).
- **What changed — Barat's half of ADR-017 (schema.sql + insert path), per PR #70's ownership split, after Owner sign-off:**
  - **Decisions confirmed by Owner:** (A) number by the receipt's own `expense_date` year, insert-date fallback; (B) backfill existing rows too; (C) gaps allowed (unique+ordered, not strict-gapless); (D) **prefix changed to `RC-`** (not the spec's default `V-`) — Owner's choice, "Receipt" not "Voucher". Format: `RC-2026-0041`.
  - **DB side (`db/2026-07-03-voucher-reference-number.sql`, folded into `schema.sql`):** nullable `expenses.voucher_no` + `UNIQUE(account_id, voucher_no)`; a `voucher_counters(account_id, year, last_seq)` table; `next_voucher_seq()` — atomic `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... RETURNING` upsert-increment, closes the race the spec explicitly flagged (post-#65, every batch path inserts near-simultaneously); a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `assign_voucher_no()` that fires only when `voucher_no IS NULL` (immutable thereafter — a later `PATCH` never re-fires it). One-time backfill `DO` block (only in the dated file, not in canonical `schema.sql`, matching how `category_corrections`' backfill was handled) numbers existing null rows in `created_at` order per account+year.
  - **App side (thin, additive, matches the spec):** `lib/supabase.js` gained `sbInsertReturning` (same as `sbInsert` but `Prefer: return=representation`) since the DB generates `voucher_no`, not the app. `assembleExpense`'s insert now uses it and echoes `voucher_no` back onto the returned `expense` — **only if the DB actually returned one**, so a pre-migration DB or an old row degrades to simply no reference shown, never a crash or a fabricated value. Both the single-photo reply (new `refLine()` helper) and the Telegram batch per-receipt breakdown (`flushGroup`'s `captureAssemble` wrapper, `formatBatchSummary`) show `🔖 Ref: RC-2026-0041` when present.
  - **Tests: 775/775** (+11: `sbInsertReturning` header/shape/fallback; `assembleExpense` echoes/omits `voucher_no` correctly; `refLine` present/absent/null-safe; batch summary shows/omits per-receipt; one media-group end-to-end wiring test). All existing test fakes (`insertExpense: async () => {}` etc.) are unaffected — the whole feature degrades to "no reference shown" when nothing provides one, by design.
- **⚠️ NOT YET APPLIED to the live Supabase project.** I have no database-schema/DDL tool available in this session (only the app's own REST API, which can't run `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE TRIGGER`). **Someone with Supabase SQL Editor access (Barat or Jeevan) must run `db/2026-07-03-voucher-reference-number.sql` once** against `xurnpvesgcybrnilxszf` before any row gets a `voucher_no` — until then the app code is fully backward-compatible (silently shows no reference, exactly like today). Idempotent — safe to re-run.
- **What's next:** open PR (Tier-2 — schema change on financial data, per the spec) → independent review → apply the SQL → merge → verify one live insert gets a `voucher_no`. Jeevan's downstream pieces (Excel column, dashboard display) are separate, already scoped in the spec.
- **Assumptions:** `RC-` was substituted for `V-` only in the generated string literal — table/column/function names (`voucher_no`, `voucher_counters`, `assign_voucher_no`) keep the spec's original naming to match ADR-017/the codebase's existing comments, avoiding a confusing two-name split for the same concept.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-J1 (for Jeevan) · fix/jeevan-config-startup-visibility
- **Files:** `lib/config.js` (new `configSummary()`), `server.js` (boot banner uses it), `tests/config-summary.test.js` (new, +3).
- **What changed — root-caused the "all live receipts saved as USD" incident:** live-data check on Barat's shared Supabase (`xurnpvesgcybrnilxszf`, NOT the FrictionAI parent) showed every expense stored as USD despite `DEFAULT_CURRENCY=MYR`. Traced via `ai_raw`: OCR read the currencies correctly (Bonda MYR 183.65, etc.) — the pipeline was converting good MYR amounts *into* USD because the **running process's `cfg.DEFAULT_CURRENCY` was still USD**. `.env` was edited to MYR mid-session, but dotenv loads `.env` into `process.env` ONCE at startup, so the file edit never reached the running process (a row ingested AFTER the `.env` edit was still USD — proof). Not a code bug: current `.env` + a fresh process resolves MYR correctly (verified by booting the server).
  - **Honest note on the fix:** the originally-proposed "live getter" (à la `AUTH_REQUIRED`) would NOT have prevented this — the stale value lives in `process.env` (dotenv-once), not the cfg snapshot, so a getter returns the same stale value. The effective mitigation is **visibility at boot**: `configSummary()` prints `account · currency · tax · auth` on every start, so a stale/misconfigured currency is seen immediately instead of discovered in the data later. Reads straight off `cfg` (no driftable second copy).
- **Data remediation (done, live, Owner-approved):** recomputed the 3 real rows to MYR from their `ai_raw` ground truth (Bonda 45.76 USD→183.65 MYR, L.C. Vegetarian relabel 68.9 MYR, 99 Speed Mart 5.17 USD→21.1 MYR; cleared fx_rate/amount_original/currency_original). Soft-deleted 3 junk rows (M/S Cash + 2× Unknown $0 rejected). Live table now = 3 clean MYR rows.
- **Tests: 764/764** (761 baseline after #64+#65 merges + 3 new). `.env` moved aside for the run per the standing gotcha.
- **What's next:** ⚠️ **the live pipeline that's still ingesting (produced `99 Speed Mart` at 13:30 UTC, still USD) runs on Barat's machine/deploy with its own `.env` + stale USD snapshot — Barat must restart it** to pick up MYR. This repo's `.env` has no `BOT_TOKEN`, so J1 can't drive the live bot. Then: voucher/reference-number spec (Barat's lane), rotate the dashboard token from the chat log, Mugi's GitHub username.
- **Assumptions:** MYR is the correct account currency (Malaysia-based Owner). L.C. Vegetarian had an empty OCR currency; treated as MYR (Malaysian receipt). M/S Cash treated as junk per the Owner's own batch-bug report.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-telegram-delete
- **Files:** `lib/telegram.js` (new `tgAnswerCallback`, `callback_query` added to webhook `allowed_updates`), `lib/bot.js` (`handleDeleteIntent`, `handleCallbackQuery`, routed from `handleUpdate`), `tests/bot.delete.test.js` (new), `tests/telegram.test.js` (+2), this WORKLOG.
- **What changed — Owner feedback during live use:** typing "Delete receipt" in the bot got the generic "Send me a photo…" fallback, which read as if the message was ignored. Two fixes:
  1. Any delete/remove/undo/cancel-shaped text now gets an answer that actually engages with the request.
  2. Built the real in-chat delete: text intent → bot looks up THIS chat's own most-recent receipt (`rep_chat_id` scoped, `deleted_at is null`) → sends a Yes/Cancel inline keyboard with the merchant/amount/date so nothing is ever deleted on the word alone → button tap → `callback_query` → **re-checks ownership + not-already-deleted at confirm time** (the callback_data only carries an id, so this re-check is the real authorization gate, not just the prompt) → `sbSoftDelete` (same soft-delete already used by the dashboard's `DELETE /api/expenses/:id`, so a Telegram-deleted row is recoverable the same way).
  - `callback_query` added to `registerWebhook`'s `allowed_updates` — was previously message-only, so button taps were never delivered.
  - `tgAnswerCallback` always acks (even on error) so a button never spins forever on the user's phone.
- **Tests: 690/690** (+16: cancel button, confirm+delete happy path, already-deleted guard, cross-chat ownership refusal [SECURITY], unknown-id guard, delete-throw never leaks/still acks, unrecognised callback_data no-ops safely, missing-chat guard, handleUpdate routing for both callback_query and delete-shaped text, tgAnswerCallback wire format + never-throws).
- **What's next:** open PR → review (touches money-adjacent data deletion + a new webhook update type, so treating as Tier-2). Once merged + webhook re-registers with the new `allowed_updates`, needs one live phone test: type "delete", tap Yes, confirm the row disappears from the dashboard.
- **Assumptions:** one confirm button offers only the SINGLE most-recent receipt for that chat (v1 scope — not a list/picker); a shared account currently maps each Telegram chat to its own `rep_chat_id`, so this naturally can't delete another rep's row. No new dependency.
- **Rebase note + SECURITY fix (same branch, before opening the PR):** this branch was cut from a stale local `main` — missed PR #63 (batch breakdown + `ALLOWED_CHAT_IDS` private allowlist), merged to `origin/main` afterward. Merged `origin/main` in (no rebase/force-push, SOP §4.4) and found the merge would have put my `callback_query` routing **before** the allowlist gate — a non-allowlisted stranger could tap a delete button and reach `handleCallbackQuery` even though they're blocked from every message-based command. Fixed in the same commit: `handleUpdate` now resolves the chat id for BOTH update shapes (message and callback_query) up front, runs the allowlist check once for both, and only then branches into callback vs message handling. A blocked callback still gets acked (`tgAnswerCallback`) so the button doesn't spin, just no reply/action. **+2 tests** (700/700 total): stranger callback blocked + never reaches `handleCallbackQuery`, allowed callback unaffected.
## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · fix/barat-batch-skip-nonreceipt-and-persist (follow-up commit)
- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (`buildInstruction`, `buildInstructionMulti`, new `skipReason`/`skipMessage`, `assembleExpense`, `expenseFromPhoto`), `lib/bot.js` (new `skipFooter`, `formatBatchSummary`, `flushGroup`), `tests/ocr.nonreceipt.test.js` (+8), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+4), `tests/bot.batch.test.js` (+5).
- **What changed — Owner feedback on the previous commit's skip message:** "Not a receipt — skipped" was one generic reply for two different situations — a genuinely unrelated photo (temple, QR code, phone-bill screenshot) AND a real receipt that's just too blurry to read. Owner wants them told apart, with specific wording for each (gave the exact blurry wording; asked for a "cool" one for not-a-receipt).
  - **The gap:** `isEmptyReceipt` (no merchant, no positive amount) can't distinguish these — both read identically to it. Needed a new signal from the model itself.
  - **Fix:** every extraction now asks for one extra key regardless of FieldSpec — `looks_like_receipt: true|false` ("true" = some kind of receipt/invoice even if unreadable; "false" = clearly something else). Read straight off `rawParsed` (untouched by the FieldSpec column loop, so customer columns are unaffected). New `skipReason(rawParsed)` classifies the untrusted signal defensively — `true`/`'true'` → `'blurry'`, `false`/`'false'` → `'not_receipt'`, anything missing/garbled → `'unclear'` (never guesses). New `skipMessage(reason)` maps each to wording:
    - blurry: *"📷 That photo's a bit blurry — can you resend a clearer shot?"* (Owner's exact wording)
    - not_receipt: *"🤨 That doesn't look like a receipt to me — try again with a real receipt photo!"*
    - unclear (fallback, unchanged): *"🚫 Not a receipt — skipped."*
  - `assembleExpense`'s skip return now carries `skipReason`; `expenseFromPhoto` uses `skipMessage(reason)` instead of the old fixed string.
  - **Extended to batch too** (not just the single-photo path this was reported on) — the multi-receipt prompt asks the same extra key per receipt, and now explicitly instructs "if the image has NO receipts at all, return one element with everything null and looks_like_receipt:false" (otherwise a totally blank image could yield zero array elements and vanish from the count instead of being explained). `lib/bot.js`'s `flushGroup` (my own file — did NOT touch Jeevan's `lib/massupload.js` again for this) now also captures each skip's reason via the existing `captureAssemble` wrapper pattern, and the batch summary breaks the skip count down: *"Skipped 1 photo too blurry to read and 1 photo that didn't look like a receipt at all — nothing was guessed."* Falls back to the old generic line if the reasons are missing/incomplete/ambiguous (`'unclear'` in the mix) — never mislabels. PDF path intentionally left counts-only (matches the existing precedent that PDF didn't get the per-item breakdown either).
- **Tests: 704/704** (+17: skipReason/skipMessage pure-function coverage incl. stringified booleans and garbled/missing signals; assembleExpense surfaces the right skipReason per case incl. no leftover key on a saved receipt; formatBatchSummary breaks down by reason + falls back safely on partial/ambiguous data; a live mixed-album flow with a blurry AND a not-a-receipt skip in the same batch, real receipt untouched).
- **What's next:** open/update PR → review. Same live re-test as before, plus: send one genuinely blurry real receipt and one totally unrelated photo in the SAME session, confirm each gets its own distinct reply.
- **Assumptions:** the model's `looks_like_receipt` answer is a best-effort classification, not ground truth — a wrong answer just picks the "wrong but still honest and non-destructive" message (worst case: a genuinely-not-a-receipt photo gets called "blurry" or vice versa), never a data-integrity issue, since `isEmptyReceipt` (unchanged) is still what decides save-vs-skip.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · fix/barat-batch-skip-nonreceipt-and-persist
- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js` (`assembleExpense`, `expenseFromPhoto`), `lib/massupload.js` (Jeevan's file — additive, see below), `lib/bot.js` (`formatBatchSummary`, `flushGroup`'s `captureAssemble`), `tests/ingest.test.js` (+3), `tests/ingest.money.qa.test.js` (deps fix), `tests/ocr.qa.test.js` (deps fix), `tests/massupload.test.js` (+2, 1 updated), `tests/bot.batch.test.js` (+3), this WORKLOG.
- **What changed — Owner report from live use:** uploaded 8 photos (mix of real receipts + a phone-bill screenshot, a temple photo, a Touch 'n Go QR screenshot); the bot listed 4 "receipts" including obvious junk (e.g. "M/S Cash — $3.79", merchant/amount the model clearly forced out of a non-receipt photo). Owner asked: when a photo isn't clearly a receipt, say so, don't force data.
  - **Root cause, traced to `isEmptyReceipt`** (the existing "no merchant AND no positive amount → don't save" guard, PR #54): it was only ever wired into `expenseFromPhoto` (the SINGLE-photo path). Every batch path — Telegram multi-photo album, Telegram PDF, and the dashboard's `/api/upload` mass-upload — calls `assembleExpense` directly via `lib/massupload.js`'s `runBatch`, which never ran this check. A non-receipt photo in a batch always got force-saved.
  - **SEPARATE, more severe finding made while tracing this (flagging clearly — Owner should know):** `assembleExpense`'s own documented contract (`docs/specs/2026-07-02-mass-upload-design.md`: "assembleExpense(...) → {expense,flagged} ... **and inserts the row**") was **never implemented** — it only ever built and returned the expense object; the actual `sbInsert` call has only ever existed inside `expenseFromPhoto` (single-photo). Confirmed via `grep sbInsert` across `lib/ocr.js` + `lib/massupload.js` on current `origin/main`: zero matches in the batch path. **Every batch upload — Telegram album, Telegram PDF, dashboard mass-upload — has told the user "N saved" while writing NOTHING to Supabase**, since this code was built (single-photo uploads were never affected — they have their own explicit `sbInsert`).
  - **Fix (both issues, one change, in `assembleExpense`):** moved the `isEmptyReceipt` check INTO `assembleExpense` itself (right after building the expense object, BEFORE currency/category/anomaly — no point spending a categorizer/FX/anomaly-history call on a photo that's about to be discarded). If empty → returns `{ expense, conf, flagged:false, skipped:true }`, never inserts. If real → runs the full pipeline as before, THEN **actually inserts** (`deps.insertExpense || sbInsert`), returns `skipped:false`. One decision point now shared by every caller — single photo AND every batch path — instead of being (correctly) checked in one and silently absent in the other three.
  - `expenseFromPhoto` simplified: no more separate `isEmptyReceipt` call or `sbInsert` call — just reads `skipped` off `assembleExpense`'s result. No behavior change for the single-photo path (same guard, same short "🚫 Not a receipt — skipped." reply), just one less duplicated responsibility.

Merchant — $X" list.

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · feat/barat-groq-key-rotation
- **Files:** `lib/groq.js` (call-site rotation loop), `tests/groq.rotation.test.js` (new). My lane. Consumes Jeevan's `lib/groqkeys.js` (PR #57, on main) read-only.
- **What changed — ADR-016 call-site, the Groq half of multi-key rotation:** factored the duplicated 4-attempt loop out of `groqChat`/`groqChatTools` into one shared `callGroq(body, extract, empty)` runner that now rotates keys per call:
  - Random start key (`pickKey`). On a 429/rate reply → `nextKey(tried)` to a fresh untried key with **NO sleep** (fresh account = fresh quota). Rotate until the whole pool has 429'd this round.
  - Full-pool exhaustion = **one** backoff attempt (sleep, reset `tried`, re-pick). Total attempts still capped at 4 — **key-swaps never eat the backoff budget** (my flagged detail #2).
  - Non-429 error (network throw) → today's backoff-and-retry (my detail #1: only a 429 rotates). Non-rate reply w/o choices → today's empty return.
  - **Single-key pool (or `GROQ_KEYS` unset) is byte-for-byte the old behavior** (verified: all 6 pre-existing `tests/groq.test.js` green, incl. the 1s-retry and 6s-exhaustion timings unchanged).
  - **`.env` shape:** SUPERSEDE-not-union (Jeevan's decision, my q#3) — already documented in `.env.example`; no change needed there.
- **Tests: 668/668** (+5 rotation: 429→different key with no sleep [timing-asserted <500ms]; no key retried twice per round; full-pool exhaustion→backoff [~1000ms]; single-key unchanged; happy-path no rotation/no sleep).
- **What's next:** open PR → Tier-2 review (multiple third-party secrets, ADR-016). This is the last piece of ADR-016 — pool + consumer both land → the free-tier daily ceiling multiplies by pool size at $0.
- **Assumptions:** `pickKey()||cfg.GROQ_KEY` fallback keeps a totally-unconfigured deploy behaving exactly as before (empty key → same as today). `tried` Set is per-attempt (reset each backoff round) so a transiently-limited key gets another chance next round — matches ADR-016 "no per-key cooldown."

## 2026-07-03 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · fix/barat-flushgroup-timer-unhandled-rejection
- **Files:** `lib/bot.js` (`flushGroup`, its `setTimeout` call site in `handleUpdate`), `tests/bot.flushgroup-guard.test.js` (new).
- **What changed — closes a QA subagent finding (Debug loop, SOP §4.8), same failure shape as PR #44's `tgSend` bug:**
  - **Root cause:** `flushGroup`'s dependency-resolution lines (`tgFileUrl`/`getFieldSpec`/`runBatch`/`extractReceipts`/`assembleExpense`) sat OUTSIDE its own `try/catch`. If any of them threw while being read (e.g. a hostile/misconfigured `deps` object, or a future refactor), the throw escaped the async function ungaurded. The `setTimeout` call site invokes `flushGroup(id, deps)` fire-and-forget (no `await`/`.catch` — production `setTimeout` ignores return values), and by the time the debounce timer fires, `handleUpdate`'s own promise chain has long since resolved and detached — so the rejection became a silent Node `unhandledRejection` with **no reply ever sent to the user**. Currently latent (not the live cause of Barat's "bot stuck" report — that was PR #44 not yet pulled locally), but a real robustness gap in the exact same "swallowed by omission" shape.
  - **Fix:** widened the `try` to cover all dependency resolution (only `send` itself is resolved before the `try`, since the `catch` block needs it to report failure even if everything else throws). Added a defense-in-depth `.catch(e => console.error(...))` at the `setTimeout` call site so even a future regression that reopens this gap can't produce a silent unhandled rejection again.
  - **Tests: 377/377** (2 new: a hostile getter-throwing dep resolved outside the old try → safe reply, not silence; a normal in-try throw — `getFieldSpec` rejecting — confirmed unaffected/still safe).
- **What's next:** open PR → review (Tier-2-ish: same class as PR #44, untrusted-input-adjacent robustness on the bot reply path). **Coordinate with open PR #46** (`fix/barat-qa-flushgroup-nonerror`) — both touch the same `catch (e) { console.error('[group]', e.message) }` line in `flushGroup`; this branch already applies #46's `e && e.message` guard inline to avoid a conflict, so #46 can be closed/superseded once this merges (confirm with Jeevan first — see ready-to-send message).
- **Assumptions:** none new. Found via an independent QA subagent pass specifically hunting for other "receives message, never replies" bugs after PR #44 fixed the first one.

## 2026-07-02 · Claude-B1 (for Barat) · fix/barat-tgsend-plaintext-fallback
- **Files:** `lib/telegram.js` (`tgSend`), `tests/telegram.test.js` (new).
- **What changed — fixes B2's Critical "photo received, no reply" finding (Debug loop, SOP §4.8):**
  - **Root cause:** `tgSend` sends with `parse_mode: 'Markdown'` and interpolates raw merchant/category text. A value with an unbalanced `*` / `_` / `[` / `` ` `` makes Telegram reject the whole message (400 "can't parse entities"). `hreq` does NOT throw on that — it returns `{ ok:false }` — so the old `tgSend` **ignored the rejection and returned as if it succeeded**: the user got total silence, not even a logged error. B2's quieter-bot branch removes the earlier "Reading…" ack, so a failed final send = complete silence (hence it gated that branch).
  - **Fix:** `tgSend` now checks `r.ok`; on a Telegram rejection it **resends the same text as PLAIN text (no parse_mode)** so a formatting glitch can never swallow the reply. Still never throws (network errors caught as before).
  - **Tests: 375/375** (4 new: Markdown-rejected→plain-retry, accepted→single-send, both-fail→no-throw, network-error→no-throw).
- **What's next:** open PR (Tier-2-ish: untrusted-input formatting on the bot reply path) → review. **This un-gates B2's `feat/barat-quieter-bot-acks`** — that branch is safe to merge once this lands (rebase on it). Optional follow-up polish: Markdown-escape interpolated merchant/category at the `lib/ocr.js`/`lib/bot.js` call sites so the styled version survives (the plain-text retry is the safety net either way).
- **Relaying B2's other open items to the Owner:** the 3 pushed branches (`fix/barat-qa-flushgroup-nonerror`, `fix/barat-qa-money-integrity`, `feat/barat-quieter-bot-acks`) need Jeevan's QA; the RM→MYR currency finding (2-char symbol falls back to USD — overlaps `lib/currency.js`, needs coordination); Supabase test-row cleanup (user-approved, pending).
- **Assumptions:** none new.

The **HTML/`<script>` render-side of #17 is intentionally NOT touched** — printable tags still pass through and must be output-escaped in the frontend lane (J1/Mugi).

## 2026-07-02 · Claude-J2 (for Jeevan) · feature/jeevan-p4-pdf-route (#1 PDF Task 7 — dashboard glue)
- **Files:** `routes/upload.js`, `tests/upload.pdf.test.js` (new), `docs/WORKLOG.md`. My lane only.
- **What changed:** Task 7 of the #1 PDF plan — `POST /api/upload` now accepts PDFs. After the existing base64→Buffer decode, each item is sniffed for the `%PDF-` magic byte; a PDF is expanded via Barat's `rasterizePdf(buf)` (already on main, PR #38) into its rendered PNG page buffers, which are just more `images[]` for `runBatch` (Buffer inputs now work end-to-end thanks to Barat's #39 `toImageUrlArg` fix). Non-PDF items keep the existing image path unchanged.
  - **Error mapping (SOP §7.2):** `rasterizePdf`'s typed `PdfError.code` → a **fixed** client message + status — `TOO_LARGE`→**413**, `NOT_PDF`/`CORRUPT`/`ENCRYPTED`/`EMPTY`→**422**. No code/internal is ever interpolated into the response. A non-`PdfError` throw falls to the existing generic **502**. This means the render-dimension-bomb finding from PR #38 QA (a tiny PDF with a huge MediaBox → `PdfError('TOO_LARGE')`) surfaces over HTTP as a clean **413**, never an OOM/500.
  - **Combined page cap:** a batch of PDFs can expand past `MASS_MAX_IMAGES` pages; the expanded list is bounded to the cap (Groq cost/DoS defense) and `pages_truncated:true` is surfaced. Reuses the existing `sanitizeSummary` for `failed[].reason`.
  - **Tests: 345/345** (+8: single PDF→pages→runBatch, image path untouched, mixed image+PDF batch, truncated surfaced, TOO_LARGE→413, ENCRYPTED/CORRUPT/NOT_PDF/EMPTY→422 with planted-secret non-leak, non-PdfError→502, combined-cap bounding). DI throughout (inject `rasterizePdf`), hermetic.
- **What's next:** open PR → **Tier-2** (untrusted PDF over HTTP + writes money rows) → independent QA (incl. a real oversized-MediaBox PDF through the route → 413) → Owner merge (self-authored → sandbox blocks self-merge). This is the **last piece of #1 PDF** — both surfaces (Telegram #38 + dashboard here) then complete. After: ADR-011 reference frontend for Mugi.
- **Assumptions:** PDF rejection fails the whole request with the mapped status (not a best-effort per-item failure) — matches the plan's Task 7 and gives the client an actionable code. `pages_truncated` is additive to the summary shape (only present when a PDF page-cap or the combined cap triggered).

## 2026-07-02 · Claude-B2 (for Barat) · fix/barat-ocr-buffer-image
- **Files:** `lib/ocr.js`, `tests/ocr.test.js` (+4 tests). My lane only — `routes/upload.js` (Jeevan's) untouched.
- **What changed:** Fixed a **confirmed live bug** flagged in the B1→B2 handoff: `extractReceipt`/`extractReceipts` set `image_url.url = image` directly, but Groq needs a STRING (URL or data-URI). The dashboard `/api/upload` path (`routes/upload.js`) decodes base64 → raw **Buffer** and passes it through `runBatch` → `extractReceipts`, so the Buffer serialised to `{"type":"Buffer",...}` and the model received no image — `/api/upload` extraction was broken for every image. (Telegram path OK — resolves to a URL string; PDF path OK — data-URI string.)
  - Added `toImageUrlArg(image)` — a Buffer → base64 **data-URI** (MIME sniffed from magic bytes: png/jpeg/webp/gif, default jpeg); a string passes through unchanged. Applied at BOTH Groq call sites. Honors the long-documented `extractReceipt(imageUrlOrBuffer, …)` contract that was promised but never implemented.
  - Fix placed in Barat's `lib/ocr.js` (single boundary, fixes all callers) rather than `routes/upload.js` (Jeevan's lane) — avoids a cross-lane edit and covers any future Buffer caller.
  - **Tests: 279/279** (+4: Buffer→png data-URI; JPEG magic-byte sniff; string passthrough unchanged; extractReceipts Buffer path — asserts the sent `image_url.url` is a `data:` string, never a Buffer object).
- **What's next:** push → J1 opens PR + Tier-2 QA (OCR/untrusted boundary) → merge. **Coordinate with Jeevan so he does NOT also fix it in `routes/upload.js` (double-fix).** Independent of PR #38 (PDF) — separate branch, keep PDF review clean.
- **Assumptions:** none new; additive — only changes behavior for Buffer inputs (currently broken); URL/data-URI string inputs unchanged (verified by the passthrough test).

## 2026-07-02 · Claude-B2 (for Barat) · feature/barat-p4-pdf (PR #38 QA fixes)
- **Files:** `lib/pdf.js`, `lib/config.js`, `tests/pdf.qa.test.js` (flipped J2's characterization lock). Pulled J2's QA tests first (dc5d1bb: 13 regressions + fixtures + `.gitattributes`).
- **What changed:** Addressed both Important findings from J2's PR #38 Tier-2 QA — both in `lib/pdf.js` (my lane; J2 did not touch my source):
  1. **Render-dimension bomb** — `rasterizePdf` capped bytes/pages/DPI but NOT rendered pixel dims, so a ~466-byte PDF with a 20000×20000pt MediaBox forced PDFium toward a ~1.6 GB bitmap alloc. Added a pre-render cap: read `page.getOriginalSize()`, compute pixel dims (`originalW/H × scale`, scale = dpi/72), and throw `PdfError('TOO_LARGE')` **before** `page.render` when `w*h > cfg.PDF_MAX_RENDER_PIXELS`. New knob `PDF_MAX_RENDER_PIXELS` (default **40 MP**, env-overridable) — fits any real page (A3@150dpi ≈ 4.3 MP, A4@600dpi ≈ 35 MP), blocks the bomb.
  2. **Raw error leak from the render loop** — the per-page `try` had only a `finally`, so a render error escaped as a raw `Error` (broke the typed-`PdfError` contract at `lib/pdf.js:58-59`; would become a 500 in Task 7). Wrapped `page.render` in a try/catch that re-throws `PdfError` and maps any other (raw pdfium/WASM) error to `PdfError('CORRUPT')` — no library internals surfaced (SOP §7.2). The dimension cap covers the flagged case (throws PdfError before render); the catch is defense-in-depth for any other render failure.
  - **Flipped J2's characterization lock** (`tests/pdf.qa.test.js` render-bomb test) from "asserts raw non-PdfError leaks" to "asserts `instanceof PdfError` + `code==='TOO_LARGE'` + `message==='TOO_LARGE'`" — the intended green state.
  - **Tests: 305/305** (J2's 13 QA regressions incl. the flipped render-bomb test all green).
- **What's next:** push → **re-request QA from J2**. Once #38 merges, J2's Task 7 (`routes/upload.js` glue) proceeds. Not self-merged.
- **Assumptions:** `page.getOriginalSize()` returns page points (public API, `node_modules/@hyzyla/pdfium/dist/page.d.ts:19`). 40 MP default chosen for headroom vs. the ~400 MP bomb; tune via `PDF_MAX_RENDER_PIXELS` if a legit very-high-DPI need appears.
